In the staircase tower, jump to the bottom, ignoring the enemies, and use the Melancholy Soul on the blue door.


Normal mode

The single easiest SB to get in the entire game. Well, possibly Mission 13 is easier. Nah. Mission 5 is.


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 6 =- -= Evil Of The Waterways =-
_______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 3:30
Red Orbs: 250
Damage: 0%

This is definitely one of the easier missions in the game to SB. If you like, you can make a detour to the orb fountain at the beginning. There's 3 green orbs worth 10 red orbs each, plus you can slash the orb fountain with the 4-Hit combo with HT finisher to get a further ~170 red orbs. To really milk the orb fountain, use Vortex 2. You can get anything up to 400-500 orbs that way. Having said that, you don't actually need the orb fountain's orbs at all for Hard or DMD mode.

You will, however, need to pick up a suspiciously gleaming "rusty" key and defeat three Plasmas. With Ifrit, the Plasmas are, quite literally, toast. A single Inferno can take out all three in one go (just be careful of instant counter hits - try not to jump right next to a Plasma). Alternatively, use uncharged meteors from a distance.

In a fresh game, you will only have Alastor, of course. I recommend a mixture of DTed Stingers and shotgun shots to get rid of the human Plasmas ASAP (you do not want the human Plasmas dividing and DTing on your arse, trust me), then use DTed shotgun shots or Roundtrips to get rid of the bat. For more info on Plasmas, check the Plasma section in the Beastiary.

Once the three Plasmas have been dispatched, go back out into the corridor, where there are three more Plasmas awaiting you - thankfully, you don't have to fight these ones. Unless you use DT to speed up, you will normally have to evade at least one attack by the Plasma bat, however. Use the so-called rusty key and enter the next area. You can Stinger straight to the Death Scissors or you can make a detour first to pick up 4 green orbs worth 10 red orbs each.

Deal with the Death Scissors in the standard way, which usually means 3 DTed Air Hiked slashes - for more info, as ever, visit the Beastiary. When the Death Scissors is defeated, hang around for a mini-eternity until your DT is replenished.


Normal mode

For Normal mode, you will need to do some extra orb farming. You can go back into the staircase tower and shoot the two Sin Scythes there - one is already there, ripe for the shooting with the shotgun, but you will have to wait a few seconds for the second one to appear after killing the first one. Killing 4 Sin Scythes in total should be just enough to guarantee your orb requirement for the mission. Alternatively, it's probably both quicker and more productive to slash away at the orb fountain with the 4-Hit combo + HT finisher. Feel free to use Vortex 2 to get a ton of red orbs, if you have it.

In the rusted key room, you will get Beezlebubs. Bah. Make light work of these overgrown insects by walking around, firing DTed shotgun shots - or use Air Raid. Once out in the corridor, shoot at the Beezlebubs to regain DT. Use the rusty key.

As in DMD mode, you can collect 4 green orbs worth 10 red orbs each in the next area. In Normal mode, this will be your first and last time that you fight a Death Scissors - for information on how to fight one, go to the Death Scissors section of the Beastiary. Again, wait for DT to replenish before ending the mission.


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 7 =- -= Holding The Key of Ardor =-
_______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 2:00 (approximately)
Orbs: 650
Damage: Varies, but keep to a minimum

Before I begin the walkthrough, it should be noted that Mission 7 ranges from being one of the easiest missions to SB (in a maxed game, at least) through to being literally impossible to SB. Most of the rest of the game is bad enough when it comes to glitchy or buggy ranks, but Mission 7 exists in a whole new proud twilight zone of screwy ranking - the requirements seem to alter with every game. Sometimes M7 can't give you the SB fast enough; at other times, it yields it up only very reluctantly but it does at least exist; and at other times, it just plain doesn't appear to exist at all. Luckily, the completely non-existent SB is rather rare, but it does still happen and has, indeed, happened to me. If it happens to you, I recommend moving on with the S-Rank and orb-farming fountain room Phantom in the Mission 7-8 interval to make up the 2000 red orb shortfall. As a guide, the requirements to shoot for are under 1:50 for time (though I have known the SB be given for this mission with a time of around 2:30), 650+ for orbs, and damage kept as low as possible.

Two other things to note: You not only don't need to do the secret mission, doing it actually hinders you getting the SB - the only way it's possible to do SM5 in DMD Mission 7 and still get an SB is if the ranking is extremely lenient for that mission. The second thing is to reduce damage to a minimum by remaining in DT mode as much as devilishly possible (do you c wut i did thar? >_>). DTing reduces damage from the Guiding Light by half in DMD mode, so it's imperative to remain in DT mode whenever possible.


Finally, the mission proper:

Begin by Vortexing or running down the corridor in DT mode. (Vortexing is fastest, though, provided you can Vortex in a reasonably straight line). Turn right at the end to trigger the appearance of Phantom for about the 15th time. He's angry, he's pissed off, he's out for revenge, and he's failed to learn from Mission 4 that crouching in corridors belching fireball after fireball simply doesn't work.

Obviously, Phantom is dealt with in pretty much the same ways as back in Mission 4. I will add a few notes here, though.

Firstly, Vortexing is much more of a pain up the arse - Phantom will sneak in swipes, and it's very hard to time your Vortexes so that you are invulnerable during them. If you have 10 DT runes, the cheap way to win is to simply keep the attack button held down without letting go and hold the analogue stick in the direction of Phantom's face - if you want to use this method, then you had better Stinger down to Phantom, because you will need every single rune of DT.

Secondly, the Critical Hit strategy is exactly the same as before, but you should remain in DT mode throughout this time to minimize damage from the Guiding Light. After the brief cutscene finishes, jump to evade the first fast-charged fireball, then resume the same deflection strategy that you hopefully learnt in Mission 4. As an aside, it's possible to taunt in between deflecting fireballs - if you cancel the sword slash, you can even fit in a double taunt. However, it's fairly hard to get down, and it isn't really necessary. It's most useful for a 3 DT rune game, where a single taunt + one deflection will restore DT at a stroke.

Thirdly, you can use DTed Air-Hiked slashes to defeat Phantom, but it isn't as easy as it sounds - you need to be careful not to get caught out by one of Phantom's many fireballs. I think the critical hit strategy is the best way to go, myself.

Once Phantom is defeated, wait for DT to be restored (or taunt, if there's only 1-2 runes which need to be refilled), nab the green orbs, then enter the next area, where two chilly Frosts await you. If you need guidance on Frost-slaying, refer to the detailed notes in the Frost section of the Beastiary. This bit will either be easy or damned fuckin' tough, depending on if it's a maxed or fresh game.

If it's a maxed game and you have Ifrit, then two Infernoes will dispatch of the Frosts - jump in between them both times, and try to DT-cancel the second Inferno, so that you get most of your DT back - one of the Frosts might survive the 2nd Inferno if it's DTed-cancelled, but, if so, it will be left hanging on for dead: quickly finish with a flame-kick, or whatever. Alternatively, you can take out both Frosts with a single Meteor 2. When you enter the area, run a few steps to the right to where the grille starts, then charge up a Meteor 2 until it automatically releases - during the charging up time, one of the Frosts should hop back to join its mate so that the two are aligned together. Goodbye Frosts.

If it's a fresh game and you only have Alastor, have fun! There isn't much I can add here that I haven't already mentioned in the Beastiary notes. What I normally do after entering the area, however, is Stinger or run over to the nearest Frost, do a 4-hit combo with a rising High Time followed by downward slash as finisher, and then launch into doing sword-cancelled 2-Hit combos, with, as always, the 2nd slash of each 2-Hit combo being cancelled - this will nearly always catch the second Frost in my slashes as it comes creeping up to join its mate. From then on, it's mainly down to luck how long I can keep them both stunned for.

Due to the Frosts' tendency to wriggle away from your sword slashes, this is often a battle that you will have to play by ear. Don't be demoralized or surprised if you have to restart a lot of times - this is a tough battle to get through, especially unscathed. Fortunately, it does get easier with practice, although it never becomes a walkover.

Once the Frosts are defeated and the brief cutscene over, DT, and make your way like a bat out of hell to the staircase tower. In the staircase tower, there will be two trigger-happy shotgun Maris in opposite corners just waiting to take pot shots at you if you dare try to light up the dial. I recommend killing them. It doesn't take long and you will get some DT back for the final leg of the journey. If you stand at the bottom of the stairs, you can Air Hike over to the Mari to the left of the dial and kill it with a single, non-DTed, downward slash - from there, you can knock down the second Mari with a Stinger. If you like, High Time the remaining Mari and double taunt it for DT before killing it.

A regular slash followed by a Stinger in DT mode is the quickest way to light up the dial. Take the elevator to the top, facing left to take pot shots at the Maris along the way. When you get to a certain point (it's wise to learn where it is beforehand), Stinger onto the balcony which has the door leading into the Castellan's bedroom, enter, and use the Guiding Light.


Normal mode

The erraticness in the ranking system applies to Normal/Hard M7 just as much to DMD M7. Also, DTing not only stops the Guiding Light from damaging you, but actually slowly heals you. (I can't remember about Hard mode; sorry =/)

As in DMD mode, Vortex or run along the corridor and turn right to trigger the fight with Phantom. Normal Phantom can be dealt with in the same ways as DMD Phantom. Vortex 2 will turn him into a puddle of goo in next to no time, but he still might sneak in a swipe at the end, so watch out for that. Alternatively, 3 deflections will nearly finish Phantom off - finish the job with a Stinger.

In the next area, you will get three pretty gosh-darned annoying Sin Scythes - annoying, because they have a tendency to hide behind the walls, out of sight, and sling their scythes at you for cheap hits. Run down the right hand side of the corridor (from the player's POV), then - as you reach the blue door which leads into the basement with the secret mission - DT, jump up, and start Air Raiding like crazy. This should get rid of all 3 Sin Scythes before they get a chance to attack you.

Upon entering the staircase tower, don't move forwards, but hang back and blast away a few times with your shotgun first - there are some surprisingly annoying Beezlebubs here. Once they've all gone, DT, light up the dial and hitch a ride up to the top. Enter the Castellan's bedroom and use the Guiding Light.

Note: Meeting the orb requirement may be a problem in Normal mode. Phantom's orbs, the Sin Scythes' orbs and the Beezlebubs' orbs are a must. If you are still falling slightly short, at the beginning of the mission you can take a detour halfway along the corridor to grab 4 green orbs - if you are fully healed by being in DT mode then they will be worth 10 red orbs each.


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Intermission =-
_______________________________________________________________________________


During the intermission between Mission 7 and 8, neither time nor damage count against you, but there is also bugger all to do. You can collect any blue/yellow orbs, items, or red orbs which you have missed from Misson 1 onwards, and you can complete Secret Mission 5 down in the former Rusty Key room. That's about it. If you want to orb farm, then - in Hard/DMD modes only - a weakened Phantom in the fountain room presents the single best orb-farming opportunity in the game. (In Normal mode, you get three pathetic Sin Scythes only - pah.) Once the fun is over, make your way up to the top level of the main hall by either Air-Hiking up from the horserider's spear tip or by going back to the Castellan's bedroom.


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 8 =- -= The Legendary Knight Returns =-
_______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 3:00
Orbs: 585
Damage: 0%

Yes. Your holiday is already over. Get over it. Before starting this mission, you can taunt the two Maris outside the Castellan's bedroom door for DT, if so desired. In the rather spiffing room with lots of candles, you must collect the two red orbs to meet the red orb requirement for this mission. Exit the room, jump up into the courtyard, and run to the gate to trigger the re-appearance with, no, I can't even say it, you know it better than your best friend's name by now.


Boss Battle - PHANTOM 2


Phantom's moves

As Phantom 1, except:

Meteors From The Sky - Phantom goes into a rigid pose for 1-2 seconds, sends a fireball skyward from his back, and then six fireballs rain down from the sky one after the other. After this attack, Phantom always enters his "burnt out" phase. To evade the fireballs, just run or roll around the courtyard without doubling back on yourself. (Although the awkward camera angle changes can make this tricky at times.) If you're close enough to Phantom, you can prevent the attack from coming out by Stingering his face 1-3 times to make him flinch.

Jumping Attack - The attack hasn�t changed, but the telegraph for it has: Phantom will now do an unmissable rearing up on his hind legs beforehand: take note of this if you want to follow the Ceiling Glass strategy...

Red Phantom - Phantom gets a speed boost, but a little bit later on than in the first battle - he gets it when about 50% of his red lifebar has been taken away. Er, his body also doesn't turn red. At any rate, as in the first Phantom fight, just be more cautious of his faster claw swipes.


Battle

The Ceiling Glass strategy (aka the easy way):

Let's see. You have 1 glass ceiling embedded in the ground and 1 armour-plated giant spider, who likes to leap about in a pure rage. Welcome to your new allies: the glass ceiling and Phantom's one remaining brain cell.

At any rate, the Ceiling Strategy is as follows:

If Phantom jumps and lands on the ceiling glass five times, he will crash straight through it and you will win the battle by default. You can tell if you've got a landing which counts, if shards of glass fly up when he lands. You can get Phantom to land on the glass immediately: he will always begin battle with a jump, so - after cancelling the cutscene - run directly to the middle of the glass ceiling; Phantom will follow in your general direction. Wait 1-2 seconds to make sure he really follows, then jump away as he descends. After that, you only need to get Phantom to land four more times. It isn't even that difficult either. The trick is to get right behind Phantom - and I mean touching-his-rear-end behind him. This will nearly always prompt Phantom into rearing up - when you see him rearing up, run towards the centre of the glass, stop for a split second to make sure Phantom tracks your position, then jump away as he lands.

The fastest I've defeated Phantom using this method is 29 seconds - still not as fast as the CH strategy in Mission 3, though... <_<. The main drawback to the Ceiling Glass strategy is that - being a cheating bastard - you don't get your DT gauge refilled at the end of battle. There are two ways around this: You can either refill your DT gauge in the intermission before starting Mission 8, or you can taunt whenever you're behind Phantom - I prefer the latter method myself.


Other ways of defeating Phantom:

Critical Hits - You can still get critical hits on Phantom's rear end via DTed Air Hiked slashes (see Mission 3 for details), but there's no technique this time for keeping him trapped throughout. If you can get Phantom to plough blindly against a wall or a corner, you may be able to keep him trapped for a while, but it's down to luck as much as it is to skill. Otherwise, just get in slashes on the fly as best you can. The battle will probably go by faster if you use Stinger to interrupt Phantom's attacks as much as possible.

Stinger 2 - Stinger (preferably Lvl 2, but 1 will do at an absolute pinch) can be used to trap Phantom into a loop he can't break out of. The principle is as follows: Being directly in front of Phantom's face will prompt his double claw swipe, which you can easily jump back from. Having jumped backwards, Stinger Phantom's face; the Stinger will usually make him flinch and rear up in pain. Wait in front of him until he has recovered from the flinch, and then prompt another double claw swipe... Rinse and repeat. Once you get this simple pattern down, you can try sneaking in a second, less-damaging, Stinger after the first Stinger: when Phantom flinches, wait on the spot until he has recovered (i.e., landed back down on the ground), and then do a Stinger - this second Stinger won't make Phantom flinch, so be prepared to jump back quickly from his double claw swipe instead.

A good way to prepare Phantom 2 for a Stinger loop strategy is to run to the nearest corner at the start of battle - Phantom will dutifully leap after you. Jump over him as he descends - he will now be trapped in the corner. When he spins around (as he will), get in front of his face to trigger the double claw swipe. Occasionally Phantom will try to break out of the loop by charging a fireball, going still for Meteors From the Sky, or burning out. The first two can be stopped easily enough with Stingers, but the burnt-out phase is a tad more tricky. The ensuing fireball can still be interrupted with Stinger or High Time, but if you're not confident about the timing (the fireball which Phantom charges up after burning out seems a bit harder to interrupt, for some reason), then evade and get Phantom back into a loop ASAP. When Phantom gains his speed boost, don't try to sneak in the second Stinger any longer! You might want to abandon Stingers altogether for Air Hiked slashes, or whatever.

Air Raid - Have fun with this on DMD mode; you'll be there all fuckin' day. On Normal (and I think Hard mode, though I'm not certain), Air Raid is considerably more damaging. Try to fly behind Phantom to hit him on his face and back simultaneously; the life will fall off him if you manage this.

Nightmare Beta - Applicable to a maxed game only. If you have this gun, though, then it's surprisingly good against Phantom since its shots penetrate through his armour. Use DTed charged shots to get the most power, and roll/jump (hop jumps are the best) to cancel the recoil so that you can fire again faster. The main drawback to using the Nightmare-Beta is running out of DT, since your non-DTed shots are about as strong as a non spinach-eating Popeye. Meh. Gain back DT in the usual ways, by taunting, using melee attacks, etc.


The rest of the mission:

Collect the 500 red orbs deposited by Phantom, and then Stinger from the upper courtyard to the lower courtyard, collecting the blue or yellow orb on the ledge. Jump down and collect the Trident, as well as two hidden red orb caches. The first cache is on top of the left horse statue. You can use the same "hold-down-triangle-to-make-Dante-do-an-automatic-leap-up" trick as described in Mission 1, or you can do a kick jump onto it - jump up as high as you can before pressing the triangle button to jump again. The second red orb cache is on top of the pedestal in the right hand side of the courtyard. You need both of these red orbs caches to meet the red orb requirement.

After collecting both caches, jump down into the lever room and slash the lever, then go down the ladder - in a fresh game, don't forget to pick up the grenade gun from the skeleton! (If you do miss it, you can pick it up next mission, but I advise picking it up and equipping it now.) In the trident room, use the trident, then cancel the cutscene and make your way down to the castle entrance like hell for leather - jump over the gate, exit the room, run down the stairs, and then run underneath the portcullis. Even without DTing, you should have more than enough time to do this, provided you don't linger - but if the portcullis should look unusually low, Stingering underneath it works better than trying to roll under it.


Normal Mode

This is almost identical to its DMD counterpart. Phantom still takes 5 landings to crash through the glass ceiling, otherwise he is as comparatively weak as any other Normal boss compared to a DMD boss is. The rest of the mission is as DMD mode.


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 9 =- -= New Strength =-
_______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 8:00
Orbs: 1500
Damage: 0%

NOTE: This is the last mission in the game where the difference between a fresh and a maxed game will really tell. From Mission 10 onwards, the differences become more and more neglible, until, finally, you have a fully maxed out character in your fresh game and there are no more differences.

As usual, the fresh game is the harder challenge. Begin by Stingering or Vortexing over the drawbridge. Say goodbye to the castle - you won't be able to go back for a while. In this area, there are three pillars which have 50 hidden red orbs each on top of them. In a maxed game, you won't need these orbs, but in a fresh one you should go for them. Unfortunately, getting on top of the pillars is something of a pain up the arse - my method is to get as close to the pillar as possible, then jump straight up and do a wall-jump off the thicker middle bit to grab hold of the edge. For a slight shortcut, you can do an Air Hike (hop-jump the second jump) from the top of the first pillar to the top of the third pillar on the right hand side of the area - from there, Air Hike towards the middle pillar on the left hand side - it's possible to grab hold of its edge in mid-air, but good luck doing it more than once in every 100 tries.

Once you've collected the 150 red orbs, there is a pathetically short shortcut you can take by hop-jumping over the railing to land a little way down the staircase. Run down the spiral stairs and enter the courtyard - you will trigger a "Do or Die" countdown with three Blades. Take them out any way you please: grenade-rolling, Vortex, critical hits, whatever... Just defeat them quickly, wait for their orbs, and move on.

In the floating platforms area, the enemy will vary in Hard/DMD mode according to whether it's a fresh or a maxed game. In a fresh game, you'll get a Death Scissors, whose appearance won't be triggered just yet. Instead, run to the dial, light it up, and then take the groovy floating platforms to the top - make sure you centre the camera behind you before doing a jump to the next platform by holding R1. There are a couple of shortcuts which are well worth learning. For the first shortcut, you can jump from the first to the last platform by waiting until the last platform reaches the height of its ascent; at this point, do a jump straight up and Air Hike forwards - you should land on the last platform every time. For the second shortcut, you can jump from the first to the third-from-last platform by doing a full Air Hike over to it. I recommend practicing both before trying to do them for real.

Anyway: once you're at the top, pick up Ifrit (assuming it's still a fresh game), then exit - but be careful. If you land within the circle of blue markers, you'll trigger an unavoidable Death Scissors fight. Flame-kick so that you land outside of the blue circle, and then exit, hugging the wall of the castle as you go - you can just avoid stepping inside the circle this way. A quicker, but slightly more risky, way to exit is to hop-jump from the Ifrit platform, then do a flame-kick as soon as the camera angle changes: if done at the right time, the flame-kick will propel you straight through the exit.

If it's a maxed game, then you'll get three Blades on your case as soon as you enter the floating platforms area. DT to outrun them and light up the dial with a couple of DTed Stingers. It's not a bad idea to wait for the Blades to enter the corridor, so that you can defeat them, but you don't have to. You can use the same shortcuts across the floating platforms, as outlined in a previous paragraph. If there are Blades yearning for your blood in the entranceway, sometimes the camera will zoom in to show you this, which is really helpful. *rolls eyes*. If this is the case, walk around on the floating platform until the camera zooms out and you can see what you're doing again. Is all this actually worth it, since you already have Ifrit? The answer is a categoric "yes", as the prize awaiting you at the top is a gigantic red orb, worth 1000 red orbs - yay! :p


Boss Battle - GRIFFON 1 (Optional)

Think you're tough enough to take on Griffon 1? You can stick around to find out the hard way or flock off, featherface! :p


Bypassing Griffon

In a maxed game, you don't even need to see Griffon - ever. After defeating the Blades in the courtyard, simply light up the unlit torch and go through - however, you'll have to forego your gigantic red orb this way, which is probably not something you want to do. If you collect the gigantic orb, then that means triggering the battle with Griffon. It's miles safer running to the unlit torch if, after triggering the battle, you re-enter and exit the floating platforms area again, because this means that Griffon will now be on the ground instead of in the air - you don't really want to be trying to light up the torch and evade Griffon's aerial attacks simultaneously, trust me <_<. If Griffon is on the ground, he'll usually do a lame attempt at charging you down, which is much easier to evade.

In a fresh game, you can also bypass Griffon after collecting Ifrit, but you will miss his 500 red orbs a lot more than you would in a maxed game. It's still possible to meet the mission's 1500 red orb requirement even without defeating Griffon, but you'll need to do a lot of orb-farming (more on this later). And besides: would you really want to wimp out on fighting Griffon 1? Featherface :p


Griffon 1's moves

I'll divide Griffon 1's moves into his ground and aerial moves, beginning with the ground ones.

Ground:

V-Bolt Attack - Griffon will flap his wings, blowing Dante backwards if he is caught in the gust of wind, then send out a single horizontal bolt, followed by several V-shaped bolts. The V-bolts start off by being a V-shape, but then converge together to form a jagged line. If Dante was blown back by the gust of wind, he will be stunned for long enough to be invulnerable to the horizontal bolt, which will safely pass over him. The subsequent V-bolts can be evaded with a hop over the side, or a roll through the bolts as they converge together (careful not to roll too soon). The V-bolts track Dante's height, e.g., if Dante jumps up high, then the next bolt will be higher than Dante's head; if Dante rolls through a bolt or stands underneath one, then the next bolt will be low enough for Dante to do a low jump over.

Horizontal Bolt Attack - The start-up is the same as above: Griffon will flap his wings, blowing Dante backwards if he is caught in the gust of wind. This time, however, Griffon will send a single V-bolt, followed by several horizontal bolts. Again, Dante will be invulnerable to the initial V-bolt if he gets blown backwards and stunned by Griffon's gust of wind. The horizontal bolts can be evaded either by jumping over them or rolling through them, and they track Dante's height in the same way that the V-bolts do. Their height can sometimes be rather difficult to judge - to make it easier, try a pattern of alternating straight jumps up over the low bolts with standing on the ground to let the high bolts pass over Dante's head - this at least takes the guesswork out of wondering whether you can make it over that bolt or not.

Divebomb Attack - Best described by saying you have to go out of your way to get hit by this attack, and even then it's hard to get hit. Griffon will rise up high into the sky above you, then plummet back down to earth with a huge thud, jagged lightning at his feet. Just jump to evade this attack - you can hardly fail to hear or see it coming. The only thing worthy of note is that you must have your feet off the ground as Griffon lands, otherwise you will take a tiny sliver of damage from the shockwave of the jump - but since you need to jump to evade this attack anyway, being off the ground isn't a problem.

Charge'n'Peck - Griffon will charge across the battlefield towards Dante; when he reaches him, he will swing his head to the side to take a good peck, followed by a second downward peck at the ground if the first one misses. The first peck is easy to evade with a roll or jump away from Griffon as he swings his head; the second peck is virtually impossible to get hit by. Take advantage of the charge'n'peck to get in a DTed Air Hiked slash against Griffon; not that it does very much damage on DMD mode, but still.

Electricity Bolts attack (rare) - The screen darkens as Griffon stands still, then several scattered electricity bolts are sent out towards Dante - as a bolt homes in towards Dante, roll to the side to evade it. This is an extremely rare attack, which seems to only be triggered by going into the deepest corners of the battlefield, e.g., the corner which has the circular building with the Holy Water on top of it. (At all costs, stay out of that particular corner. Nasty things happen to players who wander there - nasty things like Griffon sending out lightning bolts at 3x their usual speed whilst out of sight behind you.)

Life Drain - This one is important to know! If you end up standing on top of Griffon, your life will drain the same way as it does in lava, poisonous pits, etc. Dante rarely stays on top of Griffon for long, so the loss of life isn't obvious if it's just a sliver. Until Rob discovered this effect a few months ago, it was responsible for a fair number of inexplicably missing Mission 9 SBs.

Panic Bolts - Not really an attack in its own right; if you get too close to Griffon during either the V-Bolt or Horizontal Bolt attack, he will panic and wind up the attack early, sending out 1-2 converging V-bolts of lightning at a random angle as he does so. The panic bolts can hit you, but are normally easily evaded with a roll underneath them or backwards jump.


Aerial:

Now for the aerial attacks, which are also Griffon's most dangerous attacks.

Web of Lightning (1) (Hard/DMD mode only) - Griffon will send out a single V-Bolt close to the ground, then faint yellow traces of lightning will start to appear around Dante. These yellow traces lie flat on the ground. They can't hurt Dante, but they will follow him wherever he goes. Once they 'solidify' to become red and white bolts, then they become harmful. Evade them by moving backwards across the battlefield (try to ensure you have plenty of space; you don't want to end up backed against a wall), jumping back to evade a yellow trace or solidified bolt where necessary. Also, watch out for repeated V-bolts, if Griffon decides to send out WoL (1) attack after WoL (1) attack - he has a lovely habit of chaining several aerial attacks together, as you'll find out when you fight him.

Web of Lightning (2) (Hard/DMD mode only) - Griffon will send out 1-2 (usually two) V-Bolts at random angles from the blue disc on his chest, then faint yellow traces of lightning will start to appear around Dante once more - except that these yellow traces now jut out at random angles to each other in all directions, not just flat on the ground. This makes them even more dangerous, but the principle of evading them remains the same as before - keep moving across the battlefield, jumping away to evade a bolt wherever necessary. It should be noted that WoL (2) is rarer than WoL (1). In fact, I think this variation is only triggered if you use an aerial attack such as Air Raid, or else jump up very close to Griffon from the ground.

"Carpet bomb" Attack (All modes) - Griffon will glide low along the battlefield, carpet bombing it with red/white energy balls as he glides. Evading them is trivial with Air Hike - simply Air Hike away to the side or even over Griffon as he approaches. Even if there are still lingering bolts from a previous WoL attack, by the time you've landed, the bolts will have disappeared. If you don't have Air Hike (if you're using Ifrit, for example), this attack suddenly becomes more dangerous. If there aren't any lingering bolts around, then just roll 2-3 times to the side as Griffon approaches. If there are still bolts lying around, then jump away just as Griffon reaches you and pray.


Battle

Make no mistake about it. DMD Griffon 1 is no walkover - featherface won't flock off so easily on this mode >_<

In a fresh game, you'll have Ifrit by now, but without any abilities. You can buy abilities for it by running all the way back to the Statue of Time in the pillars area - Griffon will still be waiting for you when you re-enter the courtyard. However, I don't think it's worth the extra minute of time that this will take you, given that even powerful Ifrit abilities don't deal that impressive against Griffon 1. Instead, consider bringing on... the grenade gun.


Grenade Gun:

The grenade gun isn't particularly strong against Griffon, but you have the advantage of both speed and unlimited ammo. If you're not used to using the grenade gun in Griffon battles, you may find that the majority of your grenades go astray at first. If this is the case, learn how to target him accurately. Hold R1; if your gun is aiming above Griffon, then wait a split second for it to re-orient itself or run or jump backwards until it's aiming squarely at Griffon - you're just wasting time if you a fire a grenade into thin air. Try to fight from long-range as much as possible - it's much easier to aim at Griffon from long-range, and if you're a long way away from him, you can grenade-roll until he closes down the distance by charging towards you. Whenever Griffon does a lightning attack on the ground, try firing a grenade and jumping backwards as each bolt comes out, so that you end up far away from Griffon when the attack comes to an end.

A technique you should at least try to use is DT-cancelling (see Advanced Techniques). All you do is fire a DTed grenade, then press L1 to cancel DT before the grenade hits Griffon - upon impact, you will get two DT runes back. It might not sound like very much, but this will make a significant difference over the course of an entire battle. DTed grenades do twice the damage of non-DTed grenades, plus Dante can run more speedily in DT mode, which helps when there is a large area of ground to cover. You can DT-cancel grenades at any time, but it obviously makes more sense to do so when DT is running low than when you have the majority of your runes filled.

I strongly recommend making use of the re-enter/exit trick to begin battle with Griffon on the ground instead of in the air - this will make your life easier, at least for a while. Get on Griffon's case with the grenade gun as aggressively as you can. You'll have to evade about three lightning attacks on the ground before Griffon decides it's time to rise into the sky to begin his first round of aerial attacks - by this time, you should have taken off one third to a half of DMD Griffon's lifebar.

When Griffon goes airbourne you have two choices: if you don't attack Griffon at all, he will do fewer aerial attacks and come down from the sky sooner; if you do attack Griffon, he will linger in the sky for longer. If you really can't abide Griffon's aerial attacks, then you may wish to refrain from shooting at him while he is in the air (or else knock him down from the sky - more on that in a bit). If you do decide to attack Griffon in the air, then, really, your only option is the grenade gun or E&I; - charging up a Meteor is dangerous and using Air Raid is positively suicidal. Assuming you have the grenade gun equipped, then just keep jumping backwards across the battlefield, firing the grenade gun as often as you can - don't worry too much about accuracy while Griffon is in the sky, as you can't stand still and take proper aim. Typically, Griffon will do around 5-6 chained aerial attacks before coming back down to ground (if you avoid attacking him, it's more like 3-4 chained attacks).

After he comes back down to ground, I've noticed that this is when Griffon will start using his divebomb attack for the first time. (Possibly, with another strategy, it might be different, but memory eludes me.) At any rate, it's not exactly anything to worry about :p ... Just resume grenading Griffon as before, and try to finish him off before he goes up into the air to begin a second round of aerial attacks - quite a tall order, but a doable one just about. If you can't do it, it's not too important - but if Griffon goes up into the air for a third time, then you really are taking too long... >_>

I suppose at this point I should mention that it's possible to make Griffon 1 fall down. You can either knock him down on the ground, or make him fall out of the sky when you hit him with a projectile. Whenever he falls down, he will lie there for a long time, vulnerable to attack. Some attacks have stronger "knocking down" properties than others - the grenade gun has extremely weak knocking down properties, to the point it's impossible to knock Griffon down with the grenade gun alone. (It can, however, act as a "finisher" if you bring Griffon to the brink of falling down with other attacks first.) Most melee attacks will contribute towards knocking Griffon down, particularly Ifrit attacks (I'll talk more about that in the Ifrit section, though).

Finally: make sure to get the final hit from a distance, as a shield of electricity bolts instantly surrounds Griffon 1 upon defeat, which will damage you if you're too close to him.


E&I;:

I can't say too much about an E&I; strategy, except that if you would rather use E&I; instead of the grenade gun, then it should be possible. One advantage of using E&I; is that it's a much stronger "knocker down" than the grenade gun, so you can make Griffon fall down with E&I; only at least once per battle and sometimes twice. However, using E&I; is slower in general, at least for me it is.


Alastor:

There's not much to say about Alastor vs. Griffon. Even Ifrit is a slightly mediocre Devil Arm against Griffon and Alastor is just frankly pathetic. (I love Alastor normally; just not against Griffon.) If you have Air Hike, then you can do DTed Air Hiked slashes whenever Griffon charges towards you; otherwise it's better for evasion, particularly when Griffon is doing his aerial attacks. If you have Roundtrip, feel free to use it whenever it's safe to do so (usually whenever one of Griffon's ground attacks has just finished). If you have Vortex 2, then you can burrow towards Griffon whenever he is doing a lightning attack on the ground to bring it to an early end. As for knocking Griffon down when he is on the ground, hacking away at his legs will eventually do it - with emphasis on the "eventually". If you do make Griffon fall down with Alastor equipped, then DTed Roundtrips combined with gunfire or just plain hacking away at Griffon's chest is the way to go.


Ifrit:

With a maxed-out Ifrit which has all abilities, I will recommend a "knock-down" strategy, because it's so much damn easier to knock Griffon down with Ifrit than with Alastor. Four to five Infernoes, plus a few flame-kicks into Griffon's face and/or Rolling-Blazed jumps through Griffon are sufficient to knock him down. Try to arrange it so that you have a full DT gauge when Griffon falls over: with a 10-rune gauge you can charge up four Meteor 2s in a row against Griffon. It doesn't do as much damage as you might assume, but it's still nice!

It's much easier to be "in your face" with Ifrit than with Alastor, due to the homing nature of Ifrit's flame kick. For example, if you want to bring one of Griffon's lightning attacks to an end in a hurry, do a flame-kick to his face in between the lightning bolts. As a caution, occasionally horrible camera angle changes are incurred this way, however.

You can fight long range with Ifrit via fully-charged Meteor 2s - whenever one of Griffon's ground attacks has just finished, you'll have enough time to charge up a Meteor until it automatically releases, which will hit Griffon 1 for respectable damage. Unlike with Griffon 2 & 3, however, it seems to be impossible to get a "double hit" (a single Meteor hitting two different points of Griffon's body for double damage) against Griffon 1.

Apologies for the rather short Ifrit section (although maybe that's a blessing for some readers :p). It's been a long time since I've played a maxed game and hence had Ifrit with all abilities by Mission 9. Also, I've never made a speciality of the "in your face" method of fighting Griffon the way some other SB players have, so I'm a bit limited in describing it.


The rest of the mission

After the tough Griffon 1 fight, the rest of the mission is thankfully rather straightforward. Use Ifrit to enter the misty wood area. Jump on top of the tree stump to collect 50 red orbs - from there, you can do a tricky Air Hike to the right to grab hold of a ledge with 40 red orbs and a Holy Water. You can also do a wall-jump to grab the edge of the ledge and haul yourself up, or fly over to it from the top of the building with the green orb. Once finished with collecting, trigger the "Do or Die" countdown with Marionettes and Fetishes - this is easy to get through with liberal use of grenades and Vortex 2.

Once the spell on the door is broken, enter the building (this is where you can pick up the grenade gun again if you missed it in Mission 8), and pass into another outdoor area. To the right of the stairs is a narrow corridor - you may want to wall-jump inside there first and slash or Vortex the orb-fountain before awakening the Fetishes. Once you've awakened the Fetishes, then, as before, they can be easily taken out with grenades and Vortex 2 - just be careful not to get hit by a projectile attack from offscreen. You can carry on until the Fetishes have stopped spawning, or you can exit early - but don't exit until you know you have reached the 1500 red orb requirement!


Alternative (Griffon 1-less) walkthrough for a fresh game

This isn't my strategy, but sternn's - at any rate, I watched it in one of his Mission 9 videos :p

At the beginning of the mission, go back into the castle and run back up to the room below the rusty key room in the castle - where you fought the Fetishes in Mission 1. There will be more spawning Fetishes this time, but you only get about half the usual number, e.g., 7 or 8. Kill them all, then progress to the pillars area and pick up the 150 red orbs there. Defeat the Blades in the field. Pick up Ifrit, exit the floating platforms area to trigger the Griffon fight, and enter it again - this time there will be Blades, not the markings for a Death Scissors fight. Now just fight the Blades until they stop spawning - a relatively safe place for doing this is up on the left lower balcony, where you get a nice top down view of the Blades. After defeating all the Blades, carry on with the rest of the mission as normal.


Normal mode

Normal mode is the same as DMD mode, except that you get a few scattered Marionettes in the floating platforms area in a fresh game (I think you get the same in a maxed game, but to be honest I don't know) and Griffon 1 is almost stupidly easier - his daunting web of lightning attacks have been reduced to a single V-Bolt - that's it. (Still avoid Air Raid, though, as if you get too close to him in the air, he'll do two V-bolts at a random angle instead).

In a fresh game, just fight Griffon 1. Really, just fight him, and don't bother with the re-enter/exit trick either. >_>


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 10 =- -= Canyon of Mist =-
_______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 4:30
Orbs: 600
Damage: 0%

This mission is much shorter than Mission 9, but will probably still give the average player a bit of trouble. Try to buy enough purple orbs so that you have at least 9 DT runes. It will make your life easier at the end of the mission if you do so.

At the beginning, Stinger along the pathway. Kick-jump onto the top of the first beam structure with hanging cow skulls that you come across to nab 50 hidden red orbs - jump onto the right hand pole. Continue to the stone tablet, skip through the inscription, and release the white light. Now embark upon an orgy of Fetish slaying, using Vortex to close down the distances between the Fetishes in a hurry. Once the Fetishes have stopped spawning, follow the white light - you don't need to wait for the light to exit first. As long as it's bobbing along a chosen pathway, you can exit through the portal before the light does and not be dumped back at the beginning.

The next canyon is annoying as hell, because the first Blade can sometimes get in a cheap hit as you are recovering. Try jumping away the moment you regain control of Dante, and the Blade will mostly (although not always) miss you. Take out the three Blades with Vortex, the grenade gun, etc. Depending on how long you spent in the previous area, more Blades will sometimes start spawning after you've killed the initial three. If you rushed through the previous area, Blades will definitely spawn, but if you stayed to kill all the Fetishes, then they won't - don't ask me why. If you don't get spawning Blades, pay a visit to the stone tablet to collect 50 hidden red orbs. These can be collected in the next area if you fail to pick them up here.

In the final misty canyon, two Shadows await you. Switch to E&I; once you regain control of Dante and run forwards - you will mostly see the first Shadow preparing its spike attack, although it can choose to hurl itself at you instead. If it does the latter, evade, then wail on it with DTed E&I; shots as fast as possible - you may still be able to expose the core before the other Shadow can interfere. If the first Shadow does its spike attack, however, then jump on the spike, fire E&I;, and try to expose its core right after the spike retracts. When it's exposed, try to turn the Shadow red, but watch out for the second Shadow - if it sends a spike, evade, then nip back in. If you fail to turn the Shadow red, curse, and try to expose a core again ASAP.

It's almost impossible to prevent the second Shadow from DTing - you have to kill the first one amazingly fast for it not to happen. After the first Shadow has exploded, jump on the spike of the DTed Shadow to safely wear it down from a distance - taunt, if need be, for DT, but not too close! Air Hike is a must in an area where there are no ledges to jump up to and where an angry DTed red Shadow is on your tail. Needless to say, once the second Shadow explodes, don't forget to watch which way the white light goes, unless you want a 50% chance of being dumped back at the beginning!

In the courtyard are two rock-spewing, leaping Kyklops. If you've got 9 DT runes, that will just be sufficient to polish off one Kyklop if you go nuts on it with Air Raid. If your gauge isn't full, then you can replenish DT by exiting the courtyard and taunting the Fetish you will find in the canyon - this time round, you can go back into the courtyard through either portal. Once one Kyklop has gone down, go on the evasive until DT is restored, then go nuts on the second Kyklop with Air Raid - if you run out of DT whilst Air Raiding a Kyklop, use the grenade gun to refill the gauge or go for Air-Hiked slashes on their behinds.


Normal mode

Altogether an easier kettle of fish. In the first canyon, you will get mostly Marionettes, interspersed with an occasional Fetish. I wouldn't orb-farm here, as it doesn't yield very many red orbs. Instead, follow the white light through to the next area as soon as you can. In the second canyon, you will get three Sins and their irritating scythes - use either Air Raid or the shotgun to kill them. As before, there will be 50 hidden red orbs at the stone tablet in either this area or the next one. In the third canyon, you will get three Blades - again, jump the moment you regain control of Dante to evade that damned cheap lunge.

In the courtyard, the two Kyklops almost roll over and die for you. Not only will 4-5 DT runes of Air Raid defeat a single Kyklop now, but the second one obligingly remains inactive until you trigger it into action by approaching it. Exit and re-enter the courtyard one or two more times and defeat the Kyklops to ensure you meet the red orb requirement for this mission.


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 11 =- -= Fate =-
_______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 5:30
Orbs: 1000
Damage: 0%

At the very beginning of the mission, a Death Scythe will appear. With a bit of luck, you can defeat it in under 10 seconds: stand still until it comes into view on the screen, then DT, jump up, Air Hike forwards and land a downward slash on its head. This will often provoke the Death Scythe into doing two more swoops in a row - ergo, one dead Death :p

Once the Death Scythe has been killed, a floating platform will materialize. Ignore it, switch to the grenade gun, and jump into the well to meet Blades galore! The safest way to deal with them is to Vortex them all to death, then break the cracked wall and Vortex the Blades behind the wall, too - if you want to reach 'Stylish' with Vortex, then you'll have to break the wall first (yes, the auto-targeting hates and despises you), release the Blades from behind it, and then use Vortex.

You can grenade roll in the well, but there aren't exactly many safe spots. One possibility is in front of the divinity statue - it's not ideal, but it occasionally works. A better spot, however, is underneath the exit - before jumping into the well, equip Ifrit, then inside the well shatter the cracked wall with an Inferno, jump up, and go to the end wall underneath the exit. Camp out there and grenade-roll to and fro like crazy. You'll rocket up to 'Stylish' in no time at all, as well as killing all the Blades surprisingly quickly - the slowdown which occurs is quite amusing, too :p ... It's a great strategy when it works, but you will sometimes take a nick by a Blade and have to reset, or, worse, accidentally jump out of the well... >_>

After every single Blade is dead, nab the blue or yellow orb which appears in the centre of the well, then go and get the Sign of Chastity in the glass corridor. There are 200 hidden red orbs on the ledge. You seriously don't want to miss out on those, so Air Hike or wall-jump onto to the ledge and run around until you trigger the dropping of the orbs. Stinger off it; back down in the well, the Blades will have spawned again. Quickly jump forwards to evade a lunge from the nearest Blade, and make a run for it. You can stay and fight, but as long as you fought all the Blades the first time around, it isn't necessary to fight them a second time. If you need DT, pause and turn around to grenade the Blades behind you a few times on your way out of the well.

You can, by the way, fight the Blades first and the Death Scythe second (or not fight the Death Scythe at all - it's not a compulsory fight). It just means you will have to deal with a Death Scythe in the glass corridor, where it can be a humungous pain up the arse.

Back in the starting area, there's a few goodies to be collected - collect them if you are so minded. You can use the (slooow) moving platform to collect two red orbs in mid-air worth 20 red orbs each. Once you're done, exit this area through the lower door, use the Sign of Chastity to unlock the Chalice, and trigger the appearance of...


Boss Battle - NELO ANGELO 2


Nelo's moves

Exactly as Nelo Angelo 1, except:

Punch-punch-High Time combo - Nelo will swing his body round clockwise and do a low punch, followed by a very quick left-handed, Magma Drive-style, uppercut. He will invariably follow these two quick punches with either a grounded or rising High Time - the rising High Time can be followed by a downward slash or diagonal kick. The punches can easily be parried with Rolling Blaze, but you can't make Nelo slump over - probably because you are making contact with his body, rather than his sword. Just jump away from Nelo to evade this combo.

Punch-Roundhouse kick combo - A slower combo than the one above, Nelo will swing his body round clockwise, do a gut-level punch, pause for a split second, then swing his body round clockwise again, doing a Roundhouse-style kick as he does so. This is normally a stand-alone combo, after which Nelo will taunt. The punch or the kick can be parried, but again you can't make Nelo slump over. As before, just jump away from Nelo to evade.

Diagonal kick - Nelo can only do this at the end of a rising High Time; it resembles a glowing blue version of Ifrit's flame-kick, but luckily doesn't home in as well. Nelo will always taunt after completing any combo which ends with a diagonal kick.


Battle

Everything written about Nelo 1 applies to Nelo 2 too. However, there are a few differences, which I'll list below:

- Nelo 2 has about a third more health more than Nelo 1
- He will block after every 9-10 slashes now, instead of 17-19 slashes
- He has a few new martial arts moves (outlined above)
- He is a bit more likely than Nelo 1 to mix up his attacks
- The battle arena is different

Most of the differences are self-explanatory, really. You probably won't notice Nelo 2's extra defense much, if at all; Nelo doesn't have particularly high defense, even in DMD mode. And the fact that he doesn't teleport up to a higher tier at a preset stage of the battle makes the Nelo 2 battle seem about as short as the Nelo 1 battle, in my opinion.

Sword-cancelling is a bit harder now, due to the fact that you must jump away after as few as 8 slashes (or 4 pairs of 2-Hit combos) to guarantee that Nelo doesn't block you - if you are not that comfortable with triggering Nelo's meteor, this will make the Sword-Cancelling strategy quite a bit more difficult in fact. Added to that, the cramped and cluttered environment also doesn't make it any easier.

Nelo 2's new moves aren't likely to make an amazing amount of difference as they're pretty easy to avoid from by and large. (The diagonal kick is probably the most dangerous addition: if Nelo does a rising High Time, keep well away from him.) Although Nelo 2 is more likely to mix up his attacks now, he will still mostly do the 4-Hit combo if you run up close enough to him to provoke him into doing an attack.

The biggest difference of all probably lies with the battle arena rather than Nelo himself! It's far smaller and more enclosed, with pillars and other bits and pieces to obscure your view. There is a narrow brown ledge running all the way around the arena. There are also two flights of stairs each leading down through a very short tunnel into a lower area. Nelo, with his teleporting abilities, can access any area of the arena. For pity's sake, don't go inside the tunnels unless you want to be a sitting duck. As for the lower area (the place with the green orb), it's not the usual place to fight Nelo, that's for sure: you certainly won't see a single Nelo 2 video set there. However, I've tried it and it's not too... bad. Just avoid from going to the exit at all costs (where you will get the worst camera angle change in history), and you should be okay.

In case you haven't figured it out yet, the place that Nelo is generally fought in is the area between where the Sign of Chastity is used and where the entrance is. You could jump up to the narrow ledge, however - it is actually quite fun duelling Nelo on the ledge, plus Sword-Cancelling is a bit easier on there, too, because Nelo doesn't get pushed back into awkward niches.

At any rate, I favour Ifrit for the Nelo 2 battle: if you have SBed every mission up until now, then you should be able to afford all of Ifrit's abilities. Rolling Blaze is a godsend in a cramped area such as this. Every Ifrit combo outlined in the Nelo 1 battle will work here too. You begin battle right in front of Nelo this time (who once again opens with a taunt) - if you want to do an Inferno, you will have to jump straight up to do it, you'll be that close to Nelo.

One last thing to mention: occasionally Nelo will teleport up to the narrow ledge and do his blue meteor attack. If this happens, grenade-roll, using the charging up sound of the meteors to judge when to roll - if you hear a clanging sound, Nelo has blocked and you can safely charge up a Meteor 2 - expect him to come down in a hurry after the Meteor 2 has struck! This is unlikely to happen, though, but I'm just mentioning it as a possibility.

Good luck - this ain't a very difficult mission or battle, really :p


Ending the mission

Once Nelo 2 is defeated, don't move from the spot you collect 500 red orbs from, but instead switch to Alastor, jump straight up and Air Hike forwards to land on the square platform with the green orb. From there, do a Stinger leap towards the tiny room where the Chalice is. After using the Chalice, hold R1 and press Triangle to hop down to the now unlocked door below.


Normal mode

The Death Scythe is exactly as described in the DMD walkthrough. Jump down into the well, where you'll get one lone Blade - if you Stinger it from the front, you can quickly run round behind it to Stinger it in the back, which will send it flying to land belly-side down, so that you can get a critical hit (careful to only hop-jump so you don't jump out of the well). Behind the cracked wall, you will get four more Blades, which you can easily take out with Vortex.

Collect the Sign of Chastity and the 200 red orbs. I recommend fighting the Blades on the way back, as fewer Blades makes meeting the red orb requirement for Normal mode harder. In the starting area, feel free to collect the goodies. Nelo 2 is as DMD Nelo 2, except with the usual lesser health.


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 12 =- -= Ghost Ship =-
_______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 4:00
Orbs: 700
Damage: 0%

Bah. Four minutes to defeat four Marionettes, two Frosts, a Death Scythe and Griffon 2. Easy :p

You start the mission with a 15-second countdown against four Marionettes - but with your trustworthy grenade gun, they won't hold you up for long. After breaking the spell on the door, take a detour to the orb fountain if you like (bearing in mind it adds on time and its orbs aren't necessary, obviously). Stinger to the ship, jump into the water, and swim through the hole. Leap out of the water and switch to Ifrit. In the next two room are two baleful Frosts which you must defeat.

Unlike in Mission 7, you can't line the two Frosts up at the beginning, so Meteor 2 is not recommended - I would use Infernoes, Auto-Combos, and Kick-13s to get rid of them in a hurry. Try doing a hop-jumped Inferno on the first Frost, followed by a DT-Cancelled Auto-Combo. After the first Frost is killed, the second one will usually cross its arms in preparation for shooting needle-thin missiles across the room - flame kick it to stop it in its tracks, then either do another Inferno > DT-cancelled Auto-Combo, or just wail on it with non-DTed attacks - Frosts go down almost stupidly easily to repeat Kick-13s. Once the second Frost has gone down, make your way to the exit, and pick up the Needlegun if you don't have it yet.

On the deck of the ship, examine the locked door to trigger the appearance of a Death Scythe - this one is a bit more of a nuisance than the one in the previous mission, but still hardly unpassable. If you wait in front of the door and Air Hike up to do a downward slash just before or as soon as your DT runes and lifebar show, you can provoke the Death Scythe into swooping down at you. Otherwise, just hang about on deck, being careful not to get caught up in any of the whirlpools, and finish the Death Scythe off ASAP.

Once the Death Scythe is dead, equip E&I;, and go through the now-unlocked door to trigger the appearance of Griffon 2.


Boss Battle - GRIFFON 2

Sadly, Griffon 2 isn't optional... <_<


Griffon 2's moves

Hologram Wing - This is the orange-red wing which Griffon attaches to you by a series of yellow strings and which used to probably be my single most hated attack in the game. Once the wing has been attached, it will always bounce you up into the air when it first touches you. After that, it will alternate between slicing through you (for damage) and bouncing you up harmlessly up into the air. If a wing has bounced you up into the air once, then evade it on its next pass before you let it bounce you up again! If you ignore a hologram wing, it will eventually disappear - but that takes far too long for it to be a viable option. To destroy a hologram wing, use E&I; - DTed shots will get rid of it much faster. You can also do well-timed Rolling Blazed jumps through the wing to damage it. Hologram wings actually come in two different flavours: weak or strong. A weak wing can be destroyed with 3-4 non-DTed E&I; shots or a single Rolling Blaze. Needless to say, a strong wing is a different kettle of fish: you'll be there for fuckin' ever with a strong wing, unless you use DTed E&I; shots to get rid of it - fire at the wing on the ground, doing a quite well-timed jump to evade it as it swings past you. If the wing bounces you up in the air, DT (if you are not already DTed), fire like crazy, and yank yourself back down to the ground as hard as possible to try and avoid the wing slicing through you in mid-air. Visually, there is no obvious way of distinguishing between weak and strong wings; the first wing of battle that Griffon sends out will always be a weak one - after that, they probably will be strong ones, unless you refrain from damaging him.

Web of Lightning (Shortened) - Griffon will send out a V-bolt from the blue disc on his chest, then follow it up with yellow traces of lightning jutting out at all angles over the battlefield. The yellow traces solidify into harmful red and white bolts. Just keep on jumping away from the yellow traces to evade - this attack only lasts for a few seconds.

Horizontal Bolts (Shortened) - Griffon will send out 2-3 horizontal bolts towards you - that's it. Nevertheless, due to the fact Griffon is often out of sight when he does this, they can take you by unpleasant surprise. Jumping to evade is best.

Divebomb/Electrocution Attack - These two attacks only happen in tandem with one another - one won't occur without the other. To begin with, Griffon starts off by hovering above one of the decks, then rises up out of sight. He shrieks, then a split second later divebombs the deck - as with the divebomb in the first battle, you have to go out of your way to get hit by it. However, you still must jump when you hear the shriek to avoid taking a sliver of damage from the shockwave when Griffon hits the ground. Once Griffon is on the deck, he will begin the lengthy preparation for his electrocution attack - the screen will darken as his body becomes surrounded by a shield of electricity bolts. When the screen is fully darkened, Griffon will then send out three pairs of homing bolts - these are a nightmare to evade if you're next to Griffon; easy if you're a distance away from him. The first bolt of each pair can be evaded with a jump or roll to the side as it approaches you; after evading the first bolt, you can remain where you are and the second bolt will pass by harmlessly. Once the attack is over, the screen will lighten, Griffon will rise into the air out of sight, shriek, and... promptly divebomb the deck again >_>


Battle

List of decks:

I'll be referring to all of these decks.

Lower deck - This is the deck which you first enter onto after having exited the area where you fought the Frosts or Blades. There is a raised cylindrical platform in the centre.

Middle deck - This is the deck which has the door with the glowing white crossed bars - the Death Scythe emerges from this door. A mast is on this deck, too.

Upper deck - This is directly above the middle deck and is right at the very back of the ship - a black ladder leads up to it from the middle deck.


NOTE - Both Griffon 2 strategies are on the prescriptive side, I'm afraid. I used to have sheer hell with this overgrown bird until, largely by trial and error, I stumbled across a way of dealing with him. You may be able to be more lax than I am with Griffon and get away with it. In the meantime, I'm just basically going to describe what works for me.


Theory:

This section will be short and sweet. Remember where I said in the Divebomb/Electrocution Attack description that "Griffon will rise into the air out of sight, shriek, and... promptly divebomb the deck again"? That's exactly what he will do 95% of the time - get Griffon to divebomb the deck once, and he will do it again and again. Rather than waste time trying to hit Griffon in the air (which is difficult enough even without his hologram wings and lightning attacks to contend with), get him to land on deck, where he is literally a sitting duck for your attacks. Anything can be used whilst Griffy is charging up his electricity bolts: Inferno, Air Raid, melee attacks - however, the two most effective strategies are described in detail below. They are quite different to one another, so pick your favourite and master it.


Meteor 2:

You'll want to entice Griffon to land on the lower deck for this strategy - to do this, you need to camp out on, guess where, the lower deck. At the start of battle, jump down to the lower deck, then erect a tent in the lower right corner and wait. (To envision where I mean, you should be, from your POV, on the right hand side of the ship and in the opposite corner to the one which has the steps leading up to the middle deck. At any rate, it's crucial you remain on the side of the ship furthest away from Griffon.) Now here comes the next crucial bit: refrain from attacking Griffon. At all. I know, I know. Other SB players get away with using Meteor 2 against Griffon from the masts, and what have you. I don't, and I'm the one writing this strategy, so ner.

Moving on... >_>.

When Griffon comes swinging in from the left, the first thing he'll do is send out a hologram wing - and it will always be a weak one. Let it bounce you up into the air straight away and you should be able to destroy it in mid-air with 3-4 non-DTed E&I; shots. (Might as well save that DT.) When you land back on deck, wait for Griffon to send out a second wing, and don't shoot at him in the meantime! When he sends the second wing, destroy it in the same way as the first one (if you refrained from damaging Griffon, the second wing should always be weak too). Now: after destroying the second wing, Griffon will always move in to hover above the lower deck for me - and hopefully if you follow this strategy, he'll do the same for you. If he doesn't, then he will probably send out 1-2 more wings instead - it's okay if he does that, as long as he moves in to hover above the deck afterwards. In the worst-case scenario, Griffon will continue to hang back from the ship and begin a near-eternal round of hologram wings + lightning attacks, but hopefully it won't come to that.

When Griffon moves in to hover above the deck, go and stand in front of the cylindrical platform (so that you're facing the right side of the ship - the side which is opposite to the side that Griffon came in from at the beginning of battle). When Griffon shrieks, jump forwards - he'll land directly behind you; turn to face him.

Whilst Griffon is charging up his electrocution attack, you have enough time to fully charge not one, but two, Meteor 2s until they automatically release - if you're directly in front of Griffon, then they'll do more damage than if you hit him from the side. Even then, not all Meteors do identical damage: in the past I found that delaying the first Meteor very slightly (by firing 3-4 shots of E&I; first) enabled me to get more damage from it - no, I don't know why. At any rate, it's not a big deal, and you'll be cutting it very fine if you do fire a bit of E&I; first: you're cutting it fine, as it is, with charging two Meteors.

Once the second Meteor has struck, run away from Griffon ASAP. (I usually run to the steps.) Then get ready to evade the first lightning bolt - after the first bolt has passed, you should remain where you to ensure the second bolt passes safely by. In between pairs of bolts, fire E&I; at Griffy for extra damage, but, more importantly, to restore the DT gauge. In my opinion, evading these bolts is the most difficult part of this strategy, but it's not intrinsically difficult: you just need to keep a cool head.

Once the final pair of bolts have passed, run back to the front of Griffon and charge up Meteor 2 - you'll have time to hit him with it before he rises up into the sky! After he rises out of sight, stand in the same place as before to evade his divebomb - when he lands, this time he will be facing left and you'll be behind him. Align yourself with his rear end and fully charge up two more Meteors - by this time, Griffon's health should be well into the red. (In fact, if you think the 5th Meteor might defeat him outright, which is possible, depending on how much damage you caused with the previous Meteors, then don't do one.) Dodge the bolts of the electrocution attack; at some point, in between pairs of bolts, switch to Alastor. Fire DTed E&I; to whittle down Griffon's health to nothing.

When you get the final shot, Griffon 2 will depart amongst a sea of bolts, so don't get it while you're next to him! Instead, get a little distance away, defeat Griffon, and then Vortex 2 like crazy, keeping the attack button held down the entire time (yes, you will veer about more wildly than one of Griffon's hologram wings, but at least you will be immune to the parting bolts). Failing that, run to the other end of the ship, where you should easily be able to evade the occasional bolt which makes its way feebly towards you.

Phew. It's not over quite yet. Below is the stuff that I didn't want to clog up the strategy itself with.

NOTE

1. In a much older version of this FAQ, I described how to stop a would-be charged Meteor from turning into a charged punch, so I must have had a problem with this at one point. (I barely remember, though.) If you find you have the same problem, then this is what I wrote: "Solve the problem by DTing, running away from Griffon for 1-2 steps, and then start charging up a meteor. It sounds counter-intuitive, but Dante will swivel round to face Griffon every single time."

(I probably don't even have this old FAQ anymore.)

2. It's possible to get double hits (a single Meteor 2 hitting two spots consecutively for double damage) against Griffon 2, but it's so difficult to do that I advise not even wasting your time and energy trying. Occasionally, I've got one whilst standing in front of Griffon, but the window of opportunity must be crazily small, since it's not something I've been able to reliably replicate and it's something you're unlikely to achieve by chance.



E&I-from-the-mast;:

This is a strategy which has been knocking around for a long time: it was first made known about by a Japanese player called Zeno, I think. At any rate, just because it's old doesn't mean it isn't any good.

The first part of this battle is identical to the first part of the Meteor 2 strategy, except that you'll want to entice Griffon to land on the upper deck this time. It doesn't particularly matter where you are for the Griffon's first wing (although I do remain on the right hand side of the ship), but for Griffon's second wing, you must make your way to the right hand corner of the middle deck - the corner which has the black ladder. Get as far into the corner as you can before Griffon sends the wing; if you don't, you may land on the mast as you fall down, and that's not good news.

At any rate, let the second wing bounce you up and destroy it in mid-air; it should always be a weak one if you haven't damaged Griffon. As you fall, you should see Griffon swinging around from the front to the left side of the ship. Wall-jump up onto the upper deck (no need for ladders around here), and then run around like a moron in circles - I'm not really sure if it makes any actual difference or not, but it makes me feel better. Whilst you're running around, Griffon should move in to hover above the upper deck and then rise up out of sight - as soon as he shrieks (preferably 1-2 seconds earlier, if you can judge it right), Air Hike up onto the 2nd square platform. (Please now refer to my great ASCII diagram :p)


The Mast:



-----X---@-------X- <- crow's nest; 2nd beam
         |
         |
        === <- 3rd square platform
         |
         |D
--------===-------- <- 2nd square platform; 1st beam
         |
         |
        ===  <- 1st square platform; level with the upper deck
         |
         |



D = where Dante lands after Air Hiking from the upper deck
X = where Dante stands to shoot at Griffon
@ = crow's nest


From the 2nd square platform, you'll need to go to the crow's nest at the top as fast as possible: luckily, Dante can magically Air Hike through the square platforms - just stand next to the pillar and Air Hike straight up until you reach the top. By the time you reach the crow's nest, the electrocution attack will have started, but you don't need to worry about it - the bolts will pass harmlessly underneath you. Instead, run to the right hand beam and find the 'sweet' spot: the spot where you can fire DTed E&I; at Griffon for twice the normal damage! This spot can vary, but standing at the end of the beam is infallible for me - although it can be a third to halfway along the beam, too. Once you've found the right spot, you can't mistake it, as even on DMD mode the life will fall off Griffon.

Plug away with DTed E&I; like a maniac until the screen lightens (it can be difficult to tell when it does this high up - the easiest way is to observe the change from shadow to light of the beam you're standing upon). As soon as it lightens, move to the centre of the beam, DT, Air Hike off, and land a good, satisfying DTed downward slash on Griffon. (To prevent it accidentally turning into a Vortex, let go of the analogue stick entirely as you slash or hold it backwards.) On the ground, de-DT, do a double taunt as Griffon rises into the air, then, once again, just before or as you hear the shriek, Air Hike onto the second platform and make your way upwards to the crow's nest - Griffon will land facing left rather than right.

This time, go to the left beam and stand about a third of the way along. Again, plug away with DTed E&I; like a maniac; when the screen lightens, you can do one of two things. You can either Air Hike back down to the ground as before (it's exactly the same as the first time, except that when Air Hiking off you must swivel to the right first to ensure that you land in the middle of the deck); or you can take a chance with remaining on the beam. If you go for the first option, then you'll need to go to the right hand beam for a 2nd time. If you go for the second option, then refrain from shooting at Griffon as he rises into the air; there's a good chance that he'll totally ignore you and prepare to divebomb the deck again - hold R1 (to prevent hopping down!) and jump up at the same time that Griffon lands to prevent taking a sliver of damage from the shockwave. After Griffon lands, you will easily be able to finish him off in that turn.

If you've just read the last paragraph, you might well wonder why you can't stay on the right beam after Griffon's first electrocution attack ends. It's because it doesn't work; instead of ignoring you and doing the divebomb attack, Griffon will plague you with lightning attacks and hologram wings - you need to jump down onto the ground to entice Griffon into doing his divebomb again. Having said that, it's been a long time since I've tried this and it's just possible I was doing it wrong. (E.g., provoking Griffon by continuing to shoot after the screen lightened). I feel too lazy to test it, but if anybody else does and tells me it's possible to remain on the right beam, I will duly credit them.

Anyway: once you've got the finishing shot on the mast, drop down to the ground, pick up the 500 red orbs, and run to the exit. (No need to worry about parting bolts: they won't hit you.)

This is the somewhat more difficult strategy to master, in my opinion, but the fact that you bypass both the electrocution attack and the parting bolts makes it worth it.


Normal mode

Getting enough red orbs can be problematic in Normal mode, so I would attack the orb fountain with Vortex 2 or the 4-Hit combo with HT finisher - that leaves you free to run past the Blades. Up on the deck, deal with the Death Scythe the same way as on DMD mode, then trigger the battle with Griffon 2. The Web of Lightning attack has been reduced to a single V-bolt, but otherwise Normal Griffon 2's attacks are identical to Hard/DMD Griffon 2's attacks.

Normal Griffon 2 has vastly less health: two fully-charged Meteor 2s can defeat him outright if you are standing right in front of him! Therefore, if you adopt the Meteor 2 strategy you need to be careful - only partially charge the second Meteor. As for the E&I; strategy, just one trip to the top of the mast is enough to defeat Griffon. Stay on the mast, though; don't jump down when the screen goes light and you should be able to defeat Griffy before he can start anything.


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 13 =- -= Abyss =-
_______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 2:30
Orbs: 200
Damage: 0%
