A nice short, easy mission. In the captain's room, collect the Staff of Hermes to trigger a skippable cutscene. Swim through the air vent, make your way down the left hand side of the room and start firing at the Blades when you get within mid-range distance. Press R1 + Triangle + Back to swim backwards if the Blades start getting too close to you. Once they've been defeated, collect their orbs, and swim over to the exit located next to where the Needlegun was picked up from.

In the basement are three Blades which are best ignored, unless you fancy trying to take 'em out without a single hit. Instead, swim slightly forwards, swivel right, and take aim at the lone barrel - it contains a large orb worth 100 red orbs. Then swim over to the blue/yellow orb, pick that up, and use the Circle button to swiftly rotate counter-clockwise - you may wish to take a pot shot at another lone barrel on the right to collect 20 further red orbs before hastily swimming upwards to the exit.

In the room where you killed the first pair of Blades, swim to the other exit. After entering the final room, don't move from where you are, but swivel slightly clockwise and kill the further away Blade. Swim forwards to collect its orbs, rotate counter-clockwise, and kill the second Blade who, by now, will be swimming towards you from the other side of the room. Swim through the ship's hole to end the mission.


Normal mode

Exactly as DMD mode.


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Intermission =-
_______________________________________________________________________________


Another intermission in which there's absolutely nothing of any interest to do.

Well, except for completing SM7 (Water Cell) and collecting a few goodies - a blue orb, an Untouchable and a Holy Water. If you want to orb farm, this intermission ranks somewhere between "useless" and "a total waste of time", since there are only Marionettes and underwater Blades to choose from for your orb-farming kicks.

top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 14 =- -= Deep Darkness & Towering Mountains =-
_______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 5:00
Orbs: 500
Damage: 0%

For a non-boss mission, this can prove surprisingly tricky, although there are easier ways than others of getting through it.

Switch to the gun you feel most comfortable getting critical hits with against Sin Scissors, then begin the mission. Go down the stairs to get confronted immediately by a Sin Scissors. Take out at least the first two Sin Scissors. There are a total of eight Sin Scissors in this underground area, but once you've killed the initial three, only one will spawn at a time. If you don't kill any, you will have three Sins on your tail the entire time - one is dealable with; three are just a pain up the arse.

The underground area is slower to get through if you kill every Sin, but it will net you over 300 red orbs, as well as making the spike trap easier to negotiate. Don't stay put, but walk slowly down towards the skeleton, killing each Sin as they spawn. An easy way of telling when you've killed the eighth, and final, Sin is to listen - if there are still more to come, the music will continue after the Sin has died. If it's the final Sin, the music will stop instantly once it has been slain - you can now raise the lever without any fear of being bothered by enemies. The quickest way to clear the spike trap is to Vortex through it - if you jump forwards and time the Vortex when you're close to the ground, you can turn right at the end without being hampered by the low exit - otherwise, you'll have to drop down, being careful not to land on a patch of spikes. When Vortexing along the spike trap floor, don't stop until you've reached a clear patch of ground - that will enable you to re-position yourself in safety.

If you decide not to kill all of the Sins, then the underground area will be faster, but more dangerous to get through. Kill a minimum of two Sins at the beginning at least, then make your way down to the skeleton, letting a solo Sin follow you - if you kill it right before raising the lever, you'll buy yourself some time to negotiate the spike trap in peace for a while before another Sin appears. A non-DTed way of getting through the spike trap area is to run along the left hand wall, Air Hiking over a patch of spikes as soon as they retract - DT, to go a bit faster. Just don't Vortex, not while there is a Sin floating about...

Phew! You're out of there. In the "towering mountains" area (towering? Oh right: like the "deep darkness" was really deep), there is a lot of stuff to be collected, although bear in mind the tight 5:00 time limit. An Air Hike to the right will nab you a blue orb. To get on top of the dome, the easiest method is to stand in front of the elevator, jump forwards, then Air Hike back on yourself to grab hold of the edge - a leaping Stinger from there will take you over to the right hand ledge on the cliffs, if you strongly desire the 40 red orbs and the yellow orb to be found on them.

Once done in the towering mountains area, exit to an area with yet more cliffs - and enemies. In Hard/DMD, the enemies will be either three Shadows or a pack of Blades - if you don't like the enemy, then you can exit and enter until a different enemy appears. I seriously don't recommend taking on the three Shadows for time, damage and red orb reasons. For Blades, the top ledge with the exit is a good spot to fight them. The initial group are the hardest, since there are 4 of them - if you use Vortex, be careful not to accidentally exit >_>. Otherwise, keep on grenading them and go for critical hits where applicable. Once the first batch have been defeated, then it gets much easier as the Blades will usually hop up one at a time - get right behind each one (as close as you can get), fire a grenade, and the opportunity for a critical hit should be yours. Keep close to the wall to avoid stray falling boulders.

Incidentally, if you critical-killed all the Sins earlier, then you will only need to kill a couple of Blades to guarantee meeting the red orb requirement - don't stay to kill every last Blade, as you'll probably go over time. Alternatively, you can ignore the Blades entirely in favour of slashing the orb fountain at the top.

Getting to the orb fountain on DMD mode is a bit of a to-do, to say the least. I don't advise taking the bridge at the top, because you will probably get hit by the falling boulders as you cross - Rolling Blaze only offers partial protection and Vortex will send you haring off towards any nearby enemies... In which case, you'll have to take another route there. Go down the pathway, hugging the wall as you go to avoid boulders - about 2 meters from the end, do an Air Hike up to a higher ledge: it will have a blue or yellow orb on it. From there, you can do another (rather tricky) Air Hike up to the ledge with the bridge and orb fountain - you can get hit as you run past the bridge, but you would be a bit unlucky if you did - it doesn't tend to happen. Anyway, at the orb fountain you're safe from falling boulders entirely. Do the usual to get as many red orbs as you can, then jump down and exit the area.

In the garden, you'll get a compulsory fight (on DMD mode) with a Death Scythe. After entering, run forwards to the other side of the area, so that the camera zooms out and you can easily see the Death Scythe swooping towards you - kill it with the usual DTed Air-Hiked slashes. Before paying another pathetic sum of red orbs, you can jump into the narrow corridor to the right of the steps, go to the very end (the end next to the spellbound door), and trigger the dropping of 20 red orbs. In the next room, collect the 25 red orbs, and end the mission.


Normal mode

Normal mode is different to its DMD counterpart in a number of ways. Firstly, you won't get any Sin Scissors in the underground area, so the option of orb-farming the Sins doesn't exist. (Deal with the spike trap area as outlined in DMD mode, though.) Secondly, you get three, rather than two, enemy groups in the cliffs area: there are three Blades (no more will spawn after them either), two Shadows, or a Death Scythe. Thirdly, there is no Death Scythe in the garden area - only respawning Marionettes with the occasional Fetish.

Orb-farming opportunities galore, then - not. Your best bet for meeting the orb requirement is to get critical hits on the two Shadows - but don't worry, it's quite easy here. Take the tunnel through the cliffs up to the bridge and cross over it - while you're waiting, you may as well slash the orb fountain! Once the first Shadow appears on the bridge, shoot at it to provoke it, then jump down to the ground - the angered Shadow should follow you immediately. The great thing about this is that the second Shadow will be off in the tunnel somewhere and won't appear for ages, giving you loads of time to take out the first Shadow undisturbed. When the second Shadow appears, kill that with a CH, too - ergo, one red orb requirment met, and you can rush through to the end.

The falling boulders only appear on DMD, btw: not on Normal or Hard.

If you don't want to CH Shadows, meeting the red orb requirement will be tougher, but not impossible. Get the red orbs in the towering mountains area, kill the Blades or Death Scythe, shatter the red orb fountain, then kill all the Maris and Fetishes until they stop respawning - you should be okay on orbs, then.


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 15 =- -= Wheel of Destiny =-
_______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 10:00
Orbs: 1400
Damage: 0%

NOTE: The generous time limit of 10:00 covers completion of Secret Mission 9, whereas all other time limits up to now have acted as though the secret missions didn't exist (even though an unknown amount of time is allocated for secret missions). If you don't do SM9, then you will have loads of time in which to do Mission 15. If you do do SM9, then the time limit will be more stringent, obviously - the ranking points from the secret mission will award a whole extra 30 seconds of time, anyhow, to make it effectively 10:30. W00t? >_>


At the beginning of the mission, decide which Emblem Shield area you want to go to first - the Emblem Shield area you enter first will always contain the Pair of Lances; likewise the Emblem Shield area you enter second will always contain the Nightmare-Beta. (Or nothing at all if you have already picked up the NB from a previous game.)


Blue Emblem Shield

Before going down the stairs, pick up the glowing Luminite - you will need it to progress. There are a puny 20 red orbs on the semicircular ledge above you, which you can collect, but they're hardly necessary. Go down the stairs and grenade or taunt the Fetishes until DT is fully restored, then go through the blue door into a dimly lit, blueish-grey area. In this underground area is a Death Scythe - you can kill it if you like, but in my opinion it really is a bloody waste of time to do so.

Instead, I would run straight through it - DT the moment as you enter, then start running! As long as you don't hesitate or stop, the first two banks of spikes won't hit you, but you may need to pause slightly to let the third bank of spikes retract before moving on. At the poison pit area, jump up and fly over to the podium with the lances. Press R1 to drop down, pick up the lances, then fly back over to the other side and drop down - again, as long you immediately start running and don't stop, the first two spike banks won't hit you, but you will definitely need to pause at the third one this time - once the spikes have retracted, run forwards and exit the area.

You can do the above with 9 runes of DT. The Death Scythe will largely leave you alone - if it swoops down to take a swipe, then jump away to evade. (Not into the nearest bank of spikes, obviously :p)

Once back in the circular room with the Fetishes, there is another room behind a breakable wall full of goodies. Get rid of all the spawning Fetishes with the grenade gun and Vortex, then go into the room full of goodies to pick up:

- Blue or yellow orb
- Holy Water
- 1000 red orbs! (Smash the small golden urn first)

Not a bad haul.

Incidentally, it is possible to enter this room without awakening the Fetishes, as long as you are approaching it from the stairs and not the underground area. Run down the stairs, move carefully forwards until there is a camera angle change, turn to face the breakable wall, then grenade three times, hop-jumping up each time to cancel the lag. (Or you can do a roll to the left to cancel the lag, too.) Once the wall is busted, you can sneak into the room without waking up any of the Fetishes.

What's the point?? Well, it's mildly useful to know if you have entered the red Emblem Shield area first and hence are only entering the blue area to collect the goodies, not pick up the lances.


Red Emblem Shield area

If you enter this area first, the luminite will be in the same spot as in the blue Emblem Shield area. Pick it up, and kill the Fetish which wakes up. Go down the stairs and through the blue door into a brighter, reddish underground area this time. Use Stingers on both of the dials to let down two bridges.

Now: I have almost entirely ignored anything to do with collecting blue orb fragments up to this point, but I think I will make an exception here. If you want the blue orb fragment in this area, don't pick up the lances just yet! Instead, drop down from the podium to trigger the dropping of the blue orb fragment first. Once it's dropped down, jump back up to the top, and collect it along with the lances.

If not interested in collecting the blue orb, then ignore the last paragraph. Instead, use the bridges to jump over to the lances and pick 'em up - this will trigger a brief cutscene of the bridges rising back up, making access to the podium impossible after that. (This is why the blue orb must be triggered first.) Drop down from the podium and run past the Shadows - if the DTed Shadow proves hair-raising to get past, a wussy solution (in a maxed game at least) is to equip the Bangle of Time :p

Back in the circular room with the Fetishes, you can choose to slay them or ignore them as you please. There is little of note in the room behind the breakable wall - a total of 100 red orbs and one yellow orb; that's it.

If, for whatever reason, you choose to do the red Emblem Shield area first, you must pay a visit to the blue Emblem Shield area afterwards to smash the golden urn for its 1000 red orbs - otherwise you won't be able to meet the 1400 red orb requirement.

---

Back out in the field, night time has fallen and there is yet another Death Scythe laying its whirlpools about the place. Ignore it, and run to the coliseum door to use the Pair of Lances instead. Inside the coliseum, run up to the blue disc and skip through the text to trigger a fight with...


Boss Battle - GRIFFON 3

My favourite of the three fights :p


Griffon 3's moves

As with Griffon 1, I'll divide Griffon 3's moves into ground and aerial ones, beginning, this time, with the aerial ones.

Aerial:

Web of Lightning - Griffon will shriek and send out a single V-bolt which converges together, followed by yellow traces of lightning across the battlefield. As ever, the yellow bolts solidify into harmful red and white bolts. The most common variation by far is a low-lying V-bolt and yellow traces which lie close to the ground. But if Dante gets to a higher position (say by using the pink discs), then the V-bolt will appear much higher up, and the yellow traces will jut out at all heights. Once Griffon 3 begins this attack, it won't end until he is made to fall down from the sky. It's evadeable for a while by jumping away from the bolts, but sooner or later you will get hit - therefore if Griffon starts this attack, it's imperative to bring him down from the sky ASAP.

Horizontal Bolts - Griffon will send out several quick horizontal bolts in a row - keep running and jumping to evade them. You'll rarely see these while you remain on the ground. Just try going up into the stands, however, and then seeing how quickly they appear.


Ground:

"Curtain" bolts - So-called, because they are like a curtain of bolts sweeping their way towards you. Griffon will send out 8 successive "curtains" of vertical bolts towards you (fewer, if you get hit by one or more of them). They cover a huge area of the battlefield and so can't be evaded by running away. Instead, roll - the i-frames of the roll will save your arse here. Just before each curtain reaches you, do a roll either to the left or right through it. If Griffon does this as a short-range attack, then you can roll diagonally towards Griffon to bring it to an early end. If it's done as a long-range attack, then you can evade the bolts by holding R1 and walking to and fro in-between the gaps - however, I do think that rolling is the best and most reliable method.

Line and Dot attack - Griffon will send out an initial V-bolt, and then alternate between sending out "dots" (red-white balls of electricity) and "lines" (horizontal bolts). You can, at a pinch, simply evade this attack by rolling to and fro through the horizontal bolts. But it's better to keep running left and right, jumping over the low horizontal bolts and running underneath the high ones. It requires a little bit more coordination than normal, but this attack isn't hard to evade once you get used to it.

V-Bolt attack - Extremely similar to Griffon 1's attack of the same name, except that this time Griffon will send out the horizontal bolt and the first V-bolt simultaneously - without Air-Hike's double jump, I have been caught out by this more times than I care to admit. To evade, it's best to roll through the horizontal bolt - that way you can then roll again through the converging V-bolt or else jump over it. Alternatively, you can jump over the horizontal bolt and fire E&I; to suspend yourself for long enough above the V-Bolt until it converges and disappears. If you get blown back and stunned by the gust of wind from Griffon flapping his wings, not only will you be immune to the horizontal bolt that passes over you, but Griffon will delay the sending of the first V-bolt.

Divebomb attack - Why does Griffon even bother? This is as pathetic as it is in the first and second fights. Griffon will rise up high into the sky, out of sight, and plummet back down to ground with a thud, jagged lightning at his feet. As always, jump away as Griffon descends to avoid the sliver of damage from the shockwave that accompanies his landing.

Electrocution attack - This is the same as Griffon 2's attack of the same name, except that the homing bolts will now home in upon Dante with unerring accuracy. Roll to evade the first homing bolt of each pair (as before, remain on the same spot to let the second one pass safely by). You could jump, but your timing needs to be incredibly precise - rolling really is safer. This attack is extremely dangerous at close-range, because there are far more random bolts than in earlier versions of this attack and it becomes impossible to distinguish the homing bolts from the random ones. If Griffon starts to charge up his electricity bolts at close range, either run away as far as you can or go all-out to knock Griffon down with Infernoes and Rolling Blaze before the homing bolts can come out - only go for the latter option if you're right next to Griffon and have enough DT to do a few Infernoes.

Charge'n'Peck - This is the same as Griffon 1's attack of the same name, except for one thing: he will sometimes dash towards you and stop dead. When this happens, it means Griffon is about to begin a lightning attack. Sometimes he will flap his wings first and sometimes he doesn't. If he flaps his wings and blows you backwards, then you will at least be immune to the opening bolt and further away from Griffon. If he doesn't do this, then you're stuck with having to deal with a lightning attack at extremely short range. If you get enough time, flame kick Griffon to interrupt it. Otherwise, keep rolling diagonally forwards to bring the attack to an end.


Battle

Thankfully, the Griffon 3 battle doesn't have the same prescriptiveness of the Griffon 2 battle. Oh okay then, I lie. Let me give precise instructions for the first third of the battle... <_<


The first third:

Before the battle even begins: When running to the blue disc, you should press R3 or R2 to make the camera swing round behind you before triggering the battle. That way, when it begins, Griffon will be in full view in front of you. It's not crucial, but it does make it that tiny bit easier to charge up a Meteor 2 without fluffing it up.

Trigger the battle, cancel the cutscene, wait no longer than 0.5-1 second, and charge up a Meteor 2 until it automatically releases - this should hit Griffon twice for a double hit, which in turn will normally make him rise up into the air. Once the Meteor has struck, de-DT, then jump three times to the right instead of running (not essential, but makes it more likely that Griffon will delay the start up of his web of lightning). Immediately after the third jump, DT, and charge up a Meteor - the second Meteor, if it connects, won't do anything like the damage of the first Meteor, sadly. (You can dispense with doing this second Meteor entirely if you would rather.) Start grenade-rolling or Air-Hiking around, firing E&I.; With any luck, you should be able to bring down Griffy down before he can even think about starting a WoL attack.

That's about the long and short of it. If the Meteor fails to hit twice for a double hit, then evade Griffon's charge attack and charge up another one as soon as you can. Rarely, the Meteor will strike twice for a double hit, but Griffon won't rise up into the air - a few grenades should soon send him packing, however.

If things go terribly wrong and you need an emergency method for bringing Griffon down from the sky, switch to the Nightmare Beta. It is the fastest weapon for bringing him down - if, however, the cutscene isn't triggered but Griffon is lying on the ground, then he will rise back into the air again once recovered. Taunt for DT during this time, and show him what's for with the Nightmare Beta again when he rises back up into the air - this time, the cutscene should actually be triggered.


The rest of the battle:

Okay, now you can pretty much fight Griffon how you want. I would avoid Air Raid at all costs, but otherwise almost anything else goes :p

*resounding silence*

There's more? Okay :p


Meteor 2:

There's nothing to stop you from using the grenade gun against Griffon 3; read the Griffon 1 battle section for details on a grenade gun-only strategy. However, I normally use Meteor 2 in conjunction with the grenade gun. The best times to charge up a Meteor are: immediately after any of Griffon's lightning attacks have ended; after evading a charge and peck attempt; whenever Griffon hops back from you; or whenever Griffon is lying on the ground after having been knocked down.

To make this strategy more fun (and to shorten the overall length of the battle), you can try going for double hits with Meteor - to get a double hit, you need to be more-or-less directly facing Griffon and to strike him while his head is bent low and in front of his chest. There are certain times to start charging a Meteor so that, when it automatically releases, Griffon will be in the correct pose for it to strike twice - most likely, the Meteor hits twice, because it first hits Griffon's head and then continues to travel through him to hit his chest.

One of the most reliable ways to get a double hit is after the charge'n' peck attack. As Griffon swings his head forwards to peck, do a Rolling-Blazed jump diagonally left through his head, run for about 4-5 steps - no further - then stop and charge up a Meteor. (As an additional cue, listen out for Griffon's screech and stop to charge up a Meteor at the end of it.) It works because, once you get behind Griffon, he will always screech and then quickly turn around to face you. Occasionally, though, Griffon is too close to the stands and doesn't have enough space with which to hop back and turn around and face you - if this happens, you won't get a double hit.

A couple of other opportunities for getting a double hit are whenever you have just finished evading one of Griffon's lightning attacks and are in front of him (he will always bend his head forwards and screech after completing an attack) or after the divebomb attack - as Griffon descends, do a Rolling-Blazed jump through him so that you end up somewhere behind him, then start charging up a Meteor the moment you see him do a hop back.

Don't worry if you can't succeed in getting double hits or can only get them occasionally. They're not essential to winning the battle at all; but they are fun to get... >_>

Whenever I use the Meteor 2 strategy, Griffon always falls down late on in the battle, when about 75% of his life on average has gone. If this happens, and you're low on DT, run closer to him and double taunt for DT: resume normal operations when he gets up again.


Inferno/RB/flame-kick knockdown:

As in the Griffon 1 battle, Griffon 3 can be knocked down with melee attacks. He can actually be knocked down slightly faster than Griffon 1, but - unlike Griffon 1 - he has an invulnerability period while he is lying on the floor: while the blue disc on his chest is exposed he is invulnerable. He doesn't become vulnerable again until the blue disc becomes covered up with feathers.

Knock Griffon 3 down with Infernoes, Rolling-Blazed jumps, and flame-kicks - it takes 3-4 Infernoes and a few RB deflections through Griffon and flame-kicks to his face to make him fall down. When he falls down, either double taunt for DT or charge up a Meteor - by the time it releases, Griffon's invulnerability period should be over. If you're close to Griffon's face, you can run towards it and charge up one or two hits of the PPKK combo, if you like.

I would consider equipping the shotgun for this strategy - one shot at close range will restore one DT rune. If you can shotgun-twitch, then restoring DT should be a breeze.

The biggest advantage of a relentless, in-your-face-all-the-time, strategy is that it will reduce the number of lightning attacks Griffon does by a lot. On the other hand, if he does begin one, you'll be very close to him and hence in danger of being hit - if that happens, flame-kick Griffon's front or roll diagonally towards him to bring the lightning attack to an end.

It's down to preference whether you select a long-range Meteor 2 or short-range knockdown strategy for Griffon 3 - both are very effective when they are mastered.


Some notes about the camera:

When people cite the terrible camera angles in DMC1, they sometimes give Griffon 3 as an example. Needless to say, they are wrong, but it probably isn't entirely their fault: there are some things which can trigger some truly dreadful, life-threatening, anal-raping camera angle changes. These are the things you should avoid doing at all costs:

- Using the psychedelic elevators: Do not - under any circumstances whatsoever, take the elevator to the top. On Hard/DMD mode you will probably be killed by horizontal bolts from an unseen Griffon before you can pick up the green orb. I have to say, I do think it's a bit bad of the developers to include an option to travel to the top of the coliseum when it is so useless and dangerous.

- Going into the stands: There is no need to, and it will just make the camera zoom in upon the stands, while Griffon disappears from sight but sends unavoidable horizontal bolts anyway. Also, if you go up to the stands' barrier, the camera can again zoom in, even if you don't actually clamber up into the stands - if Griffon begins a 'curtains' attack when this happens, you can still evade the sideways curtains by rolling.

- Using Air Raid: This will make the camera focus on Dante and Griffon will disappear out of view - if he begins a lightning attack in the meantime, you don't have a prayer of avoiding the bolts. For some reason, I think it's different if the mid-battle cutscene hasn't been triggered, so using Air Raid should be all right during the first third of battle. I'm not certain, though, and I don't feel like booting up DMC1 to test it - sorry.

What you should do instead:

Fight Griffon on the ground floor of the coliseum only and don't use Air Raid. You'll find that the camera works with you that way and not against you - Griffon will never begin a lightning attack from out-of-sight or behind you.

---

Once battle is over, collect the 500 red orbs, and go to the elevator in the middle - kick jump onto the top of it and take a lift down into the basement of the coliseum. End mission.


Normal mode

The enemies in the two Emblem Shield areas are different this time: in the Blue area you get three, ultra-annoying, Sin Scythes and in the Red area you get two Blades. Running past the Sin Scythes isn�t an option this time: unlike the Death Scythe, they will hit you with their scythes. They are also darned near impossible to kill without taking a hit, too. For this reason, I recommend tackling the Red area first for Normal mode � the two Blades are easily dealt with by Vortex. That way you can plunder the Blue area for its goodies (in particular, the 1000 red orb cache), but avoid having to deal with the Sin Scythes.

You will also get three Sin Scythes, instead of a Death Scythe, when it becomes dark outside. Again, these Sin Scythes are a bugger for sneaking in hits. If you go outside, trigger the cutscene of night falling, you can re-enter the Emblem Shield area and exit to have the Sin Scythes further away from you and thus less liable to hitting you.

In the first third of the Griffon battle, Griffon will take the same damage from a Meteor 2 as on DMD mode. (I�m not quite sure if this applies to other attacks, too � sorry.) This presumably is to prevent the player from taking three quarters off Griffon 3�s life with a single Meteor. At any rate, the strategy remains the same � go for a double hit with Meteor 2 at the beginning of battle, then attempt to bring Griffy 3 down from the sky as quickly as possible. Normal Griffon won't do the horrible web of lightning attack, but will content himself with sending out single V-shaped bolts only.

Once Griffon has come crashing down through the walkway, then he becomes susceptible to the usual extra damage of Normal mode again. Any of the DMD strategies can be used against Normal Griffon.


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 16 =- -= Nightmare Of Darkness =-
_______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 6:00
Orbs: 1000
Damage: 0%

It�s time to pay another trip to the castle now � getting to the castle is the easy part, however.

Be sure to collect the whole blue orb at the beginning. (There are only two whole blue orbs in the game you can find, as opposed to buying them from a divinity statue � the second one is in Secret Mission 12.) Take the rising elevator back up to the coliseum, jump off it and Stinger to the exit. Run past the Death Scythe. DT, and run up the staircase � at the top of the staircase, Vortex to the bridge. Use the Wheel of Destiny and either Vortex or Stinger across the bridge.

The castle has become a lot spookier, now that night time has fallen. But you�re not scared, are...

Get back here :p

Stinger through the two small rooms to the main hall (if you smash the suits of armour, they yield 80 red orbs in total). Go to where the Mundus statue used to be, but which has now disappeared. Switch to Ifrit and use an uncharged PPKK combo to light up the dial. Cancel the cutscene, only to be greeted with a human Plasma preparing a horizontal beam for you - jump up and flame-kick it, then kick it to death with a few Kick-13s (it will never split into more Plasmas). Alternatively, wait to jump over the horizontal beam as it approaches, then do an uncharged Meteor to kill the Plasma immediately.

Once the Plasma is defeated, switch to Alastor, collect its orbs, and Stinger over to the biplane room. In the biplane room there will be another human Plasma walking towards you. If you choose to fight it DT-less, it will probably split into three. If you do a quick Stinger followed by a grenade before exiting the room, it will give you back a rune of DT.


Corridor

Upon entering the corridor, you will get a Do or Die countdown, and one of these three enemy groups:

  • a single Shadow halfway along the corridor
  • a set of five Fetishes outside the red door, which drop down immediately
  • a set of five Fetishes which drop down halfway along the corridor

Shadow:

This is by far the worst enemy to get, in my opinion. Not only will you have to waste time with a gun change (unless you have E&I; already equipped), but, to defeat the Shadow quickly, you will need to burn DT � and you will probably have already used up some DT on your journey to the castle.

However, with a bit of practice, you can use the Shadow�s horizontal spike against it straight away. Listen out for the subtle, but distinctive, 'click-click' sound of the Shadow pacing around - if you hear it, switch to E&I;, Stinger twice up the corridor, then run forwards until the Shadow appears in view � you should get just enough time to see its face going globby and to jump up and land on its spike. If you miss this initial opportunity, then you�ll have to wait quite a while before the Shadow sends out another spike � it�s probably better to just wail on the Shadow with DTed E&I;, instead of waiting for another spike.

Do as much as you can to get DT back from the Shadow fight. After turning it red, move forward a couple of steps and do a double taunt � hell, sometimes you can squeeze in a second double taunt if you then run a few steps backwards and taunt again. Also, a second Shadow will immediately spawn after the first one has exploded � you don�t need to defeat it, but you can taunt it for DT.


Fetishes:

This is much better, particularly if you get the set halfway along the corridor. If you�re very fast in killing the first group of five, then the timer will stop counting down and the spell on the cathedral door will disappear. If, however, new Fetishes begin spawning before the first five have been killed, then you�ll have to kill the newly spawned Fetishes, as well as the first five, to stop the timer. The newly spawning Fetishes always appear halfway along the corridor, regardless of whether you are at the end of the corridor or halfway along yourself.

If you don�t kill the first five in time, don�t panic. Just Vortex the newly spawned Fetishes, and the timer will soon stop counting down. Use the grenade gun to build back DT in a hurry.

---

Once the timer has been stopped, then you can make your towards the cathedral and face your first true...



Boss Battle � NIGHTMARE 1

Dear god, how Nightmare 1 lived up to his name in my first ever DMD SB game. I actually found this mission tougher to SB than Nightmare 3's mission � however, that was largely due to me not knowing nearly as much about Nightmare as I do now.

Warning: Get ready for the longest read by far yet - sorry... :-/


Nightmare 1�s moves

I�ll divide Nightmare�s moves up into two sections � actually, three sections. The Underworld definitely merits its own section.


Gloopy Nightmare (when the dials are unlit):

Ice Slugs � Two to three large black ice slugs are sent out from Nightmare's gloop at regular intervals. These chase after Dante; if they get close they will leap up and wrap themselves around him, then transform into a crystal of ice which damages Dante upon contact. To evade an ice slug, just roll as it approaches you or wait until it rears up, in readiness for leaping upon you, then jump over it. Rolling Blaze will also deflect the ice slugs to a certain extent. Where they become the biggest pain up the arse, though, is at the dials: if the dial is already partially lit up, let an ice slug latch itself onto you, finish lighting it up, then shake it off with running and jumping.

Grab Attack � Nightmare�s green bulk is constantly shifting towards you � once it gets close enough, a part of its gloop will rise up and try to grab you. The first time that you are grabbed, you will be sent to the Underworld unharmed. After your return from the Underworld, any subsequent grab attacks will merely damage you instead. If you don't want to be grabbed, then just jump away as the gloop swirls up. Nightmare can be surprisingly quick with this attack, so watch out - sometimes you only just get time to jump away!

Body swirl � This is the same as the Grab attack, except that this time Nightmare�s whole body will rise up and swirl, not just a section of it. The preparation time is considerably slower, giving you plenty of time to get away. As with the Grab attack, the first time Dante is grabbed, he will be sent down to the Underworld unharmed, but any subsequent grabs will just damage him.

Slimes � This utterly pathetic and unnoticeable 'attack' shouldn't even be worthy of mention, but in a strict SB game it is. If you look at Nightmare's gloop, you should see small, dolphin-shaped, pieces of gloop leaping out - if one hits you, you will barely feel it and the damage is virtually unnoticeable, but it will still be "goodbye SB" in a strict game. Hence you should never take shortcuts across Nightmare's gloop, but go around it instead. The slimes can also appear when Nightmare is solid (don't forget that solid Nightmare has gloop all around his body), but not until after you have been down to the Underworld - and even then, they won't appear straight away, but will start to appear at the second, not first, Triple Stab attack. You should have defeated Nightmare before this point, anyway, but if you haven't, then you should use a long-range attack such as Meteor 2 against the rear core.


Solid Nightmare (when the dials are lit):

Machine Gun attack � Six or so small silver orbs pop out from Nightmare�s body and fire needle-thin tracking fire. This attack is done immediately after Nightmare has finished solidifying and in between his 'major' attacks � Ice Beam, Triple Stab, and Boomerang (2nd/3rd fights only). It's short-range, so just keeping a distance is enough to be out of its range, but you'll need to learn to deal with it if you are to jump on Nightmare�s back or parry the Triple Stab attack. As soon as the silver orbs pop out and start firing, throw off their tracking: do a jump in the opposite direction to the one you want to go in, then double back on yourself around Nightmare as the gunfire follows uselessly after you - you can even walk when doubling back. If you�re running around Nightmare and you feel you�re being forced further than you want to go, you can safely switch to a walk mid-run.

Triple Stab attack � Immediately after the machine gun attack has ended, Nightmare will do a sharp rotation of about 90�, so that his side faces Dante, and send out three quick tentacles in a row � even if its side is already facing Dante, it will still do a recognisable sudden movement of some sort. You can evade this attack just by jumping away from Nighty as the tentacles come out � if none of them are parried, the exposed core at the back will retract immediately after the last tentacle has come out, and Nightmare will move onto a new attack or dissolve back into gloop. If any one of the three tentacles are parried, however, then the core will stay out for a while. To parry the first tentacle, you should be running or walking down Nightmare�s side as the machine gun attack is ending � immediately after the sudden movement, do a Rolling-Blazed hop-jump forwards or a Stinger or a regular slash � if timed right, the tentacle will retract and none of the subsequent tentacles will come out. The timings for parrying are slightly different for Alastor and Ifrit � with Alastor, you need to be a fraction of a second earlier.

Ice Beam � You need to be at the front of Nightmare to trigger this attack (more on this later). Just after the machine gun fire has ended, Nightmare's top core will emerge, along with a few thin white beams which slink along the ground in a straight line from Nightmare's front - if one of these thin beams hit you, they'll damage you and stun you for long enough that you could get caught in the main beam. The thin beams are easily avoided by being anywhere except directly in front of Nightmare - which is where you shouldn't be at that point anyway. Once the thin beams disappear, the main beam will come out and Nightmare will swivel around, trying to hit you with it. The behaviour of the ice beam is dependent on the top core's colour - but more about this in the core mechanics section! For now, just know that when the core comes out, you should be at Nighty's side, ready to jump onto its back - you shouldn't be too close to Nighty, though, as, when the core first emerges, protective white rays emanate from it for about 2 seconds. If they hit you, they will both hurt and stun you. Hang back until they have receded before jumping onto Nightmare.

Projectiles � You need to be at the back of Nightmare to trigger this attack (again, more on this later). After the machine gun attack, a hatch will open up at Nightmare�s rear end, its lower core will emerge, and several waves of small, black projectiles will be fired. The lower core is safeguarded by the projectiles and will retract soon after they have finished: the only safe way to attack the core during the projectiles attack is with Vortex 2. (This is strongly not recommended in an SB game, however, as Dante will still take small slivers of damage.) To evade this attack, move away from Nightmare, then jump or roll to the side as each wave of projectiles approaches you. You can also shoot them back with E&I;, but it's not worth it except for fun.

DT-siphon � I thought the Ice Beam/Projectile descriptions were long enough already, so I "siphoned" (ho ho >_>) off this part of those attacks into a separate paragraph. At the end of both the Ice Beam and Projectiles attacks, Nightmare�s core will try to siphon away some of your DT. If nothing is done to stop the siphoning, 2.5 DT runes will be drained from Dante. It�s not a vast amount, but, still, you shouldn�t just let Nightmare take it. To break even (i.e., to neither lose nor gain DT), attack the core with melee attacks during the DT-siphoning stage. To actually gain DT, taunt � double taunt.

Nightmare DT � Nightmare is the only boss in the game which possesses an ability to DT - it's pretty limited, however. Reddish bands of electricity � similar to the ones which surround regular DTed enemies � will surround Nightmare. They only last for a couple of seconds before disappearing, but if you get hit by Nightmare�s attack just after the bands of electricity have disappeared, you will take double the usual damage. It takes Nightmare a long time to DT, so it should be defeated before you have time to see it.


Underworld

And now we end up back at gloopy Nightmare again :p

Intro:

The Underworld is an optional section of the Nightmare battle, where, if you undertake to complete it, you will face a total of six Sargassoes and one weakened Shadow boss. Your reward is 500 red orbs from the Shadow boss and about 28% taken off Nightmare�s lifebar upon re-entry to the upper world. You will always face Phantom in the 1st Nightmare fight, Griffon in the 2nd Nightmare fight, and Nelo in the 3rd Nightmare fight. The Shadow bosses have been weakened in defense only � their attacks remain as powerful as their full-bodied counterparts.

Layout:

You should definitely make an effort to learn the layout of the Underworld, as getting lost and wandering about for a minute trying to find the last Sargasso will do absolutely nothing for your time requirement. Fortunately, the fact that Dante arrives on the same spot every time after being grabbed by Nightmare makes this a considerably easier task - the only thing which varies is his orientation - the way he is facing. If you can't see the Sargassoes after landing, face inland and taunt or press R3 to make the camera swing behind Dante. Below is a map of the Underworld and the recommended route through it � you don't have to take this precise route, but there aren't exactly a ton of other routes you can take.

This is the best map that I can make in ascii and without a clear overhead view of the Underworld >_<. Still, it should give you a rough idea of the Sargassoes� positions. Ignore S6 � that is the large Sargasso, which will automatically appear after the first five Sargassoes have been killed. It is perched higher up than all the others, so there is no danger of triggering its appearance as you run past it. Kill the Sargassoes in the order I have suggested, from S1 � S5. A single grenade shot will kill �em as you approach � no need for anything fancy. You can do an Air Hike from S3 to S4 and kill S4 with a downward slash. Once you�ve killed S5, kill S6 with a grenade > Stinger/Kick 13 > grenade combo.


Shadow Phantom

A shadow of his former self ;p

Shadow Phantom�s moves

As Phantom 1 & 2.

Battle

Before battle even begins, Shadow Phantom remains unique as the only Shadow boss that you can taunt in his inactive form for DT. (Shadow Nelo doesn�t have an inactive form and taunting inactive Griffon doesn�t work, for whatever reason.) However, I wouldn�t bother with taunting inactive Phantom � grenading the Sargassoes will earn back enough DT runes for the fight ahead.

After the large Sargasso is defeated, seek out Phantom immediately and try to disrupt whatever attack he is preparing � particularly if it is the time-wasting Meteors From The Sky attack. Be observant, too, as the fire-less Phantom blends all too well into the dim and dreary landscape. Defeat Phantom with DTed Air-Hiked slashes on his rear, aiming for the weak point at the base of his tail/centre of his bulb. If Phantom escapes and starts plodding away, follow him, but don�t attack until he stops: if he does a leaping attack, evade, and then land some slashes on him while he recovers from the jump. It will only take a few well-placed strikes to take him out. Don�t even think about Air Raiding on DMD mode � even the weakened Shadow Phantom is still highly resistant to Air Raid. Once he has been defeated, nab the 500 red orbs and exit via the portal.

(I�ll deal with Shadow Griffon/Nelo in the Nightmare 2/3 fights.)



Core Mechanics

We�ll get to the actual battle eventually :p


Intro:

Nightmare has a total of three cores. Two of those cores last it over the duration of three fights, and the third one - the 'final core' - serves as Nightmare�s dying throw of the dice. Of the two main cores, the first one is the top core, which comes out during the Ice Beam and Boomerang attacks, and the second one is the lower core, which comes out during the Projectiles and Triple Stab attacks. These two main cores exist in three separate states: blue/intact, green/broken, red/broken. The states have an effect on Nightmare�s defense and also, for the top core, the behaviour and length of time the ice beam stays out for.


Core colours:

Blue/intact � Nightmare has normal defense; the ice beam stays out for the shortest length of time (about 3 seconds); Nightmare doesn�t pivot around while the ice beam is out - it can only swivel within a fairly narrow angle in front of him, which means Dante can stand at the side of Nightmare and not get hit by the ice beam.

Green/broken � Nightmare has normal defense; the ice beam stays out for a bit longer; it will pivot wildly around at least once while the ice beam is out, which means Dante is no longer safe standing at Nightmare's side.

Red/broken � Nightmare�s defense is about 2.25x* greater than that of Nightmare with a blue or green core; the ice beam stays out for much longer; it will pivot wildly around at least twice while the ice beam is out.

*This is what half-arsed measurements of Nightmare�s lifebar have shown me. I�m too lazy to do more extensive testing. You get the general idea, anyway � that Nightmare�s red core has over twice the defense of the blue/green core.

Final core � This is always red, but has the defense of the blue/green core. As damage is taken, the core shatters (but doesn�t change colour from red). This has no effect on its behaviour or defense.


The impact of having a red core in later fights:

Increased defense = less damage dealt = longer time overall to defeat Nightmare. The increased defense is partly offset by the increased length of time the top core stays out for. Nevertheless, if you reach DMD Nightmare 3 with two red cores, you will feel the pain. Think of DMD Mission 3 and meeting the time requirement for that. Then think of a pain way worse than that. That�s DMD Nightmare 3 with two red cores.


Shattering a core:

Most attacks - Stinger, sword slashes, Air Raid, Inferno - will shatter the core rather quickly. To give you an idea, 2-3 Infernoes, one bout of Air Raiding, a couple of DTed 4-Hit combos, and 6-8 DTed grenades will turn a core either green or red. You may be wondering at this stage how you will ever preserve a green core, let alone a blue one, for the Nightmare 2/3 fights. Well, by happy coincidence, the attacks which do the most damage to Nightmare are also the ones least likely to shatter his cores: charged PPKK combos, charged Meteor 2s, charged Magma Drives. Meteor 2 used to be the most popular choice of attack for SB players, but has since become 'deprecated' - PPKK combos were found to do similar damage for much less DT.


So does PPKK/Meteor 2 ever shatter a core?

Of course. But as it takes 14 Meteor 2s (charged or uncharged), you will be pretty damn unlikely to ever see Meteor 2 shatter a core, if that's all you use - in fact, it's only barely possible to shatter DMD Nightmare's top core with charged Meteor 2s. PPKK is rather more likely to shatter the cores, particularly the top one. In fact, if you don't pay a visit to the Underworld, the top core will shatter before the fight has ended. But because PPKK is so powerful against Nightmare, it's still possible to keep both cores blue throughout all three encounters. (It takes 10 charged DTed PPKK hits before the core finally shatters, if you're interested - and twice that number if the hits are unDTed.)


Preserving a blue or green core until Nightmare 3:

This is partly done by only using PPKK or Meteor 2, but for PPKK that's not enough: you'll also have to reset the cores' "remembered" hit counter to zero. Both the top and lower cores are programmed to shatter after a certain number of hits, but you can make the cores "forget" about previous hits in one of two ways. One, end the fight - at the beginning of the next fight, the hit counter will be reset to zero. Two, go down to the Underworld - when you return from it, the hit counter will also be reset to zero. Another bonus about going to the Underworld is that the damage caused upon your return has no effect on Nightmare's cores.


The top and lower cores:

The lower core at the rear of Nightmare has about 33% more resistance to shattering than its top core. A combination of both higher resistance and less attacking time at the lower core means that the top core inevitably shatters far earlier than the lower one. It will be impossible to prevent the top core from shattering at some point unless you pay a visit to the Underworld.


Core states carrying over:

I've already said the hit counter resets to zero at the beginning of a fight, so in that sense 'remembered' hits can't carry over to a new encounter - but altered core states do carry over. If you turn a core green in one fight, it will be green at the beginning of the next fight, and is irreversible. It should be noted that in the Nightmare 1 fight the cores can't be turned red, however - you can go blue in the face with Air Raid, but they will stay green throughout.


Shattering the cores tactically:

While it�s quite possible to go through all three Nightmare fights maintaining two blue cores, this isn�t necessarily the most desirable option. The lower core remains exposed for the same amount of time, regardless of its colour, but the top core doesn�t - exposure time increases with each change of colour. The most desirable colour for the top core is green, as a green core has the same defense as the blue core, but it stays out for longer � thus ensuring more time to attack it. Therefore, the top core is often deliberately shattered and turned green in the first Nightmare fight.


And finally: do I need to worry about the cores in Normal/Hard modes?

No, not really. Normal/Hard Nightmare has 2.5x less health than DMD Nightmare, but the same amount of time for each mission it appears in - you do the maths. Having said that, if you follow the DMD strategies for Nightmare, you'll lay waste to Nightmare so fast that it will be a dissolving puddle almost before you know it.


Nightmare�s Attack Patterns

We're not at the battle yet... >_>

Nightmare is an extremely patterned boss, but fortunately his pattern of attacks is an extremly simple one!

For Nightmare 1:

[Beginning of battle / After return from Underworld] Ice beam/Projectiles > Triple Stab > Ice Beam/Projectiles > Triple Stab -> [Rinse and repeat]

It could hardly be simpler. The attack pattern does change with certain factors I'm not sure about, but if you defeat Nightmare speedily, the pattern should never deviate from the one above.


For Nightmare 2 & 3:

[Beginning of battle/After return from Underworld] Ice beam/Projectiles > Triple Stab > Ice beam/Projectiles > Triple Stab > Boomerang > Triple Stab -> [Rinse and repeat]

For every 2 Ice beam/Projectile attacks, you will get 1 boomerang attack.


Substituting the Ice Beam and Projectile attacks

I have touched upon this already, but not fully explained it. You can substitute an Ice Beam attack for a Projectiles one, and vice versa, by being in a certain position in relation to Nightmare when the machine gun attack comes to an end. If you are in front, or diagonally in front, of Nightmare when the machine gun attack ends, you will trigger the Ice Beam attack; if you are at the back or sides of Nightmare when it ends, you will trigger the Projectiles attack. This is extremely useful, since it means you can substitute the waste-of-time Projectiles attack with the Ice Beam attack every single time. Note that you cannot substitute the Triple Stab or Boomerang attacks with anything else.


When the runes are lit

Pre-Underworld � Nightmare will do 1-2 attacks. If it�s the Ice Beam�s turn first, then Nightmare will do only that before turning back into gloop. If it�s the Triple Stab�s turn first, but you fail to parry it, Nightmare will go onto to do an Ice Beam attack; if it�s successfully parried, then usually Nightmare will turn back into gloop once the core retracts. But in certain circumstances, Nightmare will go on to do an Ice Beam attack instead of returning to gloop - I'll mention them when they arise.

Post-Underworld � Nightmare will do 3-4 attacks. If it�s the Ice Beam's turn first, he will normally do an Ice Beam, Triple Stab, Ice Beam, Triple Stab. (This is assuming both Triple Stab attacks are parried.) If Nighty has a top red core, then the pattern will change to Ice beam, Triple Stab, Ice Beam only � the final triple stab will be dispensed with.

---

Battle

Hooray, we�re here! :p


Intro:

In my DMD Nightmare notes, I wrote a long time ago: It helps to think of Nightmare as a blob with a series of defenses you must evade or parry to reach its core. The machine gun fire is the first line of defense, followed by the second line of defense - the core's rays as it first emerges from Nightmare. The third line of defense is the triple stab attack, boomerang, or the giant ice beam, of course.

In no other boss fight is positioning anything like this important. Nightmare tries to deter you from getting close to it with a series of defenses, but you have to get into the right position for evading or parrying said defenses in order to reach the vulnerable core. As if that weren't enough, you also have to jump on his back, throw off tracking fire, or deal with jarring camera angle changes - you'll be battling with the camera more than you will with Nightmare at times. I'll give you my method of fighting Nightmare 1, step by literal step, but it won't necessarily end up being exactly the method that you use. Certain things you will almost have to keep the same as me, of course - using PPKK combos for instance. But other things - such as which dials to light up, which gun to use (some players use E&I;, but I always use the grenade gun for Nighty), etc. - you may well end up altering because it suits you better.


A walkthrough with Ifrit:

As soon as you enter the room, light up the dial, examine the puddle, then skip the cutscene. (Incidentally, the dials wind down during the cutscene, so if you didn�t skip it, Nightmare would be liquidifying back into gloop once it was over.) As Nightmare solidifies, stand in front of him - do a taunt if you like, but don�t shoot, since that makes the camera zoom in upon Dante, which taunting doesn't. Once the machine gun fire starts, throw off the tracking fire with a jump to the right (from Dante's POV) of Nightmare, then double back on yourself, running around his front and down part of his left side � in a kind of 'U' shape.

Careful of the rays which emanate from the core as it emerges - hang back. As soon as they have receded, move in closer to jump on top of Nighty�s back - try to control the momentum of your jump, as you don't want to jump too far over Nighty. If you have problems with landing on his back, then you can target the core with a flame-kick. Jump up as high as you can, then, when you're hovering right over the core, press attack to flame-kick next to it - don't flame-kick from too low or far back a position, otherwise you will fall off or, worse, do an uncontrollable slide straight into the ice beam.

A disadvantage of flame-kicking next to the core is that recovering from the flame-kick reduces your attacking time. I would recommend learning to jump on Nighty's back without using flame-kick. You should be able to do it: if Phantom in DMD Mission 3 didn't give you enough practice at learning to control the momentum of your jumps, then I don't know what else will.

Let's assume for now that you want to turn the top core green.

As you jump on Nighty, do an Inferno upon landing. To cancel the lag of the Inferno, tap the analogue stick in any direction, then do a 2nd hop-jumped Inferno - after that, a single Rolling Blaze is sometimes enough to shatter the core, but to guarantee it, do an Auto-Combo instead. As Nightmare tries to siphon your DT, remain on his back and double taunt - twice if you can. Sometimes you will get a very brief sprinkling of machine gun fire as Nightmare is descending back into gloop (this gunfire will never appear if the top core is kept blue for some odd reason). If there is any gunfire, jump away from Nightmare immediately, even if it means cancelling out of a taunt.

Run to a dial as Nightmare returns fully to gloop - for the purposes of this walkthrough, I'll assume you choose the same dial that I always do: the one in the right hand corner which contains the blue door. After lighting the dial, run leftwards, past the main brown door, then go through the gap between the small twisted 'lamppost' on the right and the much larger twisted pillar on the left and run down the room towards Nighty - keep parallel with the side corridor, but don't actually go in it.

As you reach Nighty, there will be a camera angle change; disregard it and keep on running - as you run down Nighty's side, the machine gun fire will be ending and you will be in a very good position for parrying the Triple Stab attack - if you do an RB hop-jump diagonally forwards to parry the first tentacle, you can land right next to Nighty's rear core.

Once the Triple Stab attack is parried, you can either do one charged PPKK + P or 3 charged Meteor 2s on the rear core. The 3 Meteors do a shade more damage than the PPKK + P and I mean a shade more � nothing more than a sliver�s worth. The advantage of a DTed PPKK + P is that it uses far less DT than 3 Meteors. However, I should add that the time limit for fitting in the 1st P of the second PPKK is very tight - unless you landed right next to the core after parrying the Triple Stab attack, you won't be able to do it.

At this point, if you got in a PPKK + P on the rear core, then almost a third of Nighty's life will be gone. At this stage I usually let the de-solidifying Nightmare grab me and send me to the Underworld - see above for the Underworld section.

After returning from the Underworld, a further third of Nighty's life will be removed. Run to a dial and light it up, shaking off an ice slug first if need be - I usually go to the dial in the left corner this time, since I'm usually the closest to it. Once again run down the centre of the room towards Nighty, throwing off the machinegun fire along the way. By the time you reach him, the Ice Beam attack should be triggered and you can keep on running until you reach his side: when the core rays recede, jump onto his back.

The top core will be green this time - if you land next to it as early as possible, you get just enough time for a fully-charged PPKK + PP, with the 2nd P of the second combo being almost, but not quite, fully charged. However, dispense with that 2nd P and only do a DTed PPKK + P instead. Afterwards, de-DT and do a double taunt - immediately after the double taunt, jump off Nighty as the machinegun attack starts, jump to one side to throw off the tracking fire, then double back around the front of Nighty to his other side and parry his Triple Stab attack: a DTed PPKK + P will just be enough to finish him off!


Summary:

- Trigger Ice Beam, shatter the core with Inferno, do 1-2 double taunts.

- Light dial, parry triple stab, do a PPKK, adding a P afterwards if time.

- Visit to Underworld

- Light dial, trigger Ice Beam, do a PPKK + P on the by-now green green core. Afterwards, do one double taunt before jumping off Nighty�s front.

- Parry triple stab, then do a PPKK yet again - you can finish off Nighty with a PPKK + P, but only if you have done the maximum possible damage until now.

- [If Nightmare isn�t defeated yet] Trigger ice beam, do a PPKK



So, er, what if I want to leave the top core blue?

Then you get less time at the blue core, but not miles less. If you land next to the core as early as possible, then you can just squeeze in one fully-charged PPKK - but to be on the safe side, you should only partially charge the 2nd P or the 1st K. If you lost even a little bit of time in landing next to the core (by doing a flame-kick, say), then don't charge up one of the hits of the PPKK combo at all, otherwise the core will retract before you can get the final, and most damaging, K in.

It actually isn't any slower to defeat Nightmare 1 if you leave the top core blue, but it will make a difference in the Nightmare 2 and, especially, 3 fights. (Not that you can't defeat either of those two with a top blue core either, mind.) It's up to you. If you leave the top core blue, here's a summarized strategy. You can follow the detailed walkthrough above: extremely little changes.


Another summary

- Light dial, trigger ice beam, do one PPKK, then two double taunts. (You won't have to worry about any sprinkling of gunfire after the core retracts this time.)

- Light dial, parry triple stab, do 3 Meteors or one PPKK + P.

- Visit to Underworld

- Light dial, trigger ice beam, do one PPKK, then one double taunt before jumping off Nighty.

- Parry triple stab, then do either 3 Meteors or one PPKK + P

- [If Nightmare isn�t defeated yet] > Trigger ice beam; do one PPKK


Yet another (slightly different) summary

- Light up dial; trigger ice beam; shatter the blue core with Infernoes; get grabbed by Nightmare

- Visit to Underworld

- Light up dial; trigger ice beam; doing PPKK and dispensing with the first P of the 2nd PPKK ensures just enough time to double taunt twice

- Parry triple stab; do either 3 Meteors or one PPKK + P

- Trigger ice beam; build up enough DT for the next attack

- Parry triple stab � by now the slimes will be active around Nightmare, so take him out with Meteors from a distance


Okay, nice. Can I get a cup of tea now??

Not just yet; there's still Alastor to talk about :p

Oh

Yeah :p


Alastor

Fortunately, this section is a lot shorter... >_>

Although Alastor is almost as effective as Ifrit for fighting Nightmare, I'm not too inclined to recommend it because it probably won't be possible to stop the top core from turning red - if you don't mind that, then go ahead. The most damaging Alastor combo you can use on Nightmare is a simple grenade-Stinger one - simply alternate between Stingers and grenades as fast as possible. Believe it or not, this is about as damaging as PPKK. Unluckily, it will also shatter the cores really quickly. Using repeat DTed 4-Hit combos with HT finishers is nearly as powerful as the Stinger-grenade combo, but, again, it will shatter the cores quickly.

My Alastor walkthrough is basically the same as my Ifrit walkthrough, except I use Alastor on the cores instead of Ifrit. I've noticed time and time again that there's a slight quirk in Nightmare's AI if you use Alastor. If you turn the top core green at the beginning of the fight, then light up the dials for a second time and parry the Triple Stab attack, Nightmare will always do an Ice Beam attack before returning to gloop. It may not sound like much, but I wish he did it with Ifrit. (Actually he does, but only very rarely.) At any rate, if you use Alastor, I would abuse this slight quirk to take off over half of Nighty's life before going down to the Underworld.

I've also noticed that the AI is a bit iffy when it comes to Alastor shattering the cores - for example, there have been times in the past when Alastor hasn't shattered the core when it should've had done. But in my most recent testings, which were about 3 months ago and pretty rigorous, it shattered the core every single fuckin' time - green in the Nightmare 1 fight and red in the Nightmare 2 fight. I can't explain it, but all I can say is: if you want to use Alastor for all 3 Nightmare encounters, make a back-up save at Mission 16 in case you end up with two red cores by Nightmare 3 and can't SB the mission.

That's about all for Alastor. Just one final thing I may as well add: Nightmare seems strangely resistant to Sparda, so it's not a sword I recommend for this battle at all. It seems to do the damage of un-DTed Alastor rather than DTed Alastor.


When to go to the Underworld? Can it be skipped?

'Tis up to you, but I would say the best time to go is when about a third to half of Nighty's life has gone - it's a waste of time going down whenever Nighty's life is lower than 35%, however, since you can never actually kill Nighty with a return from the Underworld: you can only lower his life to a small portion of his red lifebar.

And yes, the Underworld can be skipped in the Nightmare 1 and 2 encounters, because you can orb-farm elsewhere to meet the red orb requirement: in Mission 20, this isn't possible, so there's no escaping a trip to netherland.

It will be much harder to avoid shattering the cores without the Underworld's 'free' damage and its resetting of the cores' hit counter. It's doable, though: just make sure to use mostly Meteor 2s on the top core. Or, heck, shatter it green: it's not like you can turn it red in the Nightmare 1 fight.


What happens if I mess up? Miss a Triple Stab or something?

Carry on. You can afford to mess up a bit. The time limit is quite strict, though, for all three Nightmare missions, so you can't afford to miss too many core-wailing opportunities.


Phew! *collapses*

There�s still more to come for Nightmare 2 & Nightmare 3, though ;p

Ending the mission

It�s not quite over yet, although you don�t have far to go. Collect the 500 red orbs, wait for the DT refill, then Stinger the hell out of there. Outside, on the bridge, you can take a Stinger pathway from the left side of the bridge if you like. However, it�s quicker and safer just to Vortex straight across. Make a beeline for where you use the Staff of Hermes, and put it in. Jump into the left painting, and you�re done! Incidentally, try to avoid the huge red orbs � they are better saved for later missions. You can�t avoid picking up the one where you use the Staff of Hermes, but you can avoid the remaining four.


Normal mode

Mostly as DMD mode. In the courtyard you will get three annoying Sin Scythes instead of a Death Scythe. You will still have to fight a Plasma in the main hall of the castle. In the corridor, the enemies will be Marionettes and Fetishes. There�s no Do or Die situation here, so you can just run straight past them, but I recommend farming a few of the Fetishes.

Nightmare is exactly the same as DMD Nightmare, except that this time he will take truly massive damage from your PPKK combos � it's possible to finish him off in just two turns at the core. However, you should still go down to the Underworld to collect your 500 red orbs from Shadow Phantom. If you want, you can use Air Raid against Phantom, since he is susceptible to it. Failing that, DTed Air-Hiked slashes on the usual weak points will cause great damage.

I should add that it doesn�t really matter if you shatter Normal/Hard mode Nightmare�s cores or not, as your attacks do so much more damage. Even if you arrive at Nightmare 3 with two red cores you�ll still be all right. However, that�s not a green light to resort to Air Raid and the like. Besides, learning how to use PPKK/Meteors against the cores now will stand you in very good stead for DMD mode ;p - after all, you�re bound to want to do a DMD SB game after this one... :p


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 17 =- -= Parted Memento =-
______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 7:00
Orbs: 600
Damage: 0%

Start the mission in a tiny room with smashable furniture � if you smash the furniture, jump around, and generally make a noise, you�ll wake up the Fetishes in the room above you. The furniture doesn�t yield any orbs, so it�s best to just run straight through the room and up the staircase tower � about halfway along, stop by the small room with two Fetishes who wake up as soon as you enter. Grenade them to restore your DT gauge � also, smash up the furniture which does yield a few orbs this time. Continue up the staircase to the room at the top. Hop-jump through the painting on the left, and Stinger across the courtyard � before examining the door, switch to Ifrit.


Lotsa Frosts

Well, all right, then � 4 Frosts :p ... The hardest bit of this battle is avoiding the cheap opening lunge. Cancel the cutscene; while the screen is still black, hold R1 + analogue stick backwards and furiously tap the Triangle button � depending on whether you did a hop-jump or not, you�ll either end up sandwiched between the two Frosts or in front of them. Wherever you end up, position yourself so that the Frost furthest away from the door is standing directly in front of you, then start charging up a Meteor 2 - if you landed in front of the Frosts and not in between them, you should probably wait a couple of seconds before starting to charge a Meteor. The Frost furthest away from the door will leap back to join its mate - if it does, your Meteor will kill both of them.

Once the first pair of Frosts have been killed, run towards the door and wait for the third Frost to land � as soon as it does so, charge up another Meteor and take that one out, too. You could take out the fourth Frost with a couple of Infernoes, but I recommend building back some DT by this point ;p ... Instead, wail upon the fourth Frost with non-DT attacks � Kick 13s, punches, and Magma Drives. By the time it�s dead, your DT gauge should be almost full again.


"Do or Die" � Fetishes/Nobodies

When you enter the corridor, a 1:00 "do or die" countdown will begin, with either Fetishes or Nobodies. Neither are very hard to deal with.

Fetishes � 5 Fetishes will be scattered throughout the corridor � don�t forget the 3rd one, which will be situated in the short passageway off the main corridor. Kill off the first three Fetishes ASAP with uncharged Meteors or Vortex, but don�t kill off the fourth one � not just yet. Instead, Kick-13 or Stinger it until it joins the fifth Fetish at the end of the corridor � then finish both off with an Inferno or Vortex. The reason for doing it this way is that, if you keep two Fetishes alive, no Fetishes will spawn. Whereas if you kill four out of five Fetishes, more Fetishes will start spawning at random points along the corridor � and you will then have to track those down and kill them off. If they DT, then you have a job on your hands � refill the DT gauge with the grenade gun and spam Vortex or fully-charged Meteor 2s until they die.

Nobodies � A large Nobody and a small Nobody will be in the corridor. Infernoes and the grenade gun so own Nobodies, it�s almost ridiculous. Just do 1-2 Infernoes against the two Nobodies and finish off with grenade-rolling. Keep at a distance from the exploding remains, naturally. Once the first two Nobodies have been defeated, two more will spawn, most likely at the other end of the corridor. If you�ve got some DT left over, and the Nobodies don�t drain it with their infernal dance, feel free to take out the subsequent two Nobodies with Infernoes and grenade-rolling. If you just want to push on, then I recommend switching to Alastor and using Air Hike to get past the large Nobodies in the quite cramped corridor.


Pluto�s dragon

A dragon? Where?? I thought this level was all about Nelo 3, one of DMD mode�s most feared bosses?!

Yes, but you have to get past the even more fearsome Pluto�s dragon first. Check the tablet to the left of the mirror: "The confronter of the path, Pluto�s dragon. Prove your bravery with the sword and the dragon will fall in its own flame."

Oh, that. The dinosaur skeleton. That�s easy. I just deflect the fireballs three times with Alastor.

Pluto�s dragon. It�s a dragon, dude. Get the name right.

It�s a freaking dinosaur skeleton! It looks exactly like a Tyrannosaurus rex!

Dragon, dude, dragon =_=

Whatever. This game is dumb.

And, er, yeah :p

After deflecting three fireballs in a row, go and swivel the rotating mirror around far enough so that its light shines onto another mirror and releases the Quicksilver. Not only is this quite possibly the dullest task in DMC1, you can�t cancel the ensuing cutscene either. Once it�s over, go to where the dragon/dinosaur skeleton used to stand, and Air Hike up to the alcove where the Quicksilver is kept. Pick up the Quicksilver � if you jump onto the nearest vanishing platform, you can Stinger off it quite some way.

Back out in the corridor, you will get Fetishes or Nobodies � again, you should use Air Hike to get past the large Nobodies. If you need DT, then grenade the enemies in the corridor. Meeting the red orb requirement shouldn�t be a problem for this mission, but some extra red orbs are to be found by slashing the table outside the Pluto Dragon area and the suits of armour in the corridor. Use the Quicksilver on the ornate red door.


Boss battle � NELO ANGELO 3

Say hello to Vergil :p


Nelo 3�s moves

As Nelo 1 and Nelo 2, except:


Phantom Swords

Nelo 3 has been toughened up in many ways, but the single biggest change is his new ability to send various formations of light blue swords flying your way, all of which are possessed by one intent only: to turn Dante into a kebab faster than you can say "no gherkins on that, please." But never fear, noble SBer. Even though Nelo has many Phantom Sword formations � too many - each and every one of them can be dealt with. Firstly, the formations...


Halo formations

A loose 'halo', or circle, of spinning swords appears above Dante�s head, and contracts into a much tighter circle. Wherever Dante goes, the Halo formation also goes. All of the Halo formations begin this way. They don�t, however, all end the same way.

Rapidfire (1) � Once the halo has contracted, all the swords come down in rapidfire succession, tracking Dante�s last position. To evade this formation, simply remain running around and don�t double back on yourself. This is one of the commonest Phantom Sword formations.

Bunched-up (2) � Once the halo has contracted, it quickly rises up out of sight above Dante�s head,then, about 1 second later, the bunched-up swords are fired down towards the ground in rapidfire fashion. To evade this formation, again, remain running around without doubling back on yourself. Whenever you see the halo rise out of sight, always keep running for at least 2-3 seconds afterwards. Also, never try to jump away � that leads to at least a 50% chance of you getting hit.

Open Circle (3) � Once the halo has contracted, it quickly rises out of sight above Dante�s head, then, about 5 seconds later, the swords are fired down towards the ground in rapidfire fashion � but this time they come down vertically in the shape of a large circle. When the halo first rises into the air, you should keep running for long enough to ensure that it isn�t the bunched-up formation which is going to come flying down. Once you have established that it isn�t, then you should stand still to let the circle of swords come down around you. While you�re waiting for the swords to come down, you can bring Nelo down off his perch with a partially-charged Meteor or Roundtrip.

Flower (4) � Nice, romantic name, yeah :p ... Once the halo has contracted, the swords flip over 90 degrees so that they are all lying on a horizontal plane, with Dante stuck in the centre of this deadly 'flower'. They remain stationary for about a second before flying outwards. To evade, simply jump sideways out of the 'flower' while the swords are stationary. This is one of the rarer formations.


Other formations

You should definitely get used to the halo formations, as you�ll be seeing a lot of them. However, don�t think you�re out of the woods just yet ;p ... For all of the below formations, you should stand inside the large circle in the middle of the room. (Whereas the Halo formations, by comparison, can be dealt with from almost anywhere.) This way you'll have a decent view of both Nelo and his swords. Note that all Phantom Swords formations can 'home' in on Dante by swivelling to point at him wherever he goes.


Horizontal Row � A horizontal row of swords line up in front of Nelo, then are rapidly fired from left to right. Just jump to the left at the same time as they are fired � you can run one or two steps to the left first if you like; it�s not essential to, but it may help with timing the jump. I wouldn�t recommend trying to roll your way past this attack � it can sometimes work, but the i-frames have a nasty habit of wearing off before all the swords have been fired...
