Vertical Row � A vertical row of swords line up in front of Nelo, then are rapidly fired from top to bottom. Just do a jump to the left (or right) at the same time as they are fired � the vertical row is easier to evade than the horizontal row, but also appears less frequently in my experience.

Cross-shaped swords � A very loosely "cross" shape of swords appears in front of Nelo. The ones in the centre are fired off first, followed by the swords on the edges. This is one of Nelo's more dangerous formations. It's best to evade it with a jump to the left (or right) as soon as they are fired � you can either jump from the spot, or run 1-2 steps first before the swords are actually fired. I don�t recommend trying to roll your way through this attack, as again the i-frames have a habit of wearing off too early.

Spinning Plate � A spinning 'plate', which resembles the rim of a saucer, appears above Dante�s head. The swords spin round above Dante�s head for about 2 seconds, stop, and are fired diagonally downwards about a second later. Just do a jump to the side or straight up either a second before the swords stop spinning or after they have stopped � they will miss you every time.

Blanket of swords � A 'blanket' of swords appears above Dante�s head, almost smothering him with swords. The swords appear at different angles, but all are pointing in one direction only: that�s right :p ... And everywhere Dante goes, the swords follow, jerkily shifting their positions with it. About two seconds after first appearing, some of them will begin to fire towards Dante, with the rest following soon afterwards. Sometimes this attack can linger - instead of all the swords being fired at you, one or two will remain behind, following you everywhere like a nervous tic. Should Nelo come down while a stray Phantom Sword is still on the loose, you�ll just have to evade both him and the sword as best you can. At any rate, the blanket of swords must be counted as the most dangerous of all formations. I�ve found that the most consistently reliable way to evade it is to jump around in a triangle shape � as soon as they appear, just start to jump around in a rough triangle shape and don�t stop until all the swords have gone. That should get you past the swords unscathed. Alternatively, you can do an Inferno to get rid of the swords in one blow.

Blue Meteor [red lifebar only] � When Nelo�s lifebar gets a certain way into the red, he gains the ability to charge blue meteors from his perch. Luckily the blue meteors are slowly charged up and are easy to evade with a roll, jump, or even dash to the side. Their main danger by far is when you have to evade a blue meteor + Phantom Sword formation simultaneously, which can occasionally cause problems. If you get the Open Circle formation, for example, then standing still is contradictory to jumping/rolling to the side. But never fear ;p ... If you get such a combination, then simply jump straight up as Nelo fires the blue meteor to evade. Also, it should be noted that the blue meteors Nelo fires from his perch have no audio cue � so be careful if the camera changes focus from Nelo to Dante! (As it occasionally has an annoying habit of doing.)


When Nelo drops down

Nelo can drop down from his perch in one of three ways: unadorned or wearing a sword 'skirt'. There are two sword skirts that our in-touch-with-his-feminine-side swordsman is fond of wearing.

Vertically straight � The swords are vertical and surround Nelo in a circle around his waist. They are too close to Nelo to be able to do any harm to Dante � their function is to protect Nelo. To damage him, you will have to destroy the swords or wait until they evaporate of their own accord. Grenade gun shots will destroy a few; to get rid of all of them in one swoop, bait Nelo into doing a combo, then Inferno him. However, as it doesn�t take very long for the swords to evaporate after Nelo has dropped down from his perch, I would wait it out, unless you are highly impatient.

Tutu of Death � Sadly not my description, but accurate :p ... The swords are horizontal and radiate outwards from Nelo�s waist - and they will damage Dante if you get too close to Nelo. Even worse, they don�t just evaporate after a while, but instead are fired outwards - even if you aren�t doing anything, the swords have a greater chance of missing you than hitting you. Still, since you will curse if you are hit, it�s better to take evasive measures. Try to time a wall-jump or Air Hike as the swords are fired outwards. Alternatively, being on the stairs will elevate you above the swords� level.


Evading Nelo�s dash

Once Nelo drops down from his perch, he will begin a long sliding dash towards Dante. He can finish the dash with any attack in his range, including a teleport and another round of Phantom Sword attacks! There�s not much you can do if the latter happens, except evade all over again. Mostly, though, he will finish off with a sweeping horizontal slash or a Stinger. As long as Nelo is unadorned or wearing the vertical sword skirt, then there shouldn�t be much of a problem � just jump away as he draws close, or, for extra safety, do a wall-jump or Air Hike. If, however, Nelo is wearing the Tutu of Death, then doing a wall-jump becomes essential! Luckily, timing a wall-jump over his head as he approaches isn't actually that hard.


Stopping the Phantom Swords

If you are alert to the situation, you can bring down Nelo as soon as he flies off to his perch with a slightly charged Meteor or Roundtrip. (However, Meteor is faster � you need to be very quick if you are going to bring him down with Roundtrip before he begins a Phantom Sword attack.) You can use Vortex to bring Nelo down and destroy any current Phantom Sword formation at the same time � but be careful about where you drop back down! If you miss the initial opportunity to bring Nelo down from his perch, then you can try to squeeze in a barely charged Meteor in between formations � if you do it immediately after evading a formation, then you stand a good chance of hitting Nelo before he has time to summon the next formation. If, however, he gets enough time to summon a new formation, then all your Meteor will do is destroy a few Phantom Swords, and you�ll have to evade the remainder. Finally, you can bring Nelo down just by running up to whichever perch he is at � once you pass a certain trigger point, Nelo will drop back down � just be ready to jump away immediately :p ... This tactic can be extremely dangerous should Nelo choose to do a vertical, horizontal or cross formation as you are running up to him � instead, you should wait until Nelo sends a halo formation, which will give you enough time to run up to his perch and bring him down. Just bear in mind you will need to evade both the descending Nelo and the sword halo.


Battle

Well, now you know everything there is to know about Phantom Swords, on with the battle :p

Differences/similarities between Nelo 3 and Nelo 1&2 at a glance:

- Nelo 3 can do Phantom Swords (dur)
- He is much harder to stun with a parry
- He no longer holds his sword up for a second prior to doing the 4-Hit combo
- He begins battle with a taunt, but much further away from you than before
- Like Nelo 2, he blocks after 9-10 slashes
- He's about a foot taller and takes off his helmet - y hello thar, long lost brother
- 'S about it, apart from a different arena, of course

The most obvious change for the Nelo 3 battle is the appearance of Phantom Swords. As long as you keep enough DT to fire off a Meteor or Vortex into Nelo, then you can pretty much cut short the Phantom Swords every single time. How often Nelo does them depends on how attacking you are - the more you melee him without let up, the less he will teleport away to do his Phantom Swords; if you use sword-cancelling combined with his Meteor glitch and don't mess it up, then he will never break off to summon Phantom Swords. The time limit in Mission 17 is reasonable enough that you don't absolutely have to finish Nelo 3 off as fast as possible; nevertheless, it's better to not let him have too much free reign in this battle either.

Nelo 3 is much harder to stun than Nelo 1 and 2. It's still possible, but typically it will now take several deflections with Rolling Blaze or the sword to make him slump over, winded, whereas before it would usually only take 1-2 such deflections. Oddly enough, though, certain loops which depend on stunning him still work fine in this encounter: the Magma Drive loop and the PPKK loop. The MD loop is harder to set up than before; if it fails, then try again - eventually the MD will parry Nelo 3 for a stun and when it does, you can often keep this loop going for quite a while.

Nelo 3 has also lost the opening stance to his 4-Hit combo: standing in front of him passively waiting for an easy parry is just suicidal these days.

At the beginning of battle, Nelo 3 opens with a taunt just like in his previous two fights, but he stands further away from you this time - by the time you reach him to melee his front, he will have recovered. So instead stay where you are and pelt him with Meteor 2s - you have enough time to charge up two of them. (Very occasionally, he may veer to evade the second Meteor as he�s sliding towards you, but his attack will miss you if he does.) You can then continue doing partly-charged Meteors until DT runs out. On Normal/Hard this tactic can take off half of Nelo 3's life and feels as cheap as hell - even on DMD mode, it feels cheap, but at least the damage dealt isn't as staggering.

And... that's almost it for Nelo 3. All the strategies outlined in the Nelo 1 battle will work for Nelo 3, too. Just pick your favourite DA and go to town with it. Try to confine battle to the centre of the arena or to the area with the harlequin-patterned floor. Nelo will sometimes teleport onto the stairs to fire blue meteors at you, but never follow him onto the stairs or try to fight him there - that's just asking for a dicing. Just go up reasonably close to the stairs instead and trade projectiles with him (without you getting hit obviously) until he leaves the stairs. And if Nelo disappears off-screen, then be ultra-careful of his sliding dash, which is lethal in this fight.

Oh, go on then - here's one final technique, just for you :p


Intensive parrying technique

This is a sword strategy only - it can be done with all 3 swords, but is easiest with the Sparda due to its longest reach. It's an extremely simple button-mashing technique, which is a bit harder to execute than it sounds. Wail on Nelo with the sword until he blocks, then continue to wail on the blocking Nelo non-stop: your slashes will bounce off his sword, but that's okay. Just continue button-mashing. When Nelo unblocks, one of two things will happen: your sword will clash with Nelo's or Nelo will go into another attack, most commonly the overhead slash one. If your swords clash, then continue smashing the attack button until you force a parry which makes Nelo slump over. If Nelo goes into another attack, then interrupt it or evade - the overhead slash is by far the easiest one to interrupt, but the sweeping horizontal slash or punches can be interrupted with a very fast hop-jumped downward slash.

This isn't really a viable SB strategy due to the length of time it takes (most of your hits aren't damaging Nelo at all) and the fact it's disgustingly horrible to do damage-free. But it's fun to mess around with.

Once Nelo is defeated, the mission ends.


Normal mode

In the small room halfway up the stairway tower, you get a light blue Mari instead of two Fetishes. If you require DT, double taunt it, and be on your way again. In the courtyard, evade the cheap opening lunge of the Frost as outlined in the DMD section. Normal/Hard mode Frosts can�t be killed with a single Meteor 2, so if you line up the two Frosts for a Meteor, you�ll have to follow up with an Inferno, or other attacks, to kill them both. On Normal mode, you�ll only get two Frosts, anyway � the remaining two won�t spawn.

In the corridor, you�ll get Marionettes and Fetishes, which you can just run straight past. The dino-dragon still requires 3 deflections to defeat. Back out in the corridor, you�ll get more Marionettes and Fetishes. Just fight Nelo 3 as per DMD Nelo 3 and he�ll go down ridiculously quickly :p

Meeting the red orb requirement in this mission can be a bit tricky, compared to DMD mode. At the very beginning of the mission, you might want to go back through the portal and pick up 1 or 2 huge red orbs, worth 100 red orbs each. (Though you should save two of them for Mission 19.) Make sure to smash every piece of furniture or armour which yields red orbs. Kill a few Fetishes. You should now be okay on the orb front.


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 18 =- -= Spirit Stone, "Elixir" =-
_______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 6:00
Orbs: 900
Damage: 0%

A portal has opened up in the area where Dante defeated Nelo. If it�s a fresh game, you�ll have to go through all the palaver of brandishing your brand new sword, Sparda. It will also be automatically equipped, so now would be a good time to de-equip it if you want to build up DT for the boss fight at the end of this mission.

Anyway, go through the portal. Jump through the right hand painting this time round. (Although the left hand painting has now disappeared, so you couldn�t jump through it, even if you wanted to.) You will end up in a small labyrinth of water tunnels. The fastest way through them is to swivel left, swim through the hole in the wall, turn right, swim to the broken bars. Then turn left, swim to the end of the passageway, and turn right � that�s it. You�re now out of the water tunnels. If you want to collect some red orbs and a blue/yellow orb, there�s two extra rooms that you can swim into to get them. You�re not exactly going miles out of your way if you stop to pick these goodies up, so by all means go for it.

In the next area, which is still underwater, you will face three Sin Scissors. How to tackle them depends on what the Sin closest to you does. If it moves anticlockwise behind you, then don�t try turning around to face it � instead, swim over to the Sin on the right and kill it. After that, use O to rotate anticlockwise and kill the first Sin, then rotate clockwise and kill the third Sin who hopefully, by this time, has swam down towards you. Alternatively, if the Sin closest to you doesn�t move behind you but moves further away from you instead, kill that Sin first, then the one on the right, and finally the third one in the distance.

It seems like you can make the first Sin move further away from you every single time, simply by making sure Dante is as close to the left wall of the passageway as he can be when entering the room with the Sins. (Thanks, Sparlastor, for mentioning something I would probably have never known about otherwise.)

You need to kill the Sins very quickly indeed, if none of them are to DT. It�s not a great idea if they DT, since they gain about 4x more health. You can kill a Sin with one round of bullets, just about. (Try mashing on the shoot button � you�ll see that bullets don�t fire constantly, but in rounds. Jam on the shoot button to fire off a single round as fast as possible, but then pause to let the gun reload itself.) To kill a Sin with one round, you should be quite close to it. At any rate, aim to kill all the Sins before they can DT, but if the last one does DT, then just plug away until it dies.

Swim through the newly-opened exit and enter a staircase tower very similar to the one first encountered in Mission 4. Light up the dial, quickly swim up to the top, drop onto the highest balcony, and pick up the Philosopher�s Egg. You can turn around and do a stylish hop onto the balcony below. To do a stylish hop over a railing, simply stand in front of it and press triangle � hey presto. Dante will grab hold of the railing with one hand and stylishly leap over it. So there.

Exit the staircase tower via the green-red door. You can either trigger the fight with Nightmare 2 straight away, or you can take a brief detour to pick up 103 red orbs and a blue/yellow orb first. To take the brief detour, Stinger or Air Hike over to the balcony with the double doors.


Boss Battle � NIGHTMARE 2

Welcome to part two.


Nightmare 2�s moves:

As Nightmare 1, except:

Boomerang - Nightmare is now capable of detaching one of its body parts from its side and sending it spinning around the arena. A metal �arm� will detach from either its left or right side (more commonly the left in my experience), rise up into the air, unfold, and then start spinning like a propeller. As it does this, the core will emerge from Nightmare�s back, again with the initial protective white rays radiating from it. The boomerang will then begin to spin around the arena at a rapid rate of knots � it has two patterns to its spinning: a close-range figure-of-8 pattern and a longer-range circular pattern. Although I�m not certain, which pattern it does is probably dependent on which side it emerged from Nightmare. The boomerang, like the tentacles from the Triple Stab attack, is parriable. The easiest time to parry it is when it first emerges from Nightmare � run to stand underneath it, wait until it starts spinning, and then jump straight up to hit it with Rolling Blaze � this will make the boomerang fold up and retract. You can also use a rising High Time to parry it. Remember not to jump too soon � if you make contact with the boomerang before it starts to spin, you will only succeed in hurting yourself. If you miss this initial deflection opportunity, then you can deflect the boomerang as it�s spinning around the arena with Rolling Blaze or a sword slash, although it's much riskier that way. As long as you correctly anticipate this attack in advance, then it becomes quite easy to deflect the boomerang as soon as it emerges � simply jump to and fro in front of Nightmare until the machinegun fire ends, and then run down whichever side you spot the boomerang emerging from � careful of the core�s radiating white rays as you run down the side. Once the boomerang has been parried, you get an attacking opportunity at Nightmare�s top core.


Underworld

As per Nightmare 1, except for a new Shadow boss.

Shadow Griffon

This is the hardest out of the three Shadow bosses, in my opinion. Griffon only has two lightning attacks, but the bolts seem to come out faster than usual, and, in the knobbly, gnarled, dim landscape of the Underworld, can be a real pain to evade.

Shadow Griffon�s moves

Horizontal bolts � Griffon will send out an initial V-bolt, then follow it with several horizontal bolts, which track Dante�s height. Hop over the side of the V-bolt, and then either jump or roll to evade the horizontal bolts. If you remain on the same spot, you can jump straight up over the low bolts as they approach and stand still to let the high bolts pass over your head as they approach. You can also roll through the low bolts, but be careful not too roll too soon, as the i-frames will wear off and you will get hurt.

V-bolts � Griffon will send out an initial horizontal bolt, then follow it with several V-bolts, which converge together. You can either hop over the sides, or roll through them as the two sides of the �V� converge together.

Dash and Peck � Yes, Shadow Griffon still has his futile peck attack, whereby he dashes towards you and swings his head to take a peck. Easy as ever to evade by jumping away.

Battle

If you followed my map when defeating the Sargassoes, then you should see Griffon storming across the battlefield towards you after defeating the last Sargasso � you will have enough time to fully charge up a Meteor 2 to greet him, so feel free to do so. Afterwards, jump away to evade Griffon�s peck, then try to put some distance between yourself and him, and charge up another Meteor if you get time � otherwise, evade the inevitable lightning attack that Griffon will send out. Immediately after the lightning attack has ended, feel free once again to a) charge up a Meteor and b) evade Griffon�s peck after he has dashed towards you. It�s seemingly impossible to get the double hit with Meteor in this battle, but on the other hand, Shadow Griffon will go down to about 5 Meteor 2s, plus a generous sprinkling of grenades or E&I.;

If you like, you can battle Shadow Griffon close-range for a knockdown strategy. The usual 3-4 Infernoes + flame kicks/Rolling Blazes will knock Griffon over. Fortunately, Shadow Griffon follows Griffon 1�s - rather than Griffon 3�s - example when it comes to being knocked down: he will lie there for a decent amount of time and has no invulnerability period: so go for it!


Nightmare 2�s attack pattern

In case you skipped the relevant part in the Nightmare 1 notes ;p

[Beginning of battle / After return from Underworld] > Ice beam/Projectiles > Triple Stab > Ice Beam/Projectiles > Triple Stab > Boomerang > Triple Stab -> [Rinse and repeat]

So, in short, there is 1 Boomerang for every 2 Ice Beams. Incidentally, the Boomerang attack can�t be substituted � going round to the back of Nighty won�t trigger the Projectiles attack.

Again, the pattern can change later on in the battle due to unknown factors, but it should last for as long as you need it to.


Battle

Nightmare 2 is almost the same as Nightmare 1 - the only differences are a new attack and a new Shadow Boss. Oh, and his cores can be turned red in this fight too. (Check the Core Mechanics section in the Nightmare 1 guide if you don't know what I'm referring to.)

The battlefield is a bit smaller this time around and only has 2 dials instead of 4. There are also balconies you can jump up to - but if you think you can attack Nighty in safety from higher up, you've got another think coming, as his gloop can grab you even on the balconies.

The camera angles are also truly horrible - unless you know how to manipulate them to your advantage. This is easily done by only going down that side of Nighty which is facing the Philosopher's Egg furnace. Not only will you avoid the ultra-jarring camera angle changes, but you will get a very good, almost top down, view of Nightmare.

Before setting your Philosopher�s Egg on the fire to cook, you should light up the left dial first of all to ensure a solidified Nightmare after the brief (unskippable) cutscene has ended. Stand in front of Nightmare as he solidifies in order to trigger the ice beam attack - there's just enough time to double taunt twice if needed. Once the machine gun fire starts, do a hop-jump to the right (from Dante's POV), then double back on yourself around to Nightmare's other side (the one which is facing the furnace and the crumbling stairs). Wait until the core's rays have receded, then jump onto Nighty's back - for a teensy bit of extra damage you can fire a DTed grenade at the core whilst waiting for the rays to recede if you like.

If the core is green, do a charged DTed PPKK + PP (don't charge that 2nd P of the 2nd PPKK if you were even a tiny bit late getting up next to the core) to take up to a quarter off Nighty's life; if the core is still blue, now would be a really good time to consider shattering it with a couple of Infernoes followed by melee attacks. Double taunt, preferably twice, as Nightmare sinks back into gloop. (In my experience, the tiny sprinkling of gunfire which Nightmare sometimes does at this point in the first fight doesn't happen here.)

Before Nightmare's gloop can grab you, jump away and light up the left dial again. Again, do a hop-jump to Dante's right when the machine gun fire starts, then run around to Nightmare's other side, the one which is facing the furnace - keep running, and you should be able to parry the triple stab attack before a camera angle change can occur. Do a PPKK + P combo or, if DT is no object, 3 charged Meteor 2s on the rear core - whichever floats your boat.

At this point I go down to the Underworld - if you decide to light up the dial one more time, you'll get an Ice Beam again.

After the Underworld, Nightmare's attack pattern will reset and he will, once again, begin with an Ice Beam. As long as you go down to the Underworld, you should easily be able to polish off Nightmare 2 without even seeing his Boomerang attack.

All being well, it's possible to finish off DMD Nightmare 2 with a green top core in 3 turns at the core + 1 visit to the Underworld. For this to be possible, you do have to absolutely maximise your damage output at every turn at the core, however - otherwise expect to need at least 4 turns at the core.


I don�t have to go to the Underworld, do I?

No. But you will have to do some orb-farming to meet the 900 red orbs requirement. I recommend going back out into the corridor at the beginning of the mission: you can fight Fetishes or Nobodies in the hallway or you can go out into the courtyard and take out the two Frosts there with a Meteor 2 each. (They�re too far apart to line up.) The two Frosts alone should guarantee your red orb count, provided you collect all the available red orbs throughout Mission 18.

If you want to skip the Underworld in the Nightmare 2 fight, then I recommend leaving Nightmare 1's cores blue. Otherwise... I'm not sure you will be able to avoid turning the top core red if it's green to start off with. Hmm. Use nothing but Meteor 2s on the top core - and attack the rear core whenever you can. That should keep the top core green throughout.


Once Nightmare 2 is defeated

Grab the orbs and run over to where the Philosopher�s Egg is � it takes a little bit of time for it to finish cooking, but once it has, you�ll have a nice meal of eggs upon your hands :p ... Afterwards, the mission will end.


Normal mode

This is pretty much as DMD mode. The Sin Scissors are easier, due to the fact you don�t have to worry about them DTing. Use DMD strategies on Nightmare to watch him go down in no time at all.


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 19 =- -= Enter The Corrupted World =-
_______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 3:30
Orbs: 500
Damage: 0%

After a run of rather tough bosses � Griffon 3, Nelo 3, and Nightmares 1 & 2 � this easy mission should come as a welcome break. Chin up � you�ve only got Nightmare 3 next :p

At the start of the mission, run to the portal and end up back in the small room with the two paintings � except they will have disappeared. Run down the stairs to the bottom of the tower and exit via another portal. Make your way to the Castellan�s bedroom, examine the mirror, then walk through it into the inverted mirror world. If you have not yet picked up the Nightmare Beta, then it will be glinting brightly on the bed in the mirror bedroom. Exit into the courtyard, jump down, and pick up the Philosopher�s Stone.

Cancel the ensuing cutscene. Three Nobodies will be there. Inferno one. The Inferno won�t even have time to come out before another cutscene is triggered. Cancel that one, too, then take out the large Nobody with 1-2 Infernoes and grenade-roll the remaining two small Nobodies to death. You could finish off all 3 Nobodies with Inferno, but that would drain all your DT and that�s not such a bright idea. Avoid the spattering remains, collect the orbs, then get the hell out of there.

Back at the pier, collect all the leftover huge red orbs � there should be 2-3 left depending on what you did in earlier missions. Now you�ll need to cross the broken bridge. You can Vortex across, but I prefer to conserve DT and use a Stinger pathway. Stand at the right hand side of the bridge (left from Dante�s viewpoint; right from yours) and do two Stingers, then an Air Hike onto the other side. An alternative pathway is to Stinger once, jump onto the platform to the left, do a Stinger off that, then hop onto the other side.

Run up to the cathedral, trigger the appearance of the funky purple puddle, sink under, use the Philosopher�s Stone, end mission.


Normal mode

Exactly as DMD mode.


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 20 =- -= Showdown With Nightmare =-
_______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 4:30
Orbs: 700
Damage: 0%

It would be lovely just to be able to run straight past the Frosts, but unfortunately you need their orbs to meet the red orb requirement of 700. (Hopefully you can figure out what�s coming next: that�s right � a compulsory visit to the Underworld :p)

There are various ways of taking out the two Frosts � one way is to break the membrane, then immediately charge up a Meteor 2 to take out the Frost directly facing you. Be careful when running to collect its orbs, as sometimes the Frost by the time statue will send out a shard of ice � you can hear it travelling underground before it appears, though, so you get just enough time to jump away. Afterwards, flame-kick towards the second Frost, and take it out with melee attacks.

Alternatively, you can run towards the Frost which is directly facing you, then do an Inferno > Auto-Combo � you need to be very quick here to avoid the other Frost sending out an icy shard. If you DT-cancel the Auto-Combo, you�ll get most of your DT back, which means you can use the same combo against the second Frost � again, DT-cancel the Auto-Combo to get most of your DT back.


Boss Battle - NIGHTMARE 3

DMD Nightmare 3 � if there was a boss that I would nominate for being asked for help with the most times on the GamesFAQ DMC1 board, I would say this is it. In reality, though, he is just a cute, cuddly killing machine :p

But honestly, aside from his final core, he isn�t that bad. He has the same health as Nightmare 1 and 2, provided you�ve kept his cores blue or green. It�s really only the final core which is a true bitch � you�re likely to lose quite a few SBs to Nighty 3�s final core.


Nightmare 3's moves

As per Nightmare 1 & Nightmare 2, except:

[Gloopy Nightmare only]:

Ceiling attack � If you step on Nightmare�s gloop at certain times, it will trigger a response whereby the entire bulk instantly flies up to the ceiling and plasters itself there. If Dante remains on the edges of the arena, a total of up to 8 homing missiles will be fired one at a time. If, however, he runs towards the centre, a wire mesh cage will drop down, trapping him � if a missile has already been fired, it will follow Dante into the cage, but no more will be fired after that. Dante can�t be badly hurt inside the cage (at least not until Nightmare drops down), but nevertheless there still seem to be mysterious spatters, or whatever, which take off small pieces of his lifebar. To escape, aim the analogue stick at one of the corners of the cage and attack it with Rolling Blaze, PPKK (no need to charge the hits) or regular sword slashes. It�s imperative to escape as quickly as possible, because if you are still inside the cage when Nightmare drops down, you can kiss goodbye to a good one-and-a-half of your lifebars � minimum. Once Nightmare has dropped back down to the ground, do not step into the gloop for at least 3 seconds � if you step into it too soon after Nightmare has dropped back down, you will lose at least one-and-a-half lifebars and get trapped in an ice crystal. This entire fly-up-to-the-ceiling palaver is a massive time-waster, and you should strenuously avoid triggering it in the first place by running to light up the dials without putting so much as a toe into Nightmare�s gloop. If you do trigger it, then just keep on running around the edge of the arena � the missiles will follow behind you, but won�t hit you. Once Nightmare has dropped back down to the ground, stay away from the gloop for a few seconds. It doesn�t take long for Nightmare to recover to normal, and once he does so, you can safely step on the gloop again. After the ceiling attack, Nightmare�s gloop will revert to trying to grab you.

Trish�s yellow bolts � At a predetermined point in the Nightmare battle, a cutscene with Trish will be triggered. Immediately after the cutscene is over (or has been cancelled), get ready to jump away to dodge Trish�s stungun. Trish will continue to fire her stungun at regular intervals afterwards, including during the final core stage. Fortunately, her �contribution� is rather pathetic. You get a clear-cut indication of whenever she is going to fire by the yellow traces of lightning which appear for about a second beforehand. The bolts cause a tiny amount of damage on their own � their only real danger lies in the fact that they stun Dante briefly, leaving him at risk of getting caught up in one of Nightmare�s major attacks. However, not only are they very easy to avoid, Trish conveniently won�t fire them while Nightmare has his core exposed � so you are free to wail away without fear of attack.


Underworld

As per Nightmare 1 and 2, except for a new Shadow boss.

Shadow Nelo's moves

As per Nelo 1 and 2 � this version of Nelo has no Phantom Swords.

Battle

This is the only Underworld where the Shadow Boss isn�t visible before it becomes activated � instead, Shadow Nelo will drop down out of the sky. Once you learn where Shadow Nelo drops down, you can begin attacking him immediately. Even if he begins an attack, your DTed attacks will overwhelm him.

Sword-Cancelling + exploitation of Nelo�s meteor glitch is the fastest way to dispose of him. Shadow Nelo follows Nelo 1�s example when it comes to blocking, so you can get in up to 16 SCed slashes before needing to jump away to trigger a meteor. If you need your memory refreshed, then read the Alastor section of the Nelo 1 battle.

If you�d rather use Ifrit, then any of the Ifrit tactics discussed for the previous Nelo battles will work here. Speed is the order of the day here, so just make you sure kill Nelo fast, whichever method you use.

If you�re not playing a fresh game and have the Bangle of Time, then there is a way of getting past Shadow Nelo, which will work even on DMD mode. Simply wait until Nelo is vulnerable (e.g. taunting or slumped over) and then freeze time � use all 10 DT runes to wail upon Nelo�s front with a repeat Kick-13 > grenade combo. Afterwards, Nelo should be dead or damn close to dead. However, this isn�t a particularly speedy method (you�ll have to waste time equipping and de-equipping the BoT), and I simply refuse to believe you can make it past DMD Nelo 3 unscathed, but not DMD Shadow Nelo.

Anyway, once the battle is over, collect the 500 red orbs and hitch a ride out of the Underworld.


Final Core

Intro

This is where, once Nightmare�s first lifebar has been emptied, instead of doing the decent thing and just dying, Nightmare bounces back with 25% of his previous lifebar and throws everything but the kitchen sink at you in a last-ditch desperate attempt to stop you. As a tactic it�s not terribly subtle, but it certainly has its points � as testified to by the number of players who have torn their hair out over this section of the battle.

Despite the seeming chaoticness of the final core, its attacks are actually highly patterned. The transition from regular Nightmare to final core Nightmare is seamless � once regular Nightmare�s lifebar is emptied, the arena floods with light and the final core begins its onslaught of attacks almost instantaneously.


The Final Core�s patterns

To begin with, the core will send out white lasers one after the after, which start off by aiming diagonally downwards at the ground, then travel upwards to the ceiling. After the 7th laser, a series of lasers � resembling the spokes of a wheel radiating from a hub � will appear and simultaneously rise upwards. The lasers will then disappear for a while as the core enters a DT-siphoning stage � however, other attacks will still continue, such as the ice-slugs and Trish�s yellow bolts. Once the DT-siphoning ends, a second round of lasers will start up, along with missiles for the first time � although the missiles will only appear if some damage has been done to the core already. After the third round of lasers and the second DT-siphoning stage, the pattern changes � the lasers will plink-plonk their way twice around the core as though doing a mexican wave. After that, however, the lasers will revert back to the previous pattern for another two rounds, before the �mexican wave� appears again.

Phew. While all this is going on, there are other dangers present � Nightmare�s gloop will swirl up to damage you should you get too close to it, ice slugs are sent out to follow you on a regular basis, and, meanwhile, Trish is still trying to zap you with her useless gun. (How come it�s so good against Mundus 3? >_>)


Preparation

Feh. I used to carefully chip Nighty 3�s lifebar down to the tiniest sliver and then build up 10 DT runes via taunting the core and grenading Nighty in his gloopy form. However, such an elaborate preparation is unnecessary, as well as time-consuming. If you don�t have full DT when you trigger the final core, it doesn�t matter too much � just build up some more DT during the first round of lasers. Alternatively, if Nighty 3�s red lifebar is low enough upon your return from the Underworld, then you will have 10 DT runes with which you can embark upon an onslaught on the final core.


Evasion

When the final core is triggered, there will be about one second of peace. Make use of this time to jump away from Nightmare and run to a blue wall ASAP. By the time you�ve reached the blue wall, the lasers will have started - you can (grenade) roll through them, jump away from them, or even run/walk ahead of them � if you hold R1 and keep walking, the lasers will follow behind you, but won�t hit you. (Of course, there is no guarantee that an ice slug or two won�t try to molest you along the way.) Once the lasers have finished, there will be a temporary respite, but you will still have ice slugs, Nightmare's gloop and Trish's stun gun to contend with.

Homing missiles will accompany the second round of lasers, unless you somehow managed to avoid damaging the core. This makes evasion something of a genuine nightmare now, what with lasers, missiles, ice slugs, Trish�s bolts and Nightmare�s gloop. If you have E&I; equipped, then you can still walk through the barrage � just keep firing constantly to deflect the homing missiles which will come towards you. If you have the grenade gun equipped, you can still grenade-roll in between lasers � your grenades will shoot back the missiles. But by god is it hair-raising and dangerous stuff. It is probably better just to jump away from the lasers and missiles, and wait until the next DT-siphoning phase arrives.

If waiting for the DT-siphoning phase is too wussy for you, then be careful not to accidentally roll too close to a dial (auto-aim hates you) or Nightmare�s gloop. When walking or rolling, you can safely double back on yourself in between lasers � you should remain in front of one of the blue energy walls, as making your way past a dial is rather dangerous � Nightmare�s gloop tends to grab you near the dials, for a start.


Attaaaack...

Bah, don't tell me you want the above to end - wuss! :p

If you do, there is only one answer: Vortex Lvl 2

This powerful attack has its drawbacks. Namely, Dante�s maddening tendency to target anything and everything but the final core itself � he will end up burrowing away enthusiastically at an ice slug on the ground, mark my words. Nevertheless, there isn't anything else which is better. Air Raid is actually rather good for the final core and I'd recommend it for an S-Rank, but not SB, game - it's too easy to get hit by a rogue missile from time to time. Stick with Vortex 2 - it's by far the fastest way of finishing off the final core, though you do need some luck to go with it - the auto-aim may go against you (�ooh, look, a pretty slug!�), you may get hit, or your DT may just drain dry before the end...

Tap the attack button like crazy to maximise Vortex's damage output - you won't be able to stay at the core, but will keep spinning off past it. Keep the distances that you travel to and from the core as short as possible. Ten runes of DT can be enough to defeat the final core, even on DMD mode, but DT will often run out before the end. If this happens, scramble to one of the blue walls as fast as possible, then build up DT with grenade-rolling or by walking and firing E&I; - the moment you get a few runes back, start Vortexing again.


Nightmare 3's patterns

In case you skipped the relevant part in the Nightmare 1 and 2 notes ;p

[Beginning of battle / After return from Underworld] > Ice beam/Projectiles -> Triple Stab -> Ice Beam/Projectiles -> Triple Stab -> Boomerang -> Triple Stab -> [Rinse and repeat]

So, in short, there is 1 Boomerang for every 2 Ice Beams. Incidentally, the Boomerang attack can�t be forestalled � going round to the back of Nighty won�t trigger the Projectiles attack.

Again, the pattern can change, but it should last for as long as you need it to.


Battle

Final core aside, Nightmare 3 is similar in operation to Nightmare 1 and 2 - the biggest difference is that Trish interrupts the action very near the end of Nightmare's first lifebar to offer sweets (>_>). At any rate, for all things Nightmare-related, check the Nightmare 1 and Nightmare 2 sections for details.

The arena is by far the smallest one this time and, hence, there's very little space for avoiding the areas of the battlefield which have jarring camera angle changes. You'll just have to learn where they occur and take them in your stride. It's not as bad as it sounds, though - because the area is so small, in some ways it's easier to deal with Nightmare.

For the following walkthrough, I'll assume you have a top green core.

Cancel the cutscene and run immediately to the nearest dial to light it up. (It's impossible to distinguish between the two dials in such a featureless arena, so I won't even try. It doesn't matter which one you use, although I tend to use the same one all the time. Oh all right then - if you want to know which one it is, I make Dante run to the further away wall with his back to the camera immediately after cancelling the cutscene - there isn't a camera change on the way to the dial. That's the dial I continue to use throughout battle.)

At any rate, if you were fast enough in reaching the dial, an ice slug won't come out and you can do a double taunt if DT is required. Do the usual business of waiting in front of Nighty to trigger an Ice Beam, throwing off the tracking fire and running or walking around to the other side of Nighty to jump on top of him. Charge up a PPKK + PP (only add the 2nd P of the 2nd PPKK if you were extremely quick in landing next to the core - even then, it's a good idea to only partially charge it). Afterwards, only do a single double taunt, then jump off Nighty and head like an arrow towards the dial - if you dally even a tiny bit here, you'll trigger Nighty's annoying-as-hell ceiling attack.

Next up will be the Triple Stab attack, so parry that and then do a PPKK + P. If you do the first P of the 2nd PPKK combo, you won't get enough time to complete a double taunt before the machine gun fire starts, so jump away with a grenade gun shot and head towards the dial as Nighty sinks back into gloop.

Light the dial, trigger another Ice Beam attack this time, and do another PPKK + PP on the top core. After this, a good two thirds of Nightmare's life should be gone - or a bit less if you didn't get maximum damage from the previous turns at the core. Make sure about 65% of Nighty's life has gone before going down to the Underworld.

If you did as I suggested, upon your return to the upper world (although, strictly speaking, you are in Hell now, but that's another story), Trish's cutscene will be triggered - it's a good time saver if you trigger Trish's cutscene with a return from the Underworld, rather than having her interrupt one of your turns at the core halfway through and making Nightmare return instantly to gloop.

If Nightmare's red lifebar is very low by this point and you feel daring, trigger either the Ice Beam or the Projectiles and go to town with 10 runes of Vortex 2! Doing this makes it even less likely that you will finish Nightmare's final core off before DT runs out, but hey it is fast. Failing that, trigger the Ice Beam after your return from the Underworld, chip Nighty's lifebar down to almost zero, replenish at least most of your DT, then trigger the final core during the next turn at the core.


Summary

- Light dial, trigger ice beam, do PPKK + P or PP, depending what you have time for
- Light dial, trigger triple stab, do PPKK + P
- Light dial, trigger ice beam; do PPKK + P or PP
- (optional triggering of Nighty's triple stab for another PPKK + P)
- Visit to the Underworld, switch to Alastor
- Light dial, trigger the ice beam (or projectiles), then use Vortex Lvl 2 to trigger the final core and try to finish it off.

---

The above strategy is the fastest possible way to defeat Nighty, but you can afford to take it a bit slower than that � for example, if you missed a couple of hits, you may want to trigger a 2nd triple stab attack before going down to the Underworld � this is fine. If you have a blue top core, you�ll have to take it a bit slower anyway � if you want to trigger Trish�s cutscene upon your return from the Underworld, then you�ll need to do four rounds of attacks, not three: ice beam, triple stab, ice beam, and triple stab.

Going down to the Underworld is compulsory this time, not optional. You don�t have to go down at the time I suggested if you would rather go down earlier � you can go down to the Underworld at the very beginning of battle if you want to. You should not go down later than I suggested, though, as no matter how late you go down, Nightmare will always have a small bit of red life upon your return � it�s impossible to trigger the final core with a return from the Underworld.

If you�ve followed the Nightmare 1 and 2 strategies, it is inconceivable you will have a top or a bottom red core for Nighty 3. But if by some chance you have, then good luck � with a top red core and a blue/green lower core, I believe it should still be quite doable. With two red cores, it�s just within the realm of possibility, but you will need the mother of all flawless battles to get the time under 4:30. I think my best time has been 4:40, but I�m pretty sure I could get it under 4:30.

For details of the final core, read the final core section ;p

Good luck! Nobody I know has defeated Nightmare 3 and then not gone on to finish SBing the game � the force is with you :p



Normal mode

As DMD mode, except the Frosts have slightly more health and Nightmare is considerably weaker.


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 21 =- -= Living Cave =-
_______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 5:00
Orbs: 700
Damage: 0%

Another non-boss mission, but a much more irritating one than Mission 19 � it will probably take you a few tries to get through this.

Begin by going right until you reach the door two torches on either side and go through it. Vortex across the room to instantly light up the 'vase' thing on the other side and trigger a cutscene of a door opening. Vortex or Stinger back to the door, exit, and go through the newly-unlocked door.

It�s almost impossible to get lost in this new area, provided you keep on moving forwards and don�t turn right at the two dead-end tunnels along the way. If you�re really confused, press L2 to bring up the map, but I should imagine if you�re doing an SB game, you know which way to go by now.

It won�t take long before you come to the first lot of multi-coloured tentacles - these little fuckers get very attached to you indeed. The easiest way to get past them unscathed is to equip E&I; and walk through the centre, blasting non-stop. If you have the grenade gun equipped, then you can grenade-roll your way through - a grenade will make the tentacles closest to you retract briefly. It's quite tricky, so use Stinger or Rolling Blaze to help you along. Make the first set of tentacles on the left retract first, then quickly roll your way past them and make the second set of tentacles on the right retract, then quickly run on ahead into the first cave.

Once you get inside the cave, a spell will descend on the exits and the hooting cry of a lone Nobody will be heard. Do a single Inferno, then finish it off with grenade-rolling. If you�re using Alastor or Sparda, then repeatedly Stinger its rear end. Grab its orbs and run on ahead (the shattering hands won�t hurt you, if you didn�t know this by now). You will quickly come across a second Nobody. Kill that one, too, and be more cautious when pressing ahead as there will soon be more tentacles. Fortunately, they are more spaced out this time - there is one set of tentacles to your left, then another set of tentacles to your right, and finally another set of tentacles in the ground. These are easier to grenade-roll past than the earlier lot of tentacles.

Be careful when you reach the third Nobody � there are tentacles in this area and they have a long reach. Try to get back all your DT from this Nobody; you will need it.

The two Plasmas in the next area are maddening � you need their orbs for a start, except their orbs have an infuriating habit of dropping onto the lava if you kill them from a distance with long-range attacks. You can wait for the Plasmas to reach you, using audio cues to evade their offscreen attacks until they do. The only other way to guarantee the red orb requirement is to backtrack at the beginning of the mission back to the two Frosts in the room before the Nightmare 3 room - yes, there is enough time to do this. If you get the two Frosts' orbs then you don't need to worry about the Plasmas' orbs.

A barely-charged Meteor will take care of one bat nicely. I would kill the second bat with non-DTed attacks (grenades, Roundtrip) to rebuild your DT for the next mission - having a full, or mostly full, gauge for the next mission is an extremely good idea.

Once the Plasmas have gone down, jump across the rocky stepping stones and light up the dial. You can save some time here with Air Hike. Three circular platforms will rise up, one after the other, but you don�t need to wait for the third one to appear � you can Air Hike over to the next ledge from the second or even first platform. If you'd rather be safe than sorry, though, then wait for the second or third platform to appear, both of which are closer to the ledge than the first one. Slash away at the giant vase as fast as you can to get some red orbs, but it doesn�t seem to yield as many orbs as the normal red orb fountains do.

For the second dial, you don't even need to bother lighting it up � you can Air Hike straight over to the next ledge, and then the next ledge again after that. But again, if you�d rather be safe than sorry, etc. etc. At the top, go through the red membrane and then do an automatic leap across to the dial. Light it up, cancel the cutscene, then Stinger across to the door � you don�t have to wait for all the hands to finish shattering before you can go through. I will capitalize the next bit:

DON�T FORGET TO EQUIP THE SHOTGUN BEFORE ENDING THIS MISSION!

As at the end of Mission 2, you can't say that I didn't warn you. No :p


Normal mode

Pretty much as DMD mode, really.


top



_______________________________________________________________________________

-= Mission 22 =- -= Legendary Battle =-
_______________________________________________________________________________

Time: 8:00 (5:00 for Normal/Hard modes)
Orbs: 0
Damage: 0%

You made it! Welcome to the most difficult mission of the game so far!

You might have noticed there�s a time extension for DMD mode � it�s a necessary one too, because you really will need all of those 8 minutes to defeat DMD Mundus 1 and 2. Anyway, this is the only mission in the game where there is nothing I can write about before the boss battle. Simply Stinger or Vortex up to the statue :p



Boss Battle � MUNDUS 1

I hope you like button-mashing... >_>


Your moves

You are now in Sparda DT, and hence your moveset has changed.

Shoot � Self-explanatory. Mash on the shoot button to keep firing weak-arsed white-red energy blasts. You don�t need to hold R1 down to shoot.

Vortex � This is unrelated to Alastor�s Vortex. Tap Circle to make Dante spin once only; hold Circle down to make Dante spin up to four times. You cannot keep spinning continously - after each Vortex, you have to wait about 1.5 seconds before the game lets you do another Vortex. However, Vortex also grants Dante generous i-frames, which comes in handy for Vortexing through attacks such as Mundus's giant laser. You can also use Vortex to make Dante travel faster in a forwards direction through space.

Speed boost � Holding down the Triangle button gives Dante a speed boost, but only in a 2D plane � in either words, Dante can speed up left or right or up or down, but he cannot go any faster forwards. The speed boost is incredibly useful for evading a large variety of attacks when travelling towards Mundus in outer space - simply zoom around the edges of the screen in a rectangular or diamond-shaped pattern to evade most of the cosmic junk Mundus slings at you.

DT Dragon � Press L1 to send a huge-arse dragon! :p The power of the dragon is directly related to how many DT runes you have filled � a 10 rune dragon will deal the most damage, a 3 rune dragon the least damage. You can get a 'double' hit at preset times to double the damage you deal - a valuable trick absolutely worth making the effort to learn. The DT dragon can also be used a last resort to get out of trouble. If Mundus is laying on the pressure with stacked attacks, you can send a dragon to make Dante invulnerable from the moment the dragon is sent. The invulnerability ends after the dragon has hit Mundus - but as Mundus's attacks don't stop whilst the dragon is travelling towards him, you may need to evade very suddenly after the dragon has hit Mundus.


Movement

Dante can be moved in one of two ways: with the analogue stick or with the D-pad. Using the analogue stick gives you inverted flight-style controls, e.g., moving the analogue stick up makes Dante move down, and moving it down makes Dante moves up. Using the D-pad gives you regular controls, e.g., pressing up makes Dante move up and pressing down makes Dante move down. It is entirely up to the player which method of movement they use. I personally prefer the D-pad, though, as I prefer the control it gives me over Dante�s movements.


Mundus 1's moves

Mundus 1 has a wide variety of long-range and short-range moves, although it should be noted that all of the short-range moves can be done at long-range, too. His moves can be "stacked" together, so that two or even three attacks are occurring on the screen simultaneously. I will deal with the stacked attacks a bit later - for now, these are all the single attacks.

Long-Range moves:

Fireballs � Mundus will raise his left arm above his head to summon several waves of white-yellow fireballs which come streaking towards you from the top of the screen. Despite their large size and their filling up a good part of the screen, they are surprisingly easy to evade. Simply move around in a large, horizontal figure-of-eight pattern in the bottom half of the screen � if a fireball gets too close for comfort, give yourself a speed boost or Vortex your way through it. Alternatively, holding down triangle and zipping around the edge of the screen makes evading the fireballs trivial.

Dark Grey Rocks � Mundus will stick his right arm out horizontally to summon several grey rock slabs which come flying towards you from the centre of the screen. Sometimes the first one can take you by surprise, since the dark grey of the rock blends in rather well with the outer space background - if this happens, hold down triangle and quickly fly away or else Vortex through it and then fly away. To evade the rocks, simply move in a large diamond-shaped pattern around the screen � holding down triangle for a speed boost makes it even easier. You can Vortex through the rocks to regain about a quarter of a DT rune per rock, but, given how long it takes to recover from a Vortex, this idea isn't really the brightest star in the galaxy.

Lightning Bolts � Mundus will raise his right arm above his head to summon 'curtains' of vertical lightning bolts which usually come streaking towards you for a long time. Finding a way through the gaps isn't hard, although you may need to occasionally to put on a dash or do a Vortex should you misjudge the distance of the bolts and threaten to travel headlong into one. The lightning bolts, on their own, are pretty easy to deal with. It's what they come accompanied by that turns them into something of a nightmare :p

Laser Beam � From a position where his arms are crossed against his chest, Mundus will move his left arm across his face as if "drawing" a circle � as he moves his arm, a gigantic white circle will form, with 8 symbols appearing one after the other. Once the circle has been "drawn", Mundus will stick out both arms horizontally for a split second, then snap them back to his chest as a huge white laser beam comes out. The laser beam can travel vertically from top to bottom or horizontally from right to left � as far as I know, there is no way of knowing in advance which direction it will travel in. I recommend Vortexing through this attack, although it is possible to evade the laser beam with a well-timed move to the sides. You should move over to the left side of the screen when you see the white circle forming to give yourself the maximum amount of time to respond � as the laser beam appears, time a Vortex of 2-3 spins just before it reaches you. Once you become familiar with the extended i-frames of Vortex, you won't even break into a sweat - pause, do a quick Vortex, then carry on as almost if nothing had happened. If there are lasers being fired when the laser beam appears, it becomes a tad trickier. Keep the D-pad button or analogue stick pressed down to resume travelling as soon as you've recovered from the Vortex - if Dante is static after recovering from the Vortex, he could get hit by one of the lasers, so it's best to make him move again as soon as possible.

Propeller Blades � So-called, because this attack resembles a series of slowly rotating propeller blades making their way towards you. Mundus will throw out his right arm horizontally to summon the "propeller blades", each one consisting of two small grey spheres connected by a long straight wire. These are actually a pain. They continue to appear for a long time and they take up most of the screen. You can evade them by moving around the edges of the screen or you can move around in a circular pattern in the centre of the screen. Luckily, this is one of Mundus 1's rarer attacks.

Short-range moves:

Pink Lasers (1) � Mundus will bend his head forward and cross his arms so that that they are covering his face. He will then throw out his arms and lift his head up � a split second later, his hands will start to glow bright pinkish-orange and small, thin, scattered pink lasers will be fired from both hands. For about 5 seconds these lasers will continue to be fired. To evade them, keep continuously moving in a rectangular, circular, or figure-of-eight pattern and DON'T double back on yourself or pause.

Pink Lasers (2) � Mundus will bend his head forward and cross his arms so that they are covering his chest. He will then throw out his arms and lift his head up � but this time, his hands won�t glow. Instead, larger, wider pink lasers will be fired from a hidden spot somewhere behind Mundus�s back (his arse >_>). For about 5 seconds these lasers will continue to be fired. Evade them the same way as Pink Lasers (1).

Circle of Grey Spheres � You should learn the telegraph for this attack, as it gives you an opportunity for getting a double hit with the DT dragon. Mundus will cross his arms, then lightly rest his right hand on his left shoulder � a second later, he will throw out his right arm and a circle of six small grey spheres will appear in front of him. These grey spheres will fire small, thin, scattered white lasers for about 20 seconds before finally disappearing. Evade these lasers the same way as you would the pink ones. If Mundus does this as a long-range attack, then the circle will turn into a disorganized jumble of grey spheres � however, the lasers should still be evaded the same way as before.

Vortex � Mundus has a Vortex move very similar to your own, but he will only use it when he has had enough of your attacks at close range and breaks away to put the maximum possible distance between you and him. When he decides enough is enough, he will float off to the right of the screen, then float back to the centre again � you will temporarily lose control of your character during this time as, in a mirror of Mundus's movement, he will also float to the right of the screen and back to the centre again. Mundus will then raise both arms above his head, crossed at the wrists (at this exact point you can get a double hit with the DT dragon), then quickly move both arms around in a circle and fold his arms across his chest. After that, he will wrap his giant wings around him and begin to Vortex away � depending on how far away he is from you, he will do either one or two spins before reaching you. If you are in the centre or lower centre of the screen, then you can time a Vortex to match Mundus�s Vortex. Begin a Vortex on either his first spin if he is closer to you or his second spin if he is further away - don't try to move forwards when you Vortex, but leave the analogue stick neutral. If your Vortex connects with Mundus�s Vortex, you will damage him. If, on the other hand, Mundus's Vortex hits you, considerably more damage will be dealt to you. However, it isn't hard to learn how to Vortex Mundus consistently and I would advise doing so.


Stacked moves (Hard/DMD mode only):

Hard/DMD Mundus is capable of stacking almost any combination of attacks together. By "stacking", I mean doing two or even three attacks simultaneously. Stacked combinations can include the following:


- Lightning Bolts + Fireballs
- Rocks + Fireballs
- Lightning Bolts + Propeller Blades
- Lightning Bolts + Fireballs + Pink Lasers (1)
- Rocks + Pink Lasers (1)
- Rocks + Circle of Grey Spheres + Fireballs

Note that this isn't an exhaustive list and there may be other combinations I haven't mentioned.

By far and away the two most popular combinations are the first two which are listed: Lightning Bolts + Fireballs and Rocks + Fireballs � you will see at least one of those nearly every time you travel down towards Mundus through outer space.

Most of the stacked combinations can be evaded in the same way as the single attacks - simply keep on flying around the edge of the screen, using the speed boost if necessary. However, certain combinations are more hair-raising - namely, any stacked combination which contains lightning bolts. To evade lightning bolts + fireballs and/or lasers, your best bet is to move in a sweeping figure-of-eight pattern in the bottom half of the screen, using either Vortex or the speed dash to evade any stray fireballs, bolts or lasers which get too close for comfort. Fortunately, this madness doesn't last for very long - once you're past it, breathe a sigh of relief and carry on travelling down towards Mundus.

Normal mode Mundus can't do the stacked attacks as described, but he CAN begin a new attack right at the end of another attack: for example, fireballs can appear immediately after you've just evaded the last lightning bolt. He can still keep you on your toes in other words.


Attack patterns and the double hit

The basic pattern is straightforward enough - Dante travels towards Mundus through outer space until he reaches the point he can't go forwards any more. At this point he stops in front of Mundus and shoots at him for a while until eventually Mundus decides he has had enough. Mundus flies away and puts the maximum possible distance between himself and Dante - and the cycle begins all over again. I call each cycle a "pass."

So far, so good. You will also know that to damage Mundus, you must first disable an invisible defense shield by shooting at the four dark spheres which rotate slowly in front of him. Once the defense shield has shattered, then you can damage Mundus by shooting at his body. The only way you can damage Mundus without disabling his defense shield first is to send a DT dragon, which overcomes defense.

What you might not know is that Dante doesn't stop at an equal distance from Mundus every time. Instead, he is programmed to stop at a further distance away from Mundus on odd-numbered passes and to stop closer to Mundus on even-numbered passes. So, on pass 1, 3, 5, etc. Dante will be further away from Mundus than on pass 2, 4, 6, etc. All deeply fascinating so far. But is any of it remotely relevant to anything? Kind of. It can make a difference if you're going for a double hit with the DT dragon.

On odd-numbered passes it's impossible to get a double hit with the DT dragon with less than 10 DT runes. I found this out in one game where I reached Mission 22 with only 9 DT runes - to my surprise, I could NOT get the double hit with those 9 runes. At first I thought my timing or positioning was somehow 'off', but once I obtained the 10th rune I was fine with getting the double hit. On even-numbered passes, where you're closer to Mundus, it's much easier getting the double hit. You can even get it with as few as 3 runes, although the damage will be proportionately that much less.

However, I must admit to only having tested this for the Circle of Grey Spheres attack. In other words, I'm not sure if you can get a double hit with 9 or fewer runes at any other time on an odd-numbered pass. If you are doing an SB game, then this is all fairly academic anyway, since you can afford to buy all seven purple orbs long before you reach Mission 22 - well, unless you're doing a default health/DT game, I suppose... >_>


Getting a double hit

Learning to get a double hit comes highly recommended. It's not essential for winning the battle, but it's an important string in your bow. On DMD mode, you can expect a 10 rune dragon hitting twice for a double hit to take about 35% off Mundus's life. Below are the times when you can achieve a double hit with the DT dragon.

1). Immediately prior to the Circle of Grey Spheres - Mundus will rest his right hand on his left shoulder for 1-2 seconds, then fling it up into the air to summon the circle of six grey spheres. When you spot Mundus putting his right hand on his left shoulder, quickly get in front of his chest - positioning matters - and then DT whilst his hand is still resting on his left shoulder. Watch his fingers: when they're fully stretched out is the ideal moment to send the dragon. Although Mundus can do this as a long range attack, you can only get the double hit when it is done at short-range.

2). Immediately prior to Mundus Vortexing away - Mundus will raise both arms above his head, arms crossed at the wrists, then move them around in a circle. After that, he will wrap his wings around him and Vortex away. Send the dragon at the moment Mundus's arms are crossed at the wrists above his head or when he is moving his arms around in a circle. You should be in the centre or the lower centre of the screen when you DT. After the dragon has hit, Mundus will carry on Vortexing away - get ready to meet his Vortex with a Vortex of your own.

3). Immediately after the Laser Beam appears on the screen - After Mundus finishes "drawing" a large white circle, the huge laser beam will come out. From the centre or the bottom left of the screen, send the DT dragon just before the laser beam reaches you.

For a very nifty instructional video on how to get double hits against Mundus 1, please refer to the links section below. I don't know the name of the author who made it, but it helped me out a lot - prior to watching it, I had only known how to get a double hit during the Circle of Grey Spheres attack.


Battle

The fast way:

Get a good quality turbo controller. Set it on x30 speed. The end :p

The slow way:

Fly around. Button-mash interminably. The end. Eventually.

What? :p

Oh, STRATEGY. Right :p

*coughs* *takes a deep breath... >_>*


Begin battle by shooting at the dark spheres in front of Mundus - each dark sphere requires a few shots before it disappears. You should be able to destroy at least one sphere before needing to evade the crap which Mundus starts to sling at you. After that, concentrate on evading the fireballs, rocks, lightning bolts, etc., but also keep on shooting at the dark spheres whenever you can. Hopefully you can disable the defense shield before stopping in front of Mundus, but it's not the end of the world if you don't.

I have experimented with sending a dragon towards Mundus at the very start of battle, but came to the conclusion it wasn't worth doing. Firstly, you can't get the double hit and even a 10 rune dragon causes a fairly puny amount of damage on DMD mode unless you get a double hit with it. Secondly, Mundus will summon a new defense shield almost immediately after being hit, meaning you will still have to go to the trouble of destroying the dark spheres. Thirdly, and perhaps most importantly, sending a dragon when travelling through outer space towards Mundus cuts short the length of time he will stay put before Vortexing away: sometimes he will begin to Vortex away before you've even come to a stop in front of him! For all these reasons, then, I withhold sending a dragon until I've come to a stop in front of Mundus.

Once you get within a certain range of Mundus, he will switch over from his long-range attacks to his short-range ones: Pink Lasers (1 & 2), the Circle of Grey Spheres, and the Laser Beam. You will need to keep continuously moving to evade the lasers Mundus sends out on a near constant basis. Some players strafe Mundus in a horizontal or vertical figure-of-eight pattern. I have never got on with this - inverted flight-style controls apparently don't suit me. Therefore I use the D-pad to move around in a rectangle the length and breadth of Mundus's body. There is a trick you can use to squeeze in a few more shots - you can slow yourself down by stopping and starting; as long as you don't stop for more than a split second each time you won't get hit. When using the D-pad, it's possible to travel in a ultra narrow rectangle shape, enabling you to hit Mundus's body as you travel both down and up. However, it's risky. A rogue laser is much more likely to eventually hit you than if you play it safe by travelling in a wider rectangle. Obviously, the pay-off is doing more damage to Mundus, however.

Whichever method you use for flying around, keep firing like a maniac at Mundus until the opportunity for a double hit arises. Hopefully you will have 10 filled runes when it does! Once a double hit is achieved, the shock of it will always make Mundus Vortex away without fail. But if you get only a single hit, then sometimes Mundus will just summon a new defense shield and remain put - this is especially likely if you didn't use all 10 runes to send a dragon.

Typically, you can remove 50-60% of Mundus's life in the first pass - this includes the Vortexing of Mundus as he Vortexes away. (Remember, as it's an odd-numbered pass, Mundus is further away from you, so time your Vortex on his second spin). It's possible to finish off Mundus in two passes without a turbo controller on DMD mode, but it's TOUGH. You'll be beginning the second pass with no DT and hence will need to refill it from shooting at Mundus's body, once the defense shield has been disabled. When you have stopped in front of Mundus, keep shooting until he begins the preparations for Vortexing away, then go for a double hit - hopefully the double hit + the Vortex you do against him (time it on Mundus's first spin this time) will be sufficient to finish him off. If not, then you will have to defeat Mundus during the third pass. Much of it depends on your stamina and your button-mashing skills.

Using a turbo

I make no secret of the fight that Mundus 1 is my most detested boss in DMC1. I pretty much like every other boss, but not him. (Heck, I've even come around to Griffon 2 - kinda.) Just so you know, I have used the standard Sony dual shock controller to get past DMD Mundus 1 damageless many many times by now, so I feel that I have earned my rightful dues in using my crappy �10 turbo controller from GAME. It has to be said that using a turbo does make this fight a lot easier and if you have a good turbo controller (which I don't), it makes an actual mockery of it. It is up to you, the player, to decide whether to use a turbo or not. Technically, it's probably, whilst not exactly cheating, still a bit of a grey area. On the other hand, once I've been hit by some cheap arse laser for the 20th time running, then I really don't give a flying fuckery any more.


top

Boss Battle � MUNDUS 2

And you thought the Mundus 1 section was long :p

Your moves

On land, Dante cannot equip any Devil Arm except for Sparda, but he can equip any gun except for the Needlegun. The Sparda "piggybacks" three of Alastor's abilities - Roundtrip, Stinger and Air Hike - but they will only be available if you've already bought them for Alastor. Air Raid and Vortex aren't available at all. (If you see an ARMAX-hacked video of precisely how much damage Vortex causes to Mundus 2, you'll understand why >_>.) For Ifrit moves, the Sparda has Meteor. To charge up Sparda's Meteor, you'll need to have bought Meteor Lvl 2 for Ifrit. It works slightly differently in that you need to press Square to summon Meteor - and if you charge the Meteor by holding down Square, you cannot stop an initial uncharged one from being sent first of all.

Mundus 2's moves

As with Mundus 1, Mundus 2 can do "stacked" attacks whereby he does 2 or 3 attacks simultaneously. More on that later. For now, I'll divide his movelist into spinning spheres and everything else.

Spinning Spheres:

You'll come to love the spinning spheres almost as much as Mundus 1's lasers. *rolls eyes*. There are three beige spinning spheres formations, plus one additional formation involving the larger dark spheres. The telegraph for all four formations is identical: Mundus rests his right hand on his hip and motions upwards with his left hand, jabbing a finger into the air. You should soon become pretty familiar with it, since Mundus does it often enough.

1). Beige Spheres: "One-By-One" - Mundus summons five beige spinning spheres which appear one after the other. A beige sphere appears, remains still for about 1 second, and then starts spinning - as it spins, it sends out two waves of small white lasers, which have a wide 'area-of-effect' coverage. Two non-DTed shotgun shots, or one DTed shotgun shot, will destroy a beige sphere. If Dante doesn't destroy it before it starts spinning, however, then a second beige sphere will appear while the first one is still spinning and sending out lasers. The one-by-one spheres track Dante's position, so they cannot be given the slip by running away - if Dante runs along the platform, then the beige spheres will follow after him. In order to shoot them more easily, you should stand at the back of the platform to get a better angle on them. The shotgun-twitch technique comes in massively handy for this particular formation.

2). Beige Spheres: "Six-In-A-Row" - Mundus summons a horizontal row of six beige spheres which appears in the centre of the platform. It appears in the same position relative to Dante, that is, wherever Dante is, he will always be smack bang in the middle of the row. Thus all Dante needs to do is move forwards or backwards in a straight line and he will end up in between the 3rd and 4th spheres. To destroy the spheres, have him face one side and shoot twice, then face the other side and shoot twice - if done correctly, Dante will destroy all six spheres with a total of four shots. Heck, there is even enough time to charge the shotgun to destroy 3 spheres at a time with just 1 shot. Very commonly, not all 6 spheres are actually destroyed, but 1 or 2 at either end are left remaining behind - don't worry if this is the case. In fact, there's even something to be said for keeping at least one sphere 'alive'. This is because as long as the six-in-a-row attack is still in operation, Mundus can't summon any new spinning spheres. You should, however, keep to the front of the platform, since any leftover spheres will travel around the battlefield and pass the platform for a 2nd time, before finally vanishing. The six-in-a-row spheres can only fire their lasers outwards, so as long as you are in front of them - at the very front of the platform - they cannot actually hit you.

3). Beige Spheres: "Ring-Of-Spheres" - Mundus summons six beige spheres which surround his head in the shape of a ring. For quite a long time (about 15 seconds?) they won't do anything, but if Dante doesn't destroy them, they will start to send out streams of thin white lasers. It's usually not difficult to find a safe place to stand in between the streams of lasers; the danger arises if Mundus does an attack in the meantime which forces you into moving sideways to evade. To destroy the spheres, stand at the back of the platform and blast away with the shotgun until the ones in front of him are destroyed - don't worry about the ones behind Mundus's head as the lasers from them won't reach you. This is by far the easiest spinning spheres formation to deal with, and, needless to say, also by far the rarest one. You can increase the likelihood of this formation appearing by standing at the front-most of the platform in front of Mundus or jumping up onto the floating rocks and comboing him from there. Nevertheless, you will still be seeing a great deal of the first two formations.

4). Dark Spheres - Mundus summons four large dark green spheres which are placed at equal distances from each other around the battlefield. The dark spheres, on their own, don't do anything except spin and, very slowly, move around the battlefield. You can destroy them with four shotgun shots each or a couple of Stingers - a destroyed dark sphere will give you 3.5 DT runes. Typically, one or two dark spheres will be within easy reach of you, but the remainder will be somewhere inaccessible, such as on the lava or behind Mundus on the other side of the battlefield. It isn't in your best interest to destroy every single dark sphere which Mundus sends out anyway. Once the dark spheres have been there for a certain period of time, Mundus will gather them all up, convert them into white blasts of energy, and sling them towards you. Deflecting these fireballs back to Mundus is easily the best way of taking large chunks of damage off him...


Mundus 2's Other Attacks:

There are enough of these, god only knows.

Magma Waves - Mundus holds one hand out, palm facing upwards, as he scoops his other hand over the lava, sending a transparently thin wave of magma towards you.
