SPECIAL NOTE - This is the only secret mission in the game which is a one-try event. If you try and fail, the secret mission will no longer be there. Worse, if you save after failing, your failed status will also be saved. If you reload the game, the secret mission will NOT be there - the only way to make it accessible again is to wait until the next playthrough. In short, if you fail, do not save. Reset the game and try again. Yes, it is a pain...

Strategy - Take out one Shadow while it is still in the alcove; from the elevator run forwards for about 5 steps, then turn left (from your POV) - watch out for the jarring camera change as you swerve left - and target the Shadow in the alcove. Nine times out of ten it will start preparing its horizontal spike, giving you an easy opportunity to jump up and land on the spike. In Normal/Hard, CH it. In DMD, use DTed E&I; after the spike retracts to expose the core and turn it red with two DTed Stingers. Now run for your life as the red Shadow runs after you - it's a good idea to run and jump in case a Shadow from offscreen hurls itself at you, as is often the case. Once one Shadow has gone down, dealing with two becomes a lot more manageable, especially if you don't shoot at them, but use the CH strategy instead - try to align yourself along an imaginary line with the two Shadows, with you at one end, a Shadow in the middle and a second Shadow at the other end. That way you can keep both Shadows in view and if the middle Shadow does its horizontal spike, you should be able to land on it and shoot without the other Shadow interfering - well, in theory. The Camera Angles Of Sheer Unhelpfulness� will still conspire to trip you up as much as possible. Once the second Shadow has gone down, dealing with the remaining solo Shadow should be trivial.

top


SM5 - One-Eyed Evil

Mission it's found in - Mission 7 or the intermission between Mission 7 & 8

When it must be completed - As above

Location - The basement which contained the rusted key in Mission 6

Objective - Make one Kyklop kill the other one

SPECIAL NOTE - This secret mission is only triggered AFTER the blue orb fragment in the nearby alcove has been picked up - the easiest time to pick it up is at the beginning of Mission 6. Turn right and wall-jump in the alcove to pick up the blue orb piece. It can also be picked up later, however.

Strategy - Keep jumping in and out between the two Kyklops to bait them into spewing rocks at each other. Try to linger for just long enough to make sure that the rock attack hits the other Kyklop, as, if you jump away immediately, the Kyklop will turn away from its mate to track your position instead. DT whenever you have it for increased speed. Don't use guns or melee attacks of any kind, as you will automatically fail. Try to stick to the corner with the blue door, as that way you can see both Kyklops. If one or both of them go on a jumping spree, run and jump until it's over (Air Hike is a big help here), then get back to baiting them into spewing rocks at each other again. As soon as one Kyklop has died, collect the blue orb and exit.

top


SM6 - Wandering Ghosts

Mission it's found in - Mission 11

When it must be completed - Mission 11, before Nelo Angelo 2

Location - The courtyard which contained the two Kyklops in Mission 10

Objective - Use the Sin Scissors to get the blue orb in the sky

Strategy - Use the Sin Scissors as "stepping stones" to jump up towards the blue orb in the sky. An easy way if you have Air Hike is to wait at the beginning of the mission until the Sins start appearing at the top of the screen, Air Hike straight up, then jump forwards to collect the blue orb - it takes about 3 seconds.

top


SM7 - Water Cell

Mission it's found in - Intermission between Mission 13 & 14 only

When it must be completed - After completing Mission 14, but before beginning Mission 14

Location - The room you began Mission 13 in - examine the captain

Objective - Defeat all eight Underwater Blades

Strategy - Swim around, shooting. If the Blades overwhelm you, you can keep swimming upwards - after a certain point, they won't pursue you. On the plus side, they can't hit you, but on the down side, you can't hit them. There's an easier way than that through this mission, however. Hold the controller in such a way that you can hold down R1, Triangle, and X simultaneously. This enables you to swim and shoot at the same time. By default, Dante will swim forwards, but you can make him swim forwards or sideways by holding the analogue stick in the relevant position. At the beginning you will be facing 4 Blades, with 4 more behind them - start swimming backwards and shooting until you've put a large distance between yourselves and the Blades. (Careful not to swim too far back, though, as at some point you will bump into an invisible wall.) Then keep moving left and right, strafing the Blades ahead of you with gunfire. With this technique, you can clear this secret mission damage-less. (Not that damage counts towards ranking in an intermission, but even so... >_>)

top


SM8 - Treasure Of The Reaper

Mission it's found in - Mission 15 and 16 - and possibly later missions, too

When it must be completed - The beginning of Mission 15 or 16 is the easiest time

Location - Backtrack to the spike trap area from Mission 14 - after taking the elevator, turn right (from your POV, not Dante's) and examine the picture of the skeleton

Objective - Defeat all three Shadows

Strategy - SM8 opens with an extremely cheap lunge from a Shadow which is only just evadeable. When the Shadow emerges from the picture during the (unskippable) cutscene, hold R1 + the analogue stick to the right or left, then keep tapping Triangle as fast as possible - this should ensure you roll out of the Shadow's way when the cutscene ends. There are three Shadows in total, but there is only one on the scene to start off with. If you're fast enough, you can kill each Shadow before the next one appears. Otherwise, if you take too long, you'll have to deal with two, or even three, Shadows simultaneously. The critical hit strategy works well, otherwise use DTed E&I; to open up the core as fast as possible. DTed Nightmare-Beta shots work well here, too. If you don't have Air Hike, then wall-jumps are a great substitute for evading the red Shadow's lunges.

top


SM9 - Stairway Of Tranquility

Mission it's found in - Mission 16

When it must be completed - The beginning of Mission 16

Location - Coliseum. Exit it then re-enter it

Objective - Jump up the "stairway" of Sargassoes to pick up the blue orb at the top

Strategy - After the brief cutscene ends, don't try to change Dante's position as he is already in the ideal spot. Instead, run forwards, then keep jumping up the line of Sargassoes. When you reach the last Sargasso, jump straight up to nab the blue orb. In Normal mode, the Sargassoes won't move from their positions, but in Hard/DMD mode, they will circle around. Air Hike makes this mission easier. If you fail, you can either exit and re-enter the coliseum to trigger the SM again or you can hold down R1 or R2 to make the camera pan around behind Dante and bring the "staircase" of Sargassoes in front of him once more.

top


SM10 - Shadow Of Darkness

Mission it's found in - Mission 16

When it must be completed - Mission 16 only, AFTER defeating Nightmare

Location - Biplane room

Objective - Yes, you've guessed it... Defeat all the Shadows. *wilts*

Strategy - In the biplane room, there are two inactive Shadows on the biplane wing. Awaken one of the Shadows by jumping up and firing a single E&I; shot only - if that doesn't work, fire another shot or two: the Shadow will wake up and slink off the plane. Defeat it in any way which suits you, then awaken the second Shadow and defeat that one too - if you like, you can jump up onto the biplane to evade the second Shadow after it has been turned red. Do NOT, whatever you do, exit via the red door. If you do, you will fail the mission and have to clear the biplane room again. Instead, exit via the BLUE door into the main hall. The third Shadow is in the alcove, so jump up there and awaken it with gunfire. You can get a critical hit in the alcove or you can make the Shadow follow you down onto the ground and fight it from there. The fourth Shadow is in the room where the castle entrance is, so go through the RED door in the main hall. Try DTing, then walking forwards, firing E&I; non-stop. If your guns start to point upwards, evade sideways! If they don't, you should expose the Shadow's core then and there. Once the fourth Shadow has gone 'kaboom', make your way back to the corridor to clear the mission.

top


SM11 - Blue Gem In The Sky

Mission it's found in - Mission 17

When it must be completed - Beginning of Mission 17

Location - The other side of the broken bridge. Go back through the portal and Air Raid or Vortex across the bridge pieces to the other side.

Objective - Defeat all the Shadows - oh. Sorry :p ... Collect the blue orb in the sky >_>

Strategy - You could use the Death Scythe's tornadoes to try and lift you up to the blue orb in the sky - or use the vastly easier way. Kill the Death Scythe, wait for DT refill, then jump up to the top of the arch - from there, wall-jump or Air-Hike straight up, then quickly, at the height of your jump, press L1 to DT then R1 to activate flight mode. Now either Vortex or glide over to the blue orb high above the broken bridge pieces. Continue to the other side, drop down, and exit via the left hand painting to clear the mission.

top


SM12 - Hidden Bangle

Mission it's found in - Mission 21

When it must be completed - Beginning of Mission 21

Location - In the room you start in, go counter-clockwise around it until you come to the divinity statue - examine the wall panel opposite.

Objective - Collect the Bangle of Time!

Strategy - Keep running forwards until you come to the first fork - it doesn't matter whether you go left or right, as both paths lead into the same cave. Defeat the Nobody. Pick up the blue orb, which is a WHOLE orb this time, not just one quarter of a whole. Keep moving forwards. As long as the camera is behind Dante, you can't get lost - if you start running towards the camera, you're going back on yourself. You'll have to fight two more Nobodies along the way before finally reaching a cavernous room with a fire-breathing skeleton. There's a red orb vase you can smash, if you like. To get through the next part, there's a short way and a long way. I'll describe the long way first of all.

Use the platforms on the right to jump up to the top, then defeat the dragon skeleton with melee attacks (guns won't work) in between its fiery breaths - sword slashes or uncharged PPKK combos will do. Once the skeleton is defeated, 2-4 Frosts (depending on difficulty mode - you'll get two on Normal, more on Hard/DMD) will appear. Jump down and defeat them with Inferno, Auto-Combos, etc. Once the Frosts are defeated, three moving eye platforms will materialize. Once again, jump up to the top, then go to the left side and jump up the platforms on the left. Hop onto the first moving eye platform, then, at the top, hop onto the second one (the third one is unnecessary, by the way). From the second platform, jump onto the ledge, then jump onto the ledge in front of that one - finally, you've reached the Bangle of Time! Pick it up to clear the mission.

Now for the shortcut. Go straight to where the first moving eye platform would be, ignoring the skeleton dragon. Stand next to the wall, DT, wall-jump up, then, at the height of your jump, quickly press R1. Glide right along the wall until you reach another ledge - go up to the slight dent, then press L1 to de-DT - Dante should automatically grab hold of the edge to haul himself up. Run to the end of the ledge, wall-jump or Air Hike up, DT, then continue to fly right until you come to the ledge with the Bangle of Time - drop down and pick it up! The shortcut is really easy to do once you know how to - but you should still probably practice it a few times first. You'll also need at least 7 runes of DT.

top





Basic Techniques


I'm not going to go into a tremendous amount of detail here - there are enough FAQs on GayFAQs covering the basics of DMC1 anyhow. But the below stuff is the stuff you really should know by now, if you want to do an SB game.

Truly basic

Rolling Dodge - R1 + analogue stick left/right, then press Triangle. Dante will do a roll to the left or right. The analogue stick, of course, has to be aligned left/right with Dante's position on the screen. This can be a surprisingly tricky move to master, but is worth the effort of doing so, as the rolling dodge can come in extremely useful at times - besides, the more evasive moves you have in your repetoire the better. It gets boring just jumping away all the time :p

High Time - R1 + analogue stick in direction of Dante's back, then press Circle. Dante will do High Time, an uppercut move which slashes certain enemies up into the air. Lightly tapping the Circle button will make Dante do a grounded High Time, where he stays on the ground. Pressing the Circle button for a bit longer will make Dante do a rising High Time, where he rises up into the air.

Stinger - R1 + analogue stick in direction of Dante's front, then press Circle. Dante will move forwards a certain distance, sword held horizontally in front of him. He travels further with Stinger Lvl 2 than Lvl 1.


Combos

Sword combos:

3-Hit Combo - O-O-O - Press Circle three times in a row very quickly. Dante will do two regular slashes, followed by a heavy downward slash.

4-Hit Combo - O-O--O-O - Press Circle twice, barely pause, then press Circle twice more. Dante will do two regular slashes followed by two quick uppercut slashes, and finish off with a downward slash. When I say "barely pause", I do mean barely - in effect, you don't pause at all. If you're using Alastor, time the third button press at the end of Dante's 2nd sword swing when there is still lightning on the sword.

Multi-Stab Combo - O-O---O-O-O-O-O-O-etc.- Press Circle twice, pause, then mash Circle several times. Dante will do two regular slashes followed by a series of small, thrusting stabs, and finish off with a longer, more damaging, stab. This time, pause for about 1 second. Time the third button press at the end of Dante's 2nd sword swing when the lightning has disappeared from the sword.

Note: These sword combos cover all four swords - Force Edge, Alastor, Yamato (LDK only), Sparda. The 4-Hit Combo is very slightly different for Sparda - the final downward slash is replaced with a thrusting stab similar to the final stab of the Multi-Stab combo.


Substituting Finishers:

Dante can insert a finishing move - Stinger, High Time, or (with some lagginess) a Roundtrip - after almost any sword slash. All you have to do is to insert the command for the finishing move you want at the right time. Some examples as follows.

4-Hit Combo with High Time finisher - O-O--O-[command for a High Time] - Press Circle twice, barely pause, press Circle again, then input the command for a High Time. If done correctly, a High Time will be done in place of the final downward slash. If you learn only one combo, learn this one!

3-Hit Combo with Stinger finisher - O-O-[command for a Stinger] - Press Circle twice, then input the command for a Stinger. A Stinger will be done in place of the downward slash.

Multi-Stab Combo with High Time finisher - O-O---O-O-O-O-O-O-O-O-[command for a High Time] - Press Circle twice, pause, mash the Circle button, then quickly input the command for a High Time. A High Time will be done in place of the final, thrusting, stab.

That's about it for basic sword combos. Experiment. See what moves you can cancel out of with another move and how early you can do it.


Ifrit combos:

PPKK, where P=Punch, K=Kick - O-O-O-O - Press the Circle button four times. If you tap the Circle button 4 times, each hit will be uncharged. You can hold down the Circle button to charge up any hit of the PPKK combo - the final K is the most damaging hit of the combo. You can cancel out of charging up a hit at any time - you are not commmitted to charging it up until it automatically releases. This is a slow but powerful combo when charged up.

Auto-Combo - In DT mode, press R1 + analogue stick in the direction of Dante's front + Circle. Dante will do an ultra-fast automatic PPKK combo. You need to have bought the Kick-13 ability before you can do an Auto-Combo. Once you have begun an Auto-Combo, you can quickly de-DT and regain some DT back from the subsequent hits, although the Auto-Combo will be less powerful overall if you do this.


Substituting Finishers:

As with sword combos, you can insert finishers after any hit of the PPKK combo.

PP-Magma Drive - O-O-[command for a Magma Drive] - Press Circle twice (up to you if you charge the Ps or not), then input the command for a Magma Drive. Interestingly enough, this is the only way you can do a DTed Magma Drive without needing to begin a Magma Drive in non-DT mode first then DTing as you charge it - the only other way is to simply not buy Meteor. If you input the command for a Meteor/Magma Drive straight after doing the first or second P of a PPKK combo, then you will always do a Magma Drive instead of a Meteor.

PPK-Kick13/Auto-Combo - Press Circle three times, then input the command for a Kick-13 or Auto-Combo. It's pretty obvious what will happen. You will do the first 3 hits of the PPK combo, followed by a Kick-13 or Auto-Combo instead of the final K. This combo works well against Nelo Angelo.

Once again, experiment. See what moves cancel smoothly into other moves.

top




Advanced Techniques


This covers the more advanced stuff of DMC1. It revolves around various cancelling techniques, as you will soon find out.


CANCELLING

DMC3 took cancelling to a new level, but nonetheless, it's surprising just how much cancelling you can do in DMC1. The concept is simple enough: most moves can be cancelled out of before they are finished by doing an action - usually a jump or a roll or another move. Think of beginning a taunt but jumping away before it's finished because an enemy is about to attack - you are cancelling the taunt. Before you even ask: no, you can't jump-cancel in DMC1. Well, technically you can, but not in the DMC3 sense of the term. (To those not familiar with DMC3, Jump-Cancelling is a technique where you can reset any aerial move with a jump off the enemy; this enables pro players to spam aerial attacks several times in mid-air.) However, the only move you can reset by jumping off an enemy in DMC1 is the jump. You can rebound off an enemy as many times as you like, but you can't reset, e.g., Ifrit's flame-kick with a jump off the enemy. If you want to get really pernickety, then any move you cancel early out of with a jump could be defined as "jump-cancelling." But the closest you can get to jump-cancelling in the DMC3 sense is shotgun-hiking - see below.


The types of cancelling:

Grenade-rolling - The grenade gun has a huge recoil after it's been fired, which makes Dante immobile for a few seconds. To enable him to fire the grenade gun again much faster, cancel the recoil time with a roll. Shoot, roll, shoot, roll, etc. - it's that simple really. Practice shooting as fast as you can after a roll; once you get the technique down, it becomes possible to grenade-roll pretty fast. You can also cancel the recoil with a hop-jump straight up, but usually rolling is better.

Shotgun-twitching - As with the grenade gun, there is a recoil after each shotgun shot. It's not as big as the one for the grenade gun, but nevertheless it's still slightly laggy. You can cancel the recoil of a shotgun shot, not with a roll, but with a movement of the analogue stick in any direction - use your thumb to flick it immediately after firing. The pattern is as follows: shotgun shot, flick analogue stick, shotgun shot, flick analogue stick, etc. Note that walking is not sufficient to cancel the recoil - you need to reset the analogue stick after shooting by letting it centre first. Practice in an empty room: if shells are dropping from the shotgun as you fire, then you are not cancelling the recoil. If you can repeatedly fire the shotgun without shells dropping, then congratulations: you have learnt to shotgun-twitch :p

Sword-Cancelling (aka as Slash-Cancelling) - Not only can sword slashes be substituted with other moves, their recovery time can also be cancelled. Try doing a single sword slash and then repeating it as fast as possible: you'll have to wait quite a while for Dante to recover first, since, if you slash again too soon, Dante will simply do a different type of slash. Repeat the experiment, but with pairs of sword slashes this time: slash twice in a row, then repeat them as soon as you can - take note of how long it takes Dante to recover. Now, try cancelling the lag of the first slash. Slash once and rotate the analogue stick in one full circle (or wiggle it back and forth a few times) - if done correctly, the animation of Dante putting away his sword will be cut short and you can do the same slash again immediately. Practice in an empty room until you can do a single sword slash over and over again, without any lagginess. After that, try doing pairs of slashes and cancelling the lag of the second slash this time - rotate the analogue stick during the second slash. Again, keep practicing until you can do repeated pairs of sword-cancelled slashes without any lagginess. The final stage is cancelling the lag of the 4th slash (the 2nd uppercut slash of the 4-Hit combo) - once again, you rotate the analogue stick, this time during the 4th slash. Note that only the 1st, 2nd, or 4th slash of any combo can be cancelled - the downwards slash or the final thrust of the Multi-Stab combo can not be cancelled. This technique is most useful against Nelo Angelo, but can also be applied to regular enemies such as Marionettes, Frosts, and propped-up Nobodies.

Infinite PPK - This is the Ifrit equivalent of sword-cancelling, although not as flexible, since it only works for the first K of the PPKK combo. You can cancel the lag of the first K to immediately begin a new PPK combo. Button-mash to do the first three hits, then as soon as the first K comes out, immediately do the input for either a Kick-13 or Magma Drive - make this input as brief as possible (a split second), then let the analogue stick centre and become neutral. You should now be able to do another PPK without a Kick-13/Auto-Combo or Magma Drive/Meteor coming out. (Yes, this can be done DTed as well.) This combo can be done against the usual suspects: Nelo Angelo, Frosts, propped up Nobodies, etc.

DT-Cancelling - Input the command for an attack in DT mode and then quickly press L1 to de-DT before the attack hits the enemy - upon impact, you will regain a certain amount of DT. This works mainly for Infernoes, Auto-Combos, and grenades. It's most useful for fighting Frosts with Ifrit, since you can restore the majority of your DT, provided you finish them off with a DT-cancelled Inferno or Auto-Combo. For grenades, you will get either 1 or 2 runes back for a DT-cancelled grenade - Fetishes and Griffon will give you back 2 runes, but most other enemies will only give you back 1 rune. DT-Cancelling isn't exactly the most vital technique there is, but once you get the hang of it, it has its little uses.

Shotgun-Hiking - This technique exploits the weird physics of DMC1's shotgun, which lifts the enemy up higher with each shot. I say 'enemy', but it can only really be used against Fetishes or Marionettes. Launch the Fetish with a rising High Time, then alternate between firing the shotgun and pressing the Jump button until the Fetish dies, like so: rHT > shotgun shot > Jump > Shotgun > Jump > until the Fetish dies. If you are familiar with jump-cancelling in DMC3, you will realise this is nothing like as easy as it sounds. One thing you don't do and that is button mash the Jump and Shoot buttons as fast as possible - the correct rhythm is actually slow-ish. Unlike in DMC3, you won't need any special techniques to go from Jump to Shoot ultra-fast, such as the 'slide input' technique - individual button presses are sufficient. You will, however, need Air Hike. I used to think it was the jump which reset the Air Hike and the Air Hike which reset the jump - now I'm not so sure, but shotgun-hiking for any length of time seems to be impossible without Air Hike. A good place to start practicing is in the room below the Mission 1 Rusty Key room in Hard/DMD mode: the low ceiling constrains the Fetish and prevents it from rising too high - for some reason, that makes shotgun-hiking quite a bit easier to do. Once you've learnt it there, practice with the Fetishes in Mission 10. For DTed Fetishes/Marionettes, you need to be in DT mode to launch them.

top





Terminology


Here are some definitions of terms. It's not in alphabetical order.

Taunting - You can make Dante taunt an enemy by holding down R2. Taunting is pressure-sensitive: a normal press of the R2 button will make Dante do a single-handed taunt; a sharp press of the R2 button will make him do a double-handed taunt. If you have trouble with doing a consistent double taunt, then try an old trick which used to work for me: mash on the R2 button a few times, then hold it down - normally the act of mashing is enough to make Dante do a double taunt. Does it actually matter? Of course >_>. Taunting is a valuable way of restoring DT. A single-handed taunt will restore 0.5-1 DT runes; a double-handed taunt will restore 1-2 DT runes. (The amount depends on distance from the enemy - if you're miles away, you won't get anything; if you're not very close, you'll get the smaller amount; and if you're very close, you'll get the larger amount.) If you can master the double taunt, it's one of the fastest ways of restoring DT there is. Note that you don't get any more DT for taunting multiple enemies than you do for single ones, however.

Critical Hit (CH) - A critical hit is, technically, a hit which fatally injures your opponent in a single blow. In DMC1 terminology, its meaning has been expanded to include double hits (an attack - usually Meteor - which hits your opponent near-simultaneously in two spots) or hits which strike a weak spot for additional damage. Enemies which can be fatally injured with a single blow are Blades, Sin Scissors and Sin Scythes. Shadows can be critically hit, but, strictly speaking, it isn't a one-hit kill. Bosses can never be killed in a single hit, but the ones which are susceptible to critical or double hits are Phantom, Griffon and Mundus 1. Check the relevant enemy/boss section for the actual method of how to CH said enemies.

Hop-jump/Air Hike - You can control the length and momentum of Dante's jumps and Air Hikes to a degree. A normal press of the Triangle button will make Dante do an ordinary length jump; a light tap of the Triangle button will make him do a shorter-than-usual jump - this short jump is also known as a 'hop' jump. Knowing how to hop-jump is essential for the Phantom CH strategy, but it is also useful for other situations - it's much easier to parry Nelo with Rolling Blaze if you jump away from him with a hop-jump, for example. It's also handy for enemies which are low to the ground, such as Frosts, etc. - you can more easily hit them with a hop-jump into them, followed by a Rolling Blaze hit, Inferno, etc.

Enemy-hike/kick-jump - Enemy hiking and kick-jumping off an enemy are one and the same thing; just as you can jump off a wall, so you can jump off an enemy. It can serve as a decent substitute for Air Hike, although you do need to get right next to the enemy to be able to jump off of it.

I-frames - Or invincibility frames in full. I'm mentioning i-frames because they do crop up in DMC1, although they don't play nearly as much importance as in DMC3. Put simply, i-frames are the number of frames your character is invincible for during a certain action, e.g., jump, roll, etc. For the jump, there are i-frames on the way up, but not on the way back down - if you time it right, you can jump up, unharmed, through Phantom's fireballs, Shadows' lunges, etc. There are also i-frames on the roll and even Dante's ordinary sword slash: for an example of the latter, go here. So, if you are invincible during certain actions, doesn't that make the game that much easier? No <_<. It's usually a lot easier to just roll or jump out of the way before the attack connects than to try to make use of the i-frames in DMC1.

Bangle Of Time - I'm just putting this here, because I'm not sure where else to put it :p ... Anyway, Bangle of Time. It's a bangle which controls time and you get it by completing Secret Mission 12. To activate it, press L1: it will consume your DT to freeze time. In frozen time, you can damage enemies with non-DT attacks, but the damage doesn't affect them until you unfreeze time. Also, the Bangle of Time doesn't work on any bosses except for Nelo Angelo 1 and 2 and Shadow Nelo. To be honest, I find the BoT has very few practical uses, but I suppose if you are really determined, you can get a bit of wear out of it. For example, freeze Nelo when he is vulnerable (taunting/slumped over) and then go to town on his front with a grenade > Kick-13 combo. Or freeze the Death Scissors just as it's about to attack, get behind it and do downward slashes on its head until DT runs out. And so on. Also, if there any rooms that you don't like to go through because of the danger of getting hit, freezing time will immobilize your attack-happy chums. Where the BoT really shines, however, is with Super Dante. With infinite DT, it's just a lot more fun to play around with. You can do things like High Time a Marionette in frozen time, charge up a Roundtrip, unfreeze time, and watch the Marionette being juggled by the Roundtrip in mid-air in non-frozen time >_>


---

This is all I can think of for now. If you are unsure about the meaning of any other terms in this guide, don't hesitate to drop me a line to ask :p

Credits


Annnnd... finally: the long overdue credits section, where I acknowledge everyone's help, right down to my two goldfish :p

In no particular order, thanks for the existence of this FAQ go to:

My parents - Okay, they go first, because without them there would be no me. Luckily, this tragic fate was averted by my parents conceiving me one cold night in January.

justaNewbie - This guy played a pivotal role in my DMC development: without the existence of his superb FAQ (link given above), I doubt I would have gone on to do S-Rank games in DMC1. He seriously opened the door for me to DMC1. He also deserves credit for being one of the most steadfastly patient helpers on GameFAQs' DMC1 board over the years. He still posts there, although not as much.

Patient Vlakorados - This guy basically invented SB games <_<. (Not quite; a guy called Jacka1 did do a Normal SB game first.) At any rate, PV single-handedly invented the Phantom CH strategy, SBed the whole of DMD mode for the first time, discovered sword-cancelling, etc. etc. Where PV lead, I followed. I was the 2nd person to do a DMD SB game, however! :p

Ryan/Rob/Sternn - Hope you guys don't mind me lumping you together. These are all DMC1 SB players that I bounced ideas off with, especially Ryan. I learnt a lot of stuff from their games and hope that they learnt a bit of stuff from me.

Phantom Babies - For encouraging me to keep on going when I was ready to throw in the towel about 10 trillion times. The best forum in the universe :p

GameFAQs - The DMC1 board was an extremely good board in its day. Sadly it's gone downhill a lot, but without the existence of it, and a collection of like-minded DMC fans to discuss the game with, I wouldn't have got anywhere in DMC1. Whatever the message boards are like, GF is still a superb gaming resource in general.

Liz - She listened to my whingeing a lot... <_<

Krizy - As did she :p

My two goldfish - Of course <3


---

I hope I haven't hurt anybody by leaving them out, as, believe me, that is the last thing I want. I'm just basically thankful to every person who has been a decent and nice person in general during my time online. God only knows I've come across enough morons. Luckily, the nice ones stop me from losing faith in humanity entirely. Thanks, folks :p

top
